# Infrastructure log — 2026-09

The dated passages of the long-form reference for this month, by section, in the order they were
written (moved here from the single `INFRASTRUCTURE.md` on 2026-09-16; the index of every lead-in
is generated into that file by `scripts/infra_index.py --write`). Append a new passage under its
section heading as a bullet opening with a **bold lead-in** and a date; wrap at 100 columns
(`scripts/infra_index.py --wrap` does it); then regenerate the index.

## Hosting and delivery

- **GitHub Pages serves what `.github/workflows/build.yml` deploys — an ARTIFACT, not a branch
  (since 2026-09-08).** The Pages source is `build_type: workflow`; the `deploy` job runs
  `actions/deploy-pages` on the artifact the `build` job packaged out of **`docs/`**, whose contents
  are the domain root. So `docs/credo.html` is `merecatholicity.com/credo.html`, `docs/emoji/…` is
  `/emoji/…`. **`docs/CNAME`** points Pages at the apex domain and **`docs/.nojekyll`** disables
  Jekyll; both must be INSIDE the packaged folder, and build.yml asserts both before it packages —
  because losing CNAME from the served location once unbound the custom domain and 404'd the whole
  site ("Site not found · GitHub Pages"). The artifact IS the served folder now, so "where the site
  is served from" is that `path: docs` line, not a Settings dropdown. HISTORY, and the reason for
  the check: Pages used to serve the `main` branch's `/docs` folder directly, and `git mv`-ing the
  served files out of the root while Pages still served from root is what caused that outage.
  Switching the source back to a branch would be `PUT /repos/…/pages` `source[path]=/docs` with an
  admin token — but note `docs/` is no longer committed, so a branch source would now serve nothing.
- **Cloudflare** fronts Pages as a proxy (DNS + CDN + edge features). Do not change the SSL mode to
  Full (strict); the working configuration is deliberate.
- **THE PUBLISHED PDFs LIVE IN R2, NOT ON PAGES (2026-09-08).** All 244 of them — the book, the
  papers, the charts, and every corpus root PDF — sit in the R2 bucket **`merecatholicity-files`**,
  served from the custom domain **`files.merecatholicity.com`**. `docs/` had reached **621 MB
  against a HARD 1 GB GitHub Pages limit** and 293 MB of that was these PDFs; the site is 327 MB
  now. **Every previously-shared URL still works**: a Cloudflare **dynamic redirect** rule 301s
  `/<name>.pdf` to `https://files.merecatholicity.com/<name>.pdf`, so the pages keep linking plain
  relative `href="Mere_Catholicity.pdf"` and no page, sitemap entry or shared link had to change.
  `docs/*.pdf` is git-ignored, and **build.yml holds them back from the Pages artifact** — they were
  riding along in it whenever a run happened to rebuild them (measured: **400 MB with, 113 MB
  without**), which both re-inflated the very artifact the move was meant to shrink and made the
  deploy non-deterministic in content, since whether the PDFs existed depended on whether the
  PREVIOUS push touched a `.tex`. They are moved aside and moved back, so the `docs/` build cache
  keeps them and the next LaTeX change rebuilds one PDF instead of all 244. **`docs/pdfs.txt` is the
  manifest of what is published** (`make pdf-manifest`, built from the `WORKS` lists plus the seven
  hand PDFs), and it is how the link check survived the move: a `.pdf` href can no longer be checked
  by looking on disk, so `scripts/linkcheck.py` checks every one against that list — the check
  exists because two PDFs once shipped as dead links. **Publishing is automated and verified
  (2026-09-09): `scripts/publish_pdfs.py`.** `make publish-pdfs` lists the bucket through the R2
  REST API, MD5s every local `docs/*.pdf` (R2's ETag IS the MD5 for single-part objects — verified
  on the live objects), uploads only the ones that differ, and then **purges
  `https://files.merecatholicity.com/<name>` for exactly those** (the edge caches PDFs —
  `cf-cache-status: HIT` — so without the purge a rebuilt PDF would keep serving the old bytes for
  the TTL). `make check-pdfs` is the gate: every name in `docs/pdfs.txt` must exist in the bucket
  and every local PDF must match the bucket's copy, else it fails with the exact name and the reason
  ("absent from the bucket" / "rebuilt but unpublished"). Both need `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (Workers
  R2 Storage:Edit + Cache Purge); without it `check-pdfs` falls back to public HEADs, which see
  absence only and are 403'd by Bot Fight Mode from a runner, so it degrades to a notice rather than
  a lie, and `publish-pdfs` refuses. **CI does all of it** (`build.yml`): `make -C resources pdf`
  when LaTeX changed, `make pdf publish` when `book/**` changed, `make chart-pdfs
  CHROMIUM=/usr/bin/google-chrome` after the site build when a chart page or the stylesheet changed,
  `make logos` when the book changed or the docx is missing, then `make publish-pdfs`, and `make
  check-pdfs` in the Gates on every run. **One published PDF has no build at all —
  `The_Bishop_of_Rome.pdf` is a MIRRORED 2024 document**; it lives as a tracked source at
  `resources/docs-src/The_Bishop_of_Rome.pdf` and `make mirrored-pdfs` (run inside `make html`)
  copies it into `docs/` so the publisher and the check treat it like the rest. It was rescued there
  on 2026-09-09 because its only other copies were the bucket and the git history that was rewritten
  that day. **The first credentialed run re-published 243 of the 244**: a runner's TeX Live and
  Chrome emit different bytes from the dev box's for the SAME sources (only the mirrored
  `The_Bishop_of_Rome.pdf` matched), so from that run on **CI is the build of record for every PDF**
  and the bucket holds CI's bytes; a run that rebuilds nothing uploads nothing (`all N local PDFs
  already match the bucket`), and a local rebuild will read as "differs" — that is the dev box
  drifting from the record, not the other way round. The wrangler road (`npx wrangler r2 object put
  merecatholicity-files/<name> --file docs/<name> --remote` — **`--remote` is load-bearing**) still
  works for a one-off.
- **Deploying the site is a push to `main`.** That has not changed, but what happens after it has:
  the Build workflow restores the previous `docs/` from cache, rebuilds only what the diff touched,
  runs the gates (`make tests`, `make jscheck`, `make check`), packages `docs/` and deploys it to
  Pages, then purges the edge. There is still no build step on the Pages *server* — it serves the
  artifact as-is — but there IS one in CI, and it is now the only one. A **pull request builds and
  is gated but never deploys** (the `deploy` job is `if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' &&
  github.event_name == 'push'`), so a fork's PR cannot touch production. Building locally is
  optional; a local `make html` + push still works, but nothing you build locally is what ships — CI
  rebuilds it.
- **`comments.js` is BUILT from TypeScript now (Phase 2C, 2026-08-01).** Edit
  **`client/comments.ts`** (the typed source), never `docs/comments.js` — that is generated by `npm
  run build:client` (esbuild transpile-only: strips types + comments, preserves the standalone IIFE;
  folded into `make bundle`/`npm run build:js`). It stays the no-bundle fallback client (still
  import-free, reached from the bundle only via `window.mc*`). *(Since Wave F, 2026-09-11,
  `build:client` bundles — `--bundle --format=iife` — because the client is a module set,
  `client/comments.ts` importing its seven feature modules; it still reaches the kernel and the app
  only through `window.mc*`. See the Wave F passage.)* tsc (strict) covers it via the root
  `tsconfig.json` (`client/**/*.ts`), so it is no longer in the eslint list. `make jscheck` (lint +
  tsc) still gates it.
- **Cache-busting is AUTOMATIC — do not bump a `?v=` by hand.** `scripts/stamp_versions.py` (run by
  `make bundle`, and again as the last word of `make html`) stamps every served asset with a `?v=`
  key derived from its own content hash, across `nav.js`, the pages, `partials/*.html` and
  `content.py`'s `COMMENTS_V`. Unchanged content keeps its key so rebuilds stay byte-identical;
  changed content gets a new URL, which is the only thing that reaches a phone's own cache.
  `style.css` is stamped too (the old "unversioned, propagates on TTL" note is history — only
  `sw.js` is deliberately unkeyed, its URL being its registration identity). The live warning that
  remains: Cloudflare treats each `?v=N` URL as immutable, so **never fetch a freshly-stamped `?v=N`
  URL until the deploy has landed** — a probe mid-deploy freezes the OLD bytes under the new key.
  Fetch a throwaway query string (`comments.js?probe123`) instead, which always misses cache and
  hits origin. **Watching a deploy is usually unnecessary** — pushing is the end of a normal change;
  only probe when you genuinely need the new client live before a next step (headless testing
  against prod, reproducing a bug there).

## Build system

**The sheet owns the scroll while it is open (2026-09-09).** The owner's report, on phones:
scrolling the Settings sheet usually moved the sheet, but sometimes moved the page behind it — a
finger on the overlay must never grab what is under it. A touch reached the document by three roads,
and `<mc-sheet>` was closing none of them: **scroll chaining** (the sheet is its own scroller,
`max-height:86vh; overflow-y:auto`; at its top or bottom edge — or when its content is too short to
scroll at all, which the Settings list often is on a tall phone — the gesture is handed to the next
scroller up, the document); **the scrim** (a drag on the dimmed page is a drag on the document); and
**the document itself**, which stayed scrollable underneath. Closed in three layers, each in the
file it belongs to: `.mc-sheet{overscroll-behavior:contain; touch-action:pan-y}` on both the phone
sheet and the desktop modal, plus `overscroll-behavior:contain` on the desktop account dropdown
(`.mc-db-menu`), ends the chain at the sheet whether or not it can scroll;
`.mc-sheet-scrim{touch-action:none}` makes the scrim inert; and `lockDocument()`/`unlockDocument()`
in `app/appchrome.ts` — the road every engine honours, iOS included — puts `mc-sheet-open` on
`<html>` for exactly as long as `open` is true, with `html.mc-sheet-open
body{position:fixed;overflow:hidden;width:100%}` and the scroll offset carried in the body's inline
`top` so the page does not visibly move; on close the offset is restored with `behavior:'instant'`
(never a smooth travel), and on desktop the vanished scrollbar's width is padded back so the layout
does not shift. One lock for the document however many sheets ask; a sheet removed from the document
releases it. `app/ptr.ts` ignores the scrim and a locked document — with the body fixed, `scrollY`
reads 0 whatever the page's real offset, so its "at the top" test would lie. Locked in
`tests/js/sheet_lock.test.mjs` (source) and `tests/css/test_style_css.py` (the build carries the
rules). Not verified by hand on desktop (the owner had not seen it there); the containment applies
there too.

**Nothing scrolls sideways on a phone (2026-09-09).** The owner's report: many pages — the Bible,
the articles — scrolled horizontally in the installed app. `html{overflow-x:hidden}` had been there
all along; it stops a scrollbar, not iOS's panning of a body wider than the viewport, so the sources
of the width had to go. Measured, not guessed: a headless sweep of the built site at 390px
(`document.documentElement.scrollWidth` against `clientWidth`, plus `scrollTo(200,0)` — `hidden`
blocks the user, not scripts — and a DFS for the outermost box past the right edge, then a second
probe for elements whose `scrollWidth` exceeds their `clientWidth`, which is how a long word inside
a normal-width paragraph shows up) found 23 of 83 sampled pages over-wide, in exactly three classes.
**(1) Every art-backed article** (About, Where to begin, the Credo, the charts, every corpus page
with art): the phone's edge-to-edge rule pulled `main.prose` out by a hard `-1rem` while the phone
body pads `0.8rem`, so the column stood 0.2rem proud on each side — 3px of sideways scroll on the
pages the owner reads most. The body's inline padding is a variable now (`--page-pad`, `1rem`,
`.8rem` on phones) and the rule pulls out by `calc(-1 * var(--page-pad))`. **(2) Unbreakable runs in
the corpus**: dotted leaders in Eusebius's emperor list (npnf201: 725px), run-together Greek
footnotes in Schaff (hcc1/hcc3), an arithmetic string in a Clement footnote (anf02), rows of `=` in
Luther's theses, a ratio string in the Timaeus, and — worst — MediaWiki chrome leaked into Tacitus
by `resources/rome2tex.py`: the `<style>` element's CSS (tag stripped, body kept), the `<!-- Saved
in parser cache -->` / `NewPP limit report` comments (never a tag, never stripped) and the div-based
back/forward breadcrumb. `main.prose{overflow-wrap:break-word}` (inherited by every paragraph and
footnote; `break-word` not `anywhere`, so intrinsic sizing of tables and flex rows is untouched)
ends the spill; the converter now strips `<style>` bodies and comments and cuts the navigation divs
with a balanced scan (`drop_divs`), the translator credit in the same header deliberately kept, and
the two `-body.tex` were regenerated — the converter was proven byte-deterministic against the
committed files first, so the diff is exactly the junk (annals −420/+24 lines, histories −179/+10,
zosimus untouched). **(3) The Bible reader's bar**: the find box is an `<input>` with an intrinsic
~176px and no permission to shrink, so every KJV and Douay page ran 60–90px past the edge;
`.bible-bar` and `.bp-row` wrap now and `.bible-find{flex:1 1 11em;max-width:100%}` — stated in
`docs/bible-reader.js`'s injected block AS WELL as the stylesheet, because that block is injected
later and wins on equal specificity. **And the net**: `body{overflow-x:clip}` — `clip`, not
`hidden`, which would make the body a scroll container (overflow-y computes to auto) and break
sticky positioning. After the fixes the same sweep reads 0 of 23 and the text probe finds no element
wider than its box on any page it flagged. Locked in `tests/css/test_style_css.py` (the build
carries every rule, the reader's copy agrees) and `webtest/test_hscroll.py` (the sampled sweep
against prod, hand pages + shelves + Bible chapters, at 390px).

**The partials are load-bearing here (added 2026-09-05):** `partials/book-tail.html` carries its own
`comments.js?v=` and pandoc copies it straight into `docs/book.html`, so while the stamp scanned
only `docs/` the two fought every build — the stamp fixed the page, the next `make html` put the
stale key back — and book.html shipped `comments.js?v=199` against a kernel many versions newer. A
NEW partial carrying a versioned asset is covered automatically; a versioned key in any other
directory is not, and must be added to that scan. Migrations 0007 (profiles.muted, wall edited_at,
bookmarks) + 0008 (notifications kind + 'merecat') applied with the wave. `/api/merecat/store` (the
retired HTTP-era no-op) is deleted.

**CI builds the site (2026-09-08) — `.github/workflows/build.yml`.** The repo is
**public**, so Actions minutes are free and unlimited on standard runners; wall-clock is
the only constraint. On every PR and every push to main it builds and runs `make tests`,
`make jscheck`, `make check`, then packages `docs/` as a Pages artifact and **deploys it** — Pages
is
`build_type: workflow` now, so this workflow IS the deploy. Its last step purges the two
edge files that cannot carry a content-hash key (`sw.js`, `version.json`), which is safe
precisely there: `actions/deploy-pages` polls until GitHub reports the deployment
succeeded, so by that step the content is published, and the danger was only ever purging
BEFORE publish. A pull request — including one from a fork, which cannot read secrets at
all — builds and is gated but never deploys.

**What made CI practical: the build is incremental now.** The corpus HTML targets were
`for` loops with NO prerequisites, so every build re-ran pandoc over all ~235 works —
about 35 minutes, every time — while the PDFs beside them had been properly
dependency-tracked all along (`PDF_RULE`). Now every work has its own target
(`HTML_RULE`/`CURATED_RULE`/`CATENA_RULE`/`SUMMA_RULE` in `resources/Makefile`):
**`make html` with nothing changed went 362 s → 0.08 s**, one touched body rebuilds one
page, and touching `partials/footer.html` still rebuilds all 235 (it is a pandoc `-B`
include on every page, so `make menu` must). **The lists CANNOT be iterated by make** —
their titles contain spaces and make has no notion of the double quotes that group them,
so `$(foreach)` over `SCHAFF_HTML` produced targets like `../docs/Vol..html`; the ids come
from the PDF lists (`id=Output.pdf`, never a space) and the recipe still parses the entry
in shell.

**Two CI-specific traps, both silent, both handled in the workflow:** (1) git writes every
file at checkout time in NO GUARANTEED ORDER, so a `.tex` can land after its `.html` and
make rebuilds it for nothing (the first run rebuilt the whole Newman corpus) — while a
genuinely changed `.tex` arrives with the SAME mtime as its output and make rebuilds
nothing at all. The workflow flattens every mtime to a fixed old timestamp, then touches
only the files in the diff. (2) TeX Live is ~2 GB and most changes never touch a `.tex`,
so it installs only when the diff says LaTeX is involved. **A third, found 2026-09-09, and the worst
of the three because it shipped: the site cache clobbers the tracked half of `docs/`.**
`actions/cache` restores the previous build's WHOLE `docs/` tree — and `docs/` is a mixture, so the
tar lands the previous commit's copy of every hand-maintained file (`nav.js`, `sw.js`,
`turnstile.html`, the images, the architecture docs) over this checkout, with the previous run's
mtimes, BEFORE the gates run and before the artifact is packaged. An edit to any of those files
never reached the runner: the `turnstile.html` colour-scheme fix failed its own test on CI while
passing locally, and the run before had warned `docs/architecture/CODEBASE.md | 69 +++---` in the
drift step — the cache's older copy differing from the commit, read as expected noise. The hand
pages and nav.js self-healed because `make content` and the stamp rewrite them; a file nothing
rebuilds (turnstile.html, sw.js, an image, a doc) shipped stale for as long as the cache was warm.
Fixed in place: a step right after the restore runs `git checkout -- docs` (rewrites only what
differs from the commit, with a fresh mtime — exactly the files that ARE new) and `git clean -fd --
docs` (a hand file this commit removed must not ship from the cache; anything neither tracked nor
ignored under `docs/` is by definition not build output, which `tests/py/test_docs_sources.py`
holds), then FAILS if a tracked file still differs. `workers.yml` got the same step. The drift
step's meaning changed with it: a diff there now means stale stamps in the commit (run `make bundle`
before committing), never a clobbered source. **A fourth (2026-09-09), and the stamp's, not the
cache's: the version stamp was bumping every page's mtime.** `make bundle` ends with
`scripts/stamp_versions.py`, which rewrites the `?v=` keys in all 272 pages; a rewrite is a fresh
mtime, and `make html` runs a minute later. A commit that changed a corpus `.tex` AND the stylesheet
(the horizontal-scroll fix regenerated Tacitus and re-keyed `style.css`) therefore saw
`'../docs/annals.html' is up to date` — the cached, stale page had just been "modified" by the stamp
and outranked its touched source — and shipped the old page with the new key. Locally invisible: the
two pages had been rebuilt by hand. The stamp changes cache keys only and updates every consumer
itself, so a stamped file is exactly as fresh relative to its sources as before; it now writes
through `write_keeping_mtime()` (`os.utime` back to the original), which makes it invisible to make
— `tests/py/test_stamp_versions.py` holds that for `sub_file` and the page pass. The pages that trap
had already left stale were healed by bumping the site cache's generation (`site-` → `site-2-` in
both workflows): one cold rebuild of everything from source, then warm again. That is the documented
way to force a full rebuild from now on.

**WHAT CI/CD OWNS, AND THE EXCEPTIONS (the owner's standing rule since 2026-09-09: everything
through the pipeline as far as is sane, every exception written down).** Four workflows:
**`build.yml`** — site build, gates, PDF builds + publish + `check-pdfs`, Pages deploy, edge purge
(every push; PRs build and gate but never deploy); **`workers.yml`** — `make jscheck` + `make tests`
+ `wrangler deploy --dry-run` on PRs, and on `main` the D1 ledger (`wrangler d1 migrations apply
--remote`) then `wrangler deploy` for the comments worker and, when its paths changed, the contact
worker (path-filtered to `comments-worker/**`, `contact-worker/**`, `purescript/**`, the npm
lockfile and the TS/eslint configs); **`terraform.yml`** — plan/approve/apply, path-filtered to
`terraform/**`; **`purge-cache.yml`** — a manual button only. **Exceptions, each for a reason:** (1)
**worker secrets** — `wrangler secret put`, never in git or CI; (2) **the librarian ingest** (`make
librarian`) — needs the private shelf (a separate private clone) and the owner's admin key; (3)
**the bootstrap secrets** — `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `TF_GITHUB_TOKEN` are set by hand (`gh secret
set`), and the `merecatholicity-tfstate` bucket is unmanaged: a credential cannot be minted by the
automation it authorises, and a state store cannot manage itself; (4) **Email Routing settings** —
provider bug #7301/#7302/#7304 (`support_subaddress`), still open at 5.24.0; DNS for it IS managed;
(5) **the R2 custom-domain bindings** (`audio.`, `files.`) and **the TURN key** — the provider
supports no import and declaring them would create duplicates; (6) **Vectorize** — no provider
resource; (7) **one-off uploads** — the KJV audio and the private-shelf tarball; (8) **headless
verification against prod** (`webtest/`) — Bot Fight Mode 403s a runner, so it runs from the dev
box; (9) **the history rewrite** — a deliberate, backed-up, owner-authorised act, never automated;
(10) **the org's personal-access-token policy** (fine-grained PATs allowed, expiry not required) and
**the PAT itself** — GitHub offers no API for either, so both are browser-only owner acts.

**Four real bugs the first CI runs surfaced, none of them CI's fault:** `make bundle`
depended on `jscheck`, which runs `tsc` over `app/core.ts`, which imports the git-ignored
`purescript/output/` — so **`jscheck` never declared its dependency on `psbuild`** and any
clean clone failed (`jscheck: psbuild` now); `tests/py/test_slug_parity.py` **pinned node
to `/usr/bin/node`**, asserting the shape of one machine and failing for anyone on nvm,
Homebrew or CI (`shutil.which` now); `tests/py/test_serve_thinkstrip.py` imports
`local/serve.py`, which imports **numpy** — the GPU backend's dependency, not the site's
(class-level skip, because `make tests` runs each file directly and a module-level
`SkipTest` exits non-zero); and `tests/py/test_librarian_sources.py` asserted the
**private shelf** resolves, which it never can in public CI.

**Navigation** is generated: `nav.yml` is the single source. `scripts/nav.py` renders
`partials/nav.html` and rewrites the inline nav block in every page listed in the `PAGES` array at
the top of `nav.py` (run as `python scripts/nav.py` from the repo root, so its `nav.yml`/`docs/`
paths still resolve; the pandoc include is now `partials/nav.html`, likewise
`partials/footer.html`/`social.html`/`book-tail.html`). A new site page must be added to that list
or `make menu` will not touch it.

**Resources** (`resources/`) are public-domain texts rebuilt as our own LaTeX. `*2tex.py` scripts
convert preserved sources into `*-body.tex`; the `WORKS` list in `resources/Makefile` maps each
source to its published root PDF, and outputs land in `docs/` (the `-o ../docs/…` and `cp …
../docs/…` targets) so Pages serves them.

**The complete Schaff corpus** (the whole Ante-Nicene Fathers, 9 vols., the Nicene and Post-Nicene
Fathers, First and Second Series, 14 vols. each — 36 volumes, NPNF-2 Vol. 14 being the
already-published `councils.html` — plus Schaff's own **History of the Christian Church**, 8 vols.
`hcc1`–`hcc8`, and **The Creeds of Christendom**, 3 vols. `creeds1`–`creeds3`: 47 volumes in all) is
rendered by `resources/schaff.py`, a whole-volume driver over the shared `ccel2tex.convert_work`.
Its `VOLUMES` table (id, series, vol, title, contents blurb) drives everything: it writes each
`<id>-body.tex` (a permissive per-volume `make_volume_heading` keeps every `div1` as a chapter
except title/series pages, contents, indexes, and tables — index/table subtrees are excluded by
div-id prefix, while a skipped title page still lets real content nested under it through, e.g.
Hilary of Poitiers mis-nested in `npnf209`), a self-contained wrapper `<id>.tex` (preamble mirrors
`councils.tex` plus `amssymb`/`textcomp`), and `resources/schaff.mk` (the generated `SCHAFF_PDFS`
id=Output.pdf and `SCHAFF_HTML` id:tocdepth:"Title" lists the Makefile `-include`s). The History and
Creeds volumes put their chapters at `div2` under one container `div1`, so they convert with
`make_volume_heading(deep=True)` (top divisions always skip-promote) and `table_cells=True` (their
parallel Greek/Latin|English texts sit in `<table>` rows whose cells often carry bare text — the
Converter's opt-in td-as-paragraph mode keeps it; off elsewhere so the older bodies stay
byte-stable). `creeds3` mis-nests a `<note>` inside a `<note>` (Westminster proof-texts) — the
Converter flattens nested notes. `make body` regenerates all of it; `make pdf` builds every root PDF
(`History_of_the_Christian_Church_Vol_*.pdf`, `Creeds_of_Christendom_Vol_*.pdf`, and the Fathers
sets) via the same `PDF_RULE` machinery; `make -C resources schaff-html` (run by `make html`)
renders each `<id>.html`. The library is listed on `credo.html#library` (under the
consensus-of-the-Fathers section) and on `library.html` (`#schaff`, `#history`, `#creeds`). **Volume
source XML and per-volume intermediate PDFs are git-ignored** (reproducible): `make -C resources
schaff-fetch` re-downloads every CCEL ThML XML; the committed `<id>-body.tex`/`<id>.tex` and the
published root PDFs are what the site serves. Greek-at-scale needs care — `convert_work` strips
orphan combining marks (U+0300–036F), wraps Hebrew **including the presentation forms U+FB1D–FB4F**
(the History quotes pointed Hebrew with the alternative ayin U+FB20) using STX/ETX sentinels (not
the guillemets ‹/›, which occur in the source), and `schaff.volume_post` maps the few non-LGR
symbols (∴, low/angle quotes, stray Coptic, math-variant Greek letterforms ϰ/ϑ/ϕ, and the numeral
keraia U+02B9 → `'`, which LGR sets as the proper glyph).

**Curated extractions from the Schaff volumes** ride `ccel2tex.py`'s `EXTRA_WORKS` table (kwargs
straight into `convert_work`; heading factories are re-called per run so counters reset): **The City
of God** (`cityofgod`, from npnf102, 22 books + translator's preface, ~690 pp.), **On Christian
Doctrine** (`ondoctrine`, npnf102), **The Confessions** (`confessions`, npnf101, 13 books), **On the
Incarnation** (`incarnation`, npnf204, 57 §§), **the Canons and Dogmatic Decrees of the Council of
Trent** (`trent`, extracted from `creeds2.xml`, the 13 dogmatic sessions Latin|English plus the
Tridentine Profession), **An Exact Exposition of the Orthodox Faith** (`damascus`, npnf209, the four
books), **The Early Liturgies** (`liturgies`, anf07: St. James, St. Mark, the Holy Apostles), and
**the Westminster Confession with the Shorter Catechism** (`westminster`, from `creeds3.xml` — each
document is one flat division whose chapter heads are inline h3/h4 or English|Latin table rows, so
it converts with `inner_heads=True` and `westminster_post` promotes the "Chapter N." rows to
sections). The volume transcriptions carry a few glitched div ids with no volume prefix (Confessions
Books I–II are `I_1`/`II_1`, OCD Book IV is `IV_1`), so the heading factories track which top-level
division the consult stream is under (`_tops_tracker`) instead of trusting id prefixes. **Whole CCEL
ThML books** ride the same table: Chesterton (`orthodoxy` — bare "I"–"IX" chapter titles restored
from `ORTHODOXY_TITLES` — `heretics`, `everlasting`), **Calvin's Institutes** (`institutes`,
Beveridge, ~930 pp.; `institutes_post` repairs a Symbol-font Greek word and a mangled French *où*),
and **Luther** (`bondage`, `luther-galatians`). All the `*-thml.xml` sources are committed like the
other curated sources. Wrappers are committed hand files in the curated style; each work is a
`WORKS` line + html-stanza entry in `resources/Makefile`. Listed on `library.html` under `#fathers`,
`#creeds`, `#reformers`, `#apologetics`, and `#prayer`.

**The schism documents** convert via `resources/docs2tex.py` from preserved sources in
`resources/docs-src/`: Ravenna, Chieti, JDDJ (christianunity.va), the 1848 and **1895** Encyclicals
(orthodoxinfo.com — the 1895 is the Patriarchate's reply to Leo XIII), **Apostolicae Curae**
(papalencyclicals.net) and its Anglican answer **Saepius Officio** (the Project Canterbury PDF
`docs-src/saepius.pdf`, extracted with `pdftotext -layout`; its 43 sequentially-numbered
foot-of-page citations are re-paired to their glued in-text digits as real `\footnote`s — two
passes, space-separated refs second — with any unplaced note kept visibly in a tail block), and
Scranton. The docs bodies use raw `\section`/`\subsection`, so the shared curated html loop stars
them (`\section{`→`\section*{`) — pandoc then marks them `.unnumbered`, which is what `deeplink.js`
keys on; the 1848 encyclical's own address line is promoted to its one heading for the same reason.
Wrappers are hand files in the `encyclical1848.tex` style (Saepius carries the LGR/`textalpha` guard
for its Greek liturgical quotes).

**Project Gutenberg books** convert via `resources/gutenberg2tex.py` (the third flat-HTML converter
beside `dr2tex.py`/`kjv2tex.py`, reusing the dev2tex engine): **The Faith of Our Fathers**
(`gibbons`, PG 27435), **An Apology of the Church of England** (`jewel`, PG 17678), and **the
Ninety-Five Theses bound with Concerning Christian Liberty** (`luther-primary`, PG 274 + 1911 — a
WORKS entry may list several `srcs` bound as one volume, each with its own `firsts` cut). The driver
merges PG h1 pairs ("Chapter N." + title) or promotes h2 chapters (`htag`), inlines end-of-book `<dl
class="...footnotes">` notes at their `noteref_N` anchors (Gibbons has 545; note keys are
source-prefixed with an `x` separator — an underscore would be TeX-escaped by `finalize` and break
the match), strips pagenum spans/print indexes/Contents lists/the PG license (old and new formats),
linearizes small tables, and title-cases all-caps headings. Raw `*-src.html` files are committed
like the other preserved flat sources. Listed under `library.html#apologetics` and `#reformers`.

**The Douay-Rheims Bible** (`douay-rheims.html` / `Douay-Rheims_Bible.pdf`, ~1,744 pp.) is the whole
Challoner-revision Catholic Bible, converted by `resources/dr2tex.py` from the preserved Project
Gutenberg HTML (`resources/douay-rheims-src.html`, ebook 1581). That source is flat, not ThML, so it
has its own tiny parser: `<h2>` testaments → `\xchapter`, `<h3>` books → `\xsection` (new page
each), `<h4>` chapters → `\xsubsection`, `<p>` verses (leading `N:M.` bolded), `p.sp2` arguments →
`\drarg`, `p.expl` Challoner notes → `\drnote` (macros defined in the wrapper `douay-rheims.tex`).
It is a normal `WORKS` entry for the PDF and has its own stanza in the `html` target (it needs the
`\drarg`/`\drnote` seds and bracket-protection); listed on `credo.html#scriptures`.

**The Catena Aurea** (Aquinas' Golden Chain on the four Gospels,
`catena-{matthew,mark,luke,john}.html` / `Catena_Aurea_*.pdf`) is converted by
`resources/catena2tex.py` from the fisheaters PDFs (`resources/catena-src/xaquinas-*.pdf`,
git-ignored — re-downloadable) via `pdftotext`. The extracted text is clean (only page-number lines
and form-feeds to drop); the parser keys on `CHAP. N`, verse-range lines (`1:1`, `2:1–2`), and
Father **attributions** matched against a fixed roster (`KNOWN`) so noise like "THE."/"AMEN." never
splits a paragraph. It emits **single-argument** macros only — `\cachapter`, `\caref`, `\calemma`,
`\cafather{Name}` — so the HTML `catena-html` target can rewrite each with one `sed` (a 2-arg macro
would leave a stray brace); `\cafather{X}` carries the trailing period into the bold attribution.
`finalize()` NFC-composes, drops orphan combining marks and bidi controls, and wraps Greek/Hebrew
citation runs in `\textgreek`/`\texthebrew` (the wrapper carries the same `textalpha` guard as the
Schaff volumes). Four `WORKS` entries build the PDFs; listed on `credo.html#catena` under the
consensus-of-the-Fathers section.

**The King James Bible** (`kjv.html` / `King_James_Bible.pdf`, ~1,415 pp.) is the 66-book Authorized
Version, converted by `resources/kjv2tex.py` from Project Gutenberg ebook 10
(`resources/kjv-src.html`, committed). Same flat shape as the Douay-Rheims but chapters are *not*
marked — the chapter number lives in each verse's `C:V` ref, and a single `<p>` often packs several
verses, so the parser splits on every `C:V` and emits a `\xsubsection{Chapter N}` when the number
advances (31,102 verses, 1,189 chapters — both exact). Gutenberg's KJV carries no Apocrypha, which
matches the Scourby audio (Phase 2) exactly. A `WORKS` entry + a DR-style `html` stanza; listed on
`credo.html#scriptures` and the Library. The DRBO spoken Bible (sanctusmission.com) is linked there
as a secondary external resource.

**The Summa Theologica** (`summa-fp.html`…`summa-xp.html` / `Summa_Theologica_*.pdf`, five parts,
~3,800 pp. total) is converted by `resources/summatheologica2tex.py` from the git-ignored CCEL HTML
tree (`resources/summa-src/`, re-fetch with `make -C resources summa-fetch`). The old
`<br>`-laid-out HTML has **unclosed `<P>` tags**, so the parser (an HTMLParser) flushes at each
boundary, not on `</p>`, and keys on the files' precise structural **HTML comments** (`SMT <PART>
Q[n] A[a] Obj./OTC/Body/R.O./Thes./Out.`) plus `<H3 Align="Center">` (treatise + question title) and
`<H3 Align="Left">` (article "Whether…?"). Emits the shared
`\xchapter`(treatise)/`\xsection`(question)/`\xsubsection`(article) macros with scholastic labels
bolded, reusing `catena2tex.finalize`. Split into its five parts (FP/FS/SS/TP/Supplement), five
`WORKS` entries + a `summa-html` stanza; listed **only** on the Library page.

**The Newman corpus** (55 works — the whole public NewmanReader corpus, `arians.html`…`verses.html`
+ one root PDF each) is rendered by `resources/newman.py`, a whole-corpus driver modeled on
`schaff.py` that reuses the dev2tex engine (the same passes behind
`development.html`/`consulting.html`). One `WORKS` table (id, `path` for a multi-file work or `page`
for a single page, section, title, pdf, provenance, blurb, `exclude`, `notes_appendix`) drives fetch
→ convert → wrapper → `resources/newman.mk` (`NEWMAN_PDFS`/`NEWMAN_HTML`, `-include`d by the
Makefile; `make -C resources newman-html` runs in `make html`). `make -C resources newman-fetch`
re-spiders newmanreader.org into git-ignored `resources/newman-src/<id>/` (file set = the work's
`index.html` same-dir links, fragments dropped, nested sub-work indexes resolved recursively).
Newman's endnotes become `\footnote`s at their reference points, keyed on the `#noteN` **anchor**
(not the visible text — some works write a bare "[Note]"); the id scheme spans `note12`/`mnote12`
(margin)/`noteA` (lettered), which Athanasius' historical tracts use all at once. A chapter split
across files (`chapter6-1/-2`) is detected by the absence of its own `<h2>`. LaTeX-safety passes:
`_heal_emphasis` (no `\par`/`\begin{quote}` inside a text command), `_cap_quotes` (≤5 nesting,
orphan `\end{quote}` dropped), `_map_symbols`, `\\[`→`\\{}[`, then PUA-sentinel and known-HTML-tag
sweeps. Four source files drop a few footnote markers (cross-file note targets; text intact). Listed
on `library.html#newman` as a period shelf (Anglican / Catholic / Miscellaneous) beside the earlier
`development`/`consulting`.

**KJV Scourby audio** is Alexander Scourby's public-domain reading, one MP3 per chapter,
**self-hosted on Cloudflare R2** (the site can't put 3.26 GB in git/Pages). A third R2 bucket
**`merecatholicity-audio`** holds the objects under `kjv/<bookslug>/<chapter>.mp3` (66 canonical
books, no Apocrypha — matching Gutenberg's KJV), served from the **custom domain
`audio.merecatholicity.com`** (a `wrangler r2 bucket domain add` on the zone; the `pub-*.r2.dev` URL
also works but the custom domain is stable and Cloudflare-cached, with range requests for seeking).
The client is `kjv-audio.js` (loaded via `kjv-tail.html`, the KJV page's pandoc `-A` include instead
of the plain footer): it walks the page's `h2` book headings (canonical order → its `SLUGS` array)
and `h4` "Chapter N" headings, grafts a ▶ button onto each chapter, and drives one shared `<audio>`
element that auto-advances to the next chapter. **Re-uploading:** `scratchpad/upload_kjv_audio.py`
downloads the 66 per-book ZIPs from earnestlycontendingforthefaith.com, extracts the per-chapter
MP3s, and `wrangler r2 object put`s each — **with `--remote`**, the essential gotcha: without it
wrangler writes to a *local* simulated store and the real bucket stays empty (the public URL 404s).
The audio host must be in the report-only CSP `media-src` (the CSP is a dashboard Response-Header
Transform Rule, not in the repo).

**The Library page** (`library.html`) is a hand-authored master catalog of every hosted work
(Scripture, Summa, the Fathers, the Schaff library, councils, Newman, the rule of prayer, our own
papers), generated once via `scratchpad/gen_library.py` (which imports `schaff.VOLUMES` so the 37
volumes never drift) then spliced into the `resources.html` skeleton. It replaced **Sources** in the
nav (`nav.yml`: `Library: library.html`; added to `nav.py`'s `PAGES`); `resources.html` stays in
place, off-nav. A nav change rebuilds every pandoc page's baked-in nav, so it is followed by a full
`make html`.

**Deep-linking (`deeplink.js`).** Every generated Scripture/Fathers page is addressable down to the
verse or paragraph, entirely **client-side** so no page had to be rebuilt: `nav.js` (already on
every page) injects `deeplink.js`, which no-ops unless the page has pandoc `.unnumbered` headings.
It stamps each **KJV verse** with a canonical `id="<bookslug>-<chapter>-<verse>"` (walking the 66
book `<h2>` in canonical order for the slug — verses get the id but no visible marker, ~31k would be
noise), and every **Fathers heading/paragraph** with an id (headings keep pandoc's own slug so the
TOC still resolves; paragraphs hang off the nearest heading as `<heading-id>__p<n>`). Each heading
and Fathers paragraph gets a hover-revealed `¶` marker (one delegated click handler, not thousands)
whose click copies the permalink; arriving at a `#hash` scrolls to it and `:target`-tints it
(style.css). **Scripture autolinking + picker (`comments.js`).** `BIBLE` (a book→slug table with
every abbreviation, common-word 2-letter forms omitted) yields a regex fragment spliced into
`INLINE_MD`; `appendRich` turns `Rom 8:28-30`/`John 3:16` into a same-site link to
`kjv.html#romans-8-28` — a bare book name never links (a chapter:verse is required). A `✝ Scripture`
toolbar button (beside the emoji picker, in `mdEditor`) opens `buildScripturePanel`, which lazily
fetches **`kjv.json`** (book→chapter→verse text, generated by `resources/kjv-json.py`, the same lazy
pattern as `emoji-data.json`) and inserts the chosen passage as a `>` blockquote plus the reference.
On desktop, hovering an autolinked reference pops the verse text (`scriptureHover`, reusing the
cached `kjv.json`); `scriptureDecor` grants the same hover to ANY anchor whose href is a KJV verse —
markdown links as merecat writes them, sources-footer entries, member-pasted links — reading a range
from the link's own text since the URL carries only the first verse. Any `comments.js` change needs
the usual `comments.js?v=NN` bump; `deeplink.js`/`nav.js`/`style.css` are unversioned (cache-TTL).

## The PureScript application layer

**The site's application/domain logic IS a PureScript kernel; Lit.js is strictly the render layer,
and JS is interop only (DOM, effects, crypto).** The migration reached its intended end state —
**`purescript/src/Domain/*.purs` is now 28 modules** covering the meaningful application logic
(constants, validation, permissions, parsing, routing, identity classification, FTS sanitization),
consumed by both the client bundle and the Cloudflare worker. **Coding rules (this is the whole
rulebook — there is no separate rules file):** new application logic goes in PureScript as ADTs +
newtypes with **smart constructors**, `Maybe`/`Either` instead of null/throw, and **illegal states
made unrepresentable** (e.g. `Fts.SafeMatch` has no public constructor, so an un-sanitized FTS MATCH
cannot exist; `Auth.AuthState` forbids a hash without a key); keep logic **pure** and push effects
(DOM, fetch, crypto, storage, Turnstile, WebSocket) to thin typed FFI at the edges; migrate in
**vertical slices** each proven by the four gates below, deleting the JS it replaces (or, for
`comments.js`, keeping the classic body as the no-bundle fallback); never recreate the classic JS
architecture for its own sake or weaken the types to dodge the boundary. **All new application logic
(validation, permissions, state transitions, business rules, parsing) goes in PureScript, not JS.**
What deliberately STAYS in JS is interop by design: DOM/Lit rendering, the
Turnstile/nacl/WebCrypto/fetch/WebSocket effects, the raw identity key/hash storage (the secret is
sent in every keyed request), the DM E2E crypto + `E1.` framing (opaque by contract), and the
composer/DM SEND orchestration (Turnstile-gated). The classic per-function bodies in
`docs/comments.js` are kept as the **no-bundle fallback** (app disabled / storage blocked ⇒ no
`window.mcCore`), NOT dead code. The modules (27, and this list is the count — keep both in step
when a slice adds one): `Access`, `Auth`, `Blocked`, `Board`, `Cache`, `Call`, `Compose`, `Dm`,
`Emoji`, `Faith`, `Fts`, `Handle`, `Links`, `Live`, `Media`, `Mute`, `Pager`, `Prefs`, `Presence`,
`Profile`, `Pseudonym`, `Ptr`, `Rank`, `Route`, `Scripture`, `Turnstile`, `Wall`. A 26-commit
adversarial review (parity/security/robustness vs the pre-migration code) found the kernel clean —
one low-severity lookup drift (fixed) — and confirmed the migration fixed several latent bugs (the
bio-cap drift, dead scripture autolinks on invalid/leading-zero refs, FTS injection).

**The seam.** PureScript sources live in **`purescript/src/Domain/*.purs`** (plus a thin FFI where a
real JS engine is needed — `Domain/Fts.js` runs the exact tokenization regexes so `\S`/`\s` stay
byte-identical). The npm-installed `purs` compiles them to **`purescript/output/**/index.js`**
(ESM). A small hand-written barrel **`app/core.js`** re-exports what the UI needs;
**`app/shell.js`** imports it and sets **`window.mcCore = Core`** (mirroring the existing
`window.mcRich`/`mcStore`/`mcApi` bridges). The Lit views under `app/views/` **import from
`app/core.js` directly** (typed, tree-shaken); the un-bundled `docs/comments.js` reaches the same
functions through **`window.mcCore`** using the exact `if (window.mcCore) return
window.mcCore.fn(...) else …classic…` delegate idiom at ~29 sites (each classic branch is the
no-bundle fallback). **esbuild needs no command change** — it follows `core.js`'s relative imports
into `purescript/output/` and inlines everything into the one committed `docs/app.js`. `purs` emits
plain ES with **no `eval`/`Function`**, so CSP `script-src 'self'` holds.

**`app/core.js` is the TRANSLATION MEMBRANE — the ONE audited place types are erased.** PureScript
ADTs don't survive the JS boundary (`Maybe`/`Either` compile to `{tag,…}` objects the classic path
reads as truthy). So **nothing exported on `window.mcCore` (or imported by `comments.js`) returns a
raw PS constructor**: the barrel erases `Maybe a → a|null`, `Either e a →
{ok:true,value}|{ok:false,error}`, `data T → discriminant string`. Keep it tiny; type safety lives
inside PureScript. A `webtest` asserts the `window.mcCore` shape so a PS rename fails headlessly.

**Toolchain (npm-12 + byte-determinism + no-globals safe).**
- **The `purs` compiler is an npm devDependency** (`purescript@0.15.16`, exact-pinned) restored by
  `npm ci` like everything else. npm-12 blocks its install-script by default, so the approval is
  **committed in `package.json`'s `allowScripts`** (`"purescript@0.15.16": true`) — `npm ci` then
  runs the approved postinstall and materializes **`node_modules/.bin/purs`**. **No vendored
  binary** (`make purs`/`vendor/purs/` are gone).
- **`spago`** is a clean pure-JS **devDependency** (no install-script); it only resolves deps +
  orchestrates, shelling out to the npm-installed `purs` (found on PATH). Package set pinned in
  `purescript/spago.yaml` (`registry: 80.2.0`).
- **`make psbuild`** = `npm run build:ps` = `rm -rf purescript/output` (determinism guard) then
  `spago build` (spago finds `node_modules/.bin/purs` on the npm-run PATH) → `purescript/output/`.
  **`make bundle` runs it via `npm run build:js`** (which does `build:ps` then esbuild), and a bare
  `npm run build:js` is self-sufficient too. **`make pstest`** runs the Node-native pure-unit tests
  (`tests/purescript/*.test.mjs`, one file per `Domain` module, over the compiled ESM output — the
  PureScript slice of `make tests`; see the **Testing policy** section).
- **eslint**: `eslint.config.js` **ignores `purescript/output/**`** (generated) and **lints
  `app/core.js` + `purescript/src/**/*.js`** (hand-written FFI). `.gitignore`: `purescript/output/`,
  `purescript/.spago/`. The committed artifact stays `docs/app.js` (PS output is inlined into it);
  `output/` is never committed.
- **`?v=N` law applies to PS exactly as to any bundle change**: `docs/sw.js` caches `app.js` by
  exact URL, so any `docs/app.js` byte change **must** bump `app.js?v=N` in `docs/nav.js`.
  Determinism holds given pinned `purs` + pinned package set + the `rm -rf output` guard; a `purs`
  version bump is a deliberate, reviewed, `?v=`-bumping event.

**A slice ships only when all FOUR gates pass:** (1) byte-deterministic rebuild (`make bundle` twice
→ `git diff --exit-code docs/app.js`); (2) parity with the JS it replaces (`make pstest` + headless
delegate-vs-classic equality — this licenses deleting the classic fallback); (3) `npm run lint`
green; (4) headless render parity against a **locally served** bundle (`make serve` +
`MC_BASE=http://127.0.0.1:8000` — never test an unshipped slice against prod), console clean. Then
bump `?v=` and ship. (Where a classic fallback is genuinely dead — a bundled-only view — delete it
as the closing step; the `comments.js` fallbacks are kept ON PURPOSE as the no-bundle path.)

**Lit stays presentational.** "Keep Lit unchanged" = the library (vendored
`app/vendor/lit-all.min.js`), the ``html`` templates, and the rendered output stay; the view
**files** get thinner as reducers/permission-checks/math/parsing move into PS. **FFI is a small,
typed escape hatch** in `purescript/src/Ffi/*` for the genuine browser surfaces only: WebSocket
lifecycle (`app/live.js`), `fetch`/retry/pace (`app/api.js`), WebCrypto AES-GCM + tweetnacl X25519
for DM E2E, `localStorage`, DOM-safe render (no `innerHTML`), Turnstile, history/popstate soft-nav,
the Audio dock. Never move ordinary logic into JS to dodge PureScript.

**The worker shares the kernel — DONE.** `comments-worker/src/index.js` **imports the same
`purescript/output/`** for its 9 shared surfaces — `Rank` (ranks), `Faith`
(`FAITHS`/`FAITH_LABELS`), `Profile` (`MAX_*` caps), `Pseudonym` (`displayName`), `Dm` (`DM_TTLS`),
`Scripture` (the `/config` bible table + `MERECAT_BIBLE`'s 66-book core), `Fts` (the `SafeMatch`
`buildMatch`/`merecatMatch`), `Board` (`CAT_META`/`BOARD_CATS`/`ADMIN_CAT`), `Emoji`
(`EMOJI_PACKS`/`NAMED_EMOJI`). Every constant/table/validator the worker and client both held is now
single-sourced from one kernel — the **"two files must stay identical" hazard class is closed at the
root**, and the old bio-cap drift (client 1000 vs worker `MAX_BIO` 500) is fixed
(`Profile.limits.bio = 500`, one source). `make worker-deploy` runs `psbuild` before wrangler;
wrangler's esbuild follows the `../../purescript/output/...` imports and inlines them (CSP-clean, no
`eval`). When adding a NEW shared constant, put it in a `Domain.*` module and import it both sides —
do not reintroduce a duplicated copy.

- **The review's "two UIs" was stale; nine comments still said "no-bundle fallback" (2026-09-17)** —
  the 2026-09-17 standing review counted thirteen Lit elements and fourteen `window.mcViews` doors
  and reported the 09-16 review's P1-3 ("two complete UIs") still open. It was not: the *One UI*
  passage above had deleted the thirteen classic bodies the day before, every door is an
  unconditional one-liner, and no fallback renders. The classic client is the WRITE half of one UI —
  the composer, the DM thread, merecat, the seven admin consoles, the feed, journal, saved, join,
  the profile editors — and Lit the read half plus the chrome; the coupling runs Lit → classic
  through `window.mcKit` (64 members). The owner, told the stale finding, asked whether the classic
  UI could simply be dropped as dead code; it cannot, and the road is the write-path port, designed
  in `docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-17-port-plan.md` (P0–P7, ~23 agent-days, invariants first)
  and not begun — CODEBASE.md's roadmap carries the row. What WAS stale in the tree: nine comments
  (`client/profile.ts`, `client/comments.ts`, `app/richtext.ts`, `app/views/post.ts`,
  `app/shell.ts`, `app/core.ts`) still describing an "un-bundled comments.js" and a "deliberate
  no-bundle fallback" — rewritten to say what stands: the bundle always stands, the boot waits for
  it, and the two frozen copies still behind `if (window.mcRich)` / `if (window.mcCore)` in the
  classic helpers retire with the port's P1. Lesson for the next reviewer: a count of elements and
  doors looks like duplication; read one door's body, or the dated log, before calling anything
  twice-built.

## Testing policy

**Tests here exist to CLARIFY what the code does for a human reading the repo, and to GUARD the
rules that would break silently — not to chase coverage.** There is **no numeric coverage target**
(no "99%"), and no test for a trivial getter. Write the tests a human would write: each names a real
behavior and locks it; the assertions document ACTUAL behavior (derive expected values by
reading/running the code, never guess). If a piece of logic is worth a human understanding it and
getting it wrong would break something, it deserves a test; otherwise it doesn't. Tests that clarify
the codebase are the goal — AI-compulsion "test everything" is the anti-goal.

**`make tests`** is the standing unit suite (`tests/`, full how-to in `tests/README.md`). It runs
`psbuild` first (so the compiled `Domain.*` output the tests import is fresh), then: **PureScript +
JS via Node's built-in runner** (`node --test` + `node:assert/strict` — no new dep, matches the
stdlib/lockfile discipline) over `tests/**/*.test.mjs`, and **Python + the CSS/build invariants via
stdlib `unittest`** (pytest is not installed) over `tests/**/test_*.py`. `make pstest` is the fast
PureScript-only alias. One file per concern: `tests/purescript/` (one per `Domain` module — the
rulebook: rank, FTS injection-safety, route priority, scripture, access, auth, pager, …),
`tests/js/` (the `app/core.js` membrane coercions, `store.js` cache/dedup, `api.js`
request+invalidation), `tests/worker/` (the security-critical worker helpers), `tests/py/`
(nav/frontmatter/converter helpers, the cross-language slug parity, the serve/llm math),
`tests/css/` (the Tailwind `source(none)`/freshness invariants on the committed `docs/style.css`).

**Three layers, so a new test has an obvious home.** Layer 1 = these unit tests (`tests/`): pure,
hermetic, fast. Layer 2 = `webtest/`: headless Chromium render/soft-nav parity, mostly vs prod (see
below). A pure function (validation, permission, parsing, math, a state transition, a formatting
rule) is Layer 1. Something only observable when client JS runs in a real browser (anchor jumps,
board/DM rendering, scroll-to-comment) is Layer 2 — don't force it into the unit layer. The center
of gravity is the PureScript `Domain` layer: its modules already distil the app's rules, so each
`tests/purescript/*.test.mjs` reads as that rule's spec (and covers the classic no-bundle fallbacks
too, which mirror the same logic). A few pure worker helpers that aren't in PureScript yet —
IP/ban-key normalization and the back-room privacy predicate `boardEventPublic` — were extracted
into `comments-worker/src/pure.js` so they can be unit-tested in plain Node (`index.js` itself
imports `cloudflare:workers` and can't be); behavior is unchanged, and the extraction is a stepping
stone toward the PureScript kernel.

**`make tests` must pass before any commit or push (standing gate).** Green unit tests are a
precondition to shipping — run `make tests` before you commit/push, and before `make worker-deploy`
(which also runs `jscheck` + `psbuild`). If a change breaks a test, either the change is wrong or
the rule genuinely moved: fix the code, or update the test IN THE SAME COMMIT to document the new
rule (never delete a test to make a red build green). This sits alongside the existing gates — `make
jscheck` after any worker/client JS edit, `make check` for links — and the four PureScript ship
gates.

**WHEN to add a test (the standing rule for future work).** When you add or change a rule — a
validation, a permission, a state transition, a parser, a piece of math, a shared constant/table —
add or adjust its test **in the same change**; the test is that rule's documentation (the same
discipline as the `?v=` bump and the nav `PAGES` list). Prefer to put new application logic in a
`Domain.*` module and test it under `tests/purescript/` — that keeps the rule single-sourced (client
+ worker) AND testable in one move. Do **not** add tests for trivial one-liners or for
DOM/network-coupled code (that is Layer 2's job; the pure logic under it usually already lives in a
`Domain` module). A slice's Layer-1 parity test is one of the four ship gates above and is what
licenses deleting a classic fallback.

- **The nightly report goes out as a browser (2026-09-17)** — the first scheduled run of
  `scripts/webtest_nightly.py` (04:30) finished and then failed to report: `urllib`'s own user agent
  is answered 403 "error code: 1010" by Cloudflare on the workers.dev host as on the zone, so the
  Health card never heard from it. The report now sends the browser UA `publish_pdfs.py` sends, and
  `tests/py/test_webtest_nightly.py` holds it there. The same morning showed what a crowded box does
  to the suites: a manual run overlapping the scheduled one, orphaned Chrome trees from killed runs
  and another session's browsers left 300 MB free, and suites died with no summary (a WebDriver call
  timing out, `hcc3.html` failing to finish loading after thirty large pages in one session) — the
  page loads in six seconds alone. Before chasing a no-summary crash, check `free -m` and `pgrep -f
  cloakbrowser`, and never start a run while the timer's run is going.

- **A sweep that asserts absence must prove its reach (2026-09-17)** — the first guard written
  after the `/recent` disclosure called all 129 routes anonymously with an empty body and a
  SENTINEL `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, so 114 of its calls were refused at the origin gate before any
  handler ran: it proved the GET routes and nothing else, and a leak behind a POST or a key would
  have passed green. `tests/_support/sweep.mjs` takes every road as anon, a member, an outsider and
  an admin (the gates real: a real origin, the admin in `ADMIN_HASHES`, the members established)
  against a fresh ledger per call, seeded with a sentinel in every secret the Env declares that is
  not a public var and in every private column — fingerprint IPs, user agents and timezones, the
  alert address and webhooks, reports, held and back-room posts, the call's SDP, DM ciphertext and
  per-reader sealed keys, the librarian conversation — each with the readers allowed to see it.
  `ROUTE_HINTS` give the bodies a success path needs (uploads go multipart; the harness's `call`
  now passes a `FormData`), so every one of the 129 routes answers some identity with success, and
  the admin reaches 123 with a 200. The roads outside the table are taken too: the workers.dev
  doors, `/@handle` (through a small `HTMLRewriter` stand-in), both upgrades, the back room's
  attachment, and the four cron chains — whose alert mail, Discord posts and backup object are read.
  `tests/worker/env_leak.test.mjs` asserts no secret anywhere, no private value to a reader outside
  its list, no "Bad origin.", no handler throw, no egress refusal (in CI a refusal is a handler bug,
  never a pass — the per-call log is read, since the ledger note is throttled), and per-identity
  REACH FLOORS (19 / 84 / 63 / 123 on the day): lowering one is a decision stated in its commit. It
  also runs the env-flow law (`tests/_support/env_flow.mjs`): Node's own type stripper, espree, and
  each call's callee resolved to its definition, so the env may reach only a parameter named `env`
  (or an entry's `rawEnv`), or a truth test before a property read, in every file of both workers;
  two named shapes are allowed and an allowance nobody uses fails. `sweep_control.test.mjs` plants
  leaks around the worker and in synthetic sources and shows each detector firing. The DO runtime
  the hub test built moved to `tests/_support/hub_runtime.mjs`. The whole sweep is ~15 s.

- **Every answer keeps its committed shape, and the lists the wire promises are kept at both ends
  (2026-09-17)** — the `/recent` leak changed the SHAPE of a public answer (`items` became one
  object with thirty keys) and the Recent activity view read `d.items.length` as "Nothing here
  yet" for six weeks. `tests/_support/shapes.mjs` reads the leak sweep's answers into key paths and
  JSON types (data-keyed maps — categories, hashes, emoji codes, IP addresses — collapsed to `*`)
  and `scripts/response_shapes.mjs --write` commits them as
  `tests/_support/response_shapes.json`, 129 routes; `tests/worker/response_shapes.test.mjs` fails
  on any new, lost or retyped key, so a change to what a route answers is a diff someone reads. The
  top-level fields a successful answer ALWAYS carries as lists are `Domain.Wire.lists` (45
  routes), held to the snapshot; `brokenFields` is the rule (a listed field present and neither a
  list nor null). The worker (`serve.ts`) notes such an answer — `shape_broken`, an `ops_shapes`
  tally, told once a day, the health verdict red for a day, a Health card line — and lets it go; the
  page refuses it: the shell wraps `fetch` once (`app/wirecheck.ts`), so any `/api/` answer's
  `json()` rejects and the view lands in its "could not be loaded" state, and `app/store.ts` never
  caches one. `tests/py/test_api_parity.py` now holds every JSON GET's documented `{ok, …}`
  top-level keys to what the sweep saw it answer: `/config`'s `calls` and `turnstile` and the
  whole `/journal` read were undocumented, and are documented now. `webtest/test_public_shapes.py`
  joins the nightly: every public read (and alice's unread counts) on production against the
  snapshot — top-level keys known, lists lists, no key named like a worker binding or secret — 52
  checks on its first run, all passing.

- **The nightly's browsers filled a RAM-backed /tmp, and every tab crashed (2026-09-17)** — the
  nightly run started by hand after the pipeline's key moved reported eight regressions: 93 and 98
  failures in two suites, all `webdriver: tab crashed`, the first a `localStorage` read refused on a
  page that never loaded. Run alone on an idle box, the worst suite still crashed; the box's `/tmp`
  is a 1.9 GB tmpfs and stood at 100%, holding 246 browser profiles — `webtest/flows.py` and
  `audit.py` make a fresh `--user-data-dir` per run and never removed one, and a suite the nightly
  killed at its timeout left its chromedriver and browser alive for hours, holding theirs. With the
  dead profiles gone the same suite passed with a clean console, so the client was never the cause
  (the morning's five regressions had the same shape). Now `close()` removes its own profile, and
  `scripts/webtest_nightly.py` runs each suite in its own process group and kills the group at the
  timeout (proven: a suite's grandchild outlives nothing). This alert and an earlier one, from a run
  in an agent worktree that lacks `librarian/.key`, were both false; a proof run of the nightly
  belongs to the systemd unit in the shared checkout. That proof run, on the cleaned box, was green
  but for one console line — a GitHub Pages 503 for `dr.json` minutes after a deploy, answering 200
  again when checked — and it too reached the owner as a regression. So a regressed suite now runs
  once more and only a failure that comes back is told, in the words first seen; a suite clean on
  its re-run says so in its report line (`confirm`, `suite_line`; `test_webtest_nightly.py`). The
  same run put two suites on a leftover driver: a driver that a killed run left behind keeps its
  port, the new run's own driver exits at once, the kit asked whatever answered there for its
  session, and its `close()` stopped only the driver already gone. The kit now takes a free port
  when its own is held and refuses a driver that exited at start (`driver_port`, `start_driver`,
  which `audit.py` shares; `test_webtest_kit.py`).

- **A route ships alone, is documented by calling it, and is read by a security review first
  (2026-09-17)** — the disclosure's last remedy is about how a change is read. The line that leaked
  rode in `27fd749`: 55 files and 5,209 changed lines under one message, a diff no reviewer weighs
  line by line. API.md gave `/recent` as `{ok, items, page, more}` the whole six weeks, right about
  every key and silent about `items` being the env, so a written contract checks names and the
  types come from what the code is seen to answer. Hence CICD §11 and the first of CLAUDE.md's
  silent laws: a new worker route is its own commit; it brings the `ROUTE_HINTS` entry that lets
  `env_leak.test.mjs` see it succeed for someone, its entry in the committed response shapes, its
  `{ok, …}` shape in API.md (which `test_api_parity.py` holds to what the sweep saw), and a list it
  always answers goes into `Domain.Wire`; and `/security-review` reads it before a public route
  ships. The review ran on every phase of the remedy and found one real hole, in the pipeline:
  since 2026-09-09 `merecat.yml` had spliced its `only` dispatch input unquoted into the ingest's
  command line, so anyone who could dispatch the workflow (the private shelf's dispatch token
  included) could append `--api` and receive the ingest's credential. It is now one validated
  argument, and `test_pipeline_workflows.py` refuses any input spliced into a script. Two
  credentials the env named are still the owner's to rotate, because no token on the dev box can
  mint them: the TURN key and `CF_USAGE_TOKEN` (CICD §4).

- **A fresh open painted last visit's unread count, and now paints none (2026-09-17)** — the owner's
  report, and the sharper half of it was the question it came with: whether readers should be pinned
  to one backend. They should not, and the flicker was not the database at all — the timing ruled
  replicas out, because the numbers were seen jumping before replication was ever switched on. The
  cause was in the chrome: `badgeCount` read `mc-dm-unread` / `mc-notif-unread` and painted whatever
  number was stored, at any age, while the fresh read was still in flight; both classic checks made
  it worse by RE-STAMPING the stored count (`{n: c.n, at: now}`) to keep parallel boots quiet, which
  told the chrome that last visit's number was current. Recorded from the first frame on a
  phone-sized fresh open with a planted count: the bar painted 5 at 211 ms and dropped it at 343 ms
  — on a slow link the wrong number stands for as long as the read takes. The rule now lives in the
  kernel: `Domain.Cache.badgeShows` says a stored count may be PAINTED only while it is Fresh
  (`badgeTtlMs`, the same ninety seconds both clients always used) — the ladder's lower rungs serve
  CONTENT while it refreshes, and a count is not content. `badgeCount` asks the kernel through
  `app/core.ts` and paints nothing without it; `app/badges.ts` asks for an unpaintable count AT ONCE
  on the fresh open (no debounce, one read per open, none per soft navigation, none when the stored
  number is still fresh) and a live frame keeps its 300 ms debounce; `dmUnreadCheck` and
  `notifUnreadCheck` defer to the shell where it stands and otherwise use an in-flight flag instead
  of a dishonest stamp. So a badge now appears a moment later than it used to, and never appears
  wrong. Proven by `webtest/test_badge_freshness.py` (the recorder that caught it: a stale count is
  never painted on the inbox or the community page, the bar ends on the server's answer, and a FRESH
  stored count still paints at 233 ms — the instant badge is not lost), which went 5/7 against the
  deployed bug before the fix shipped; plus `tests/purescript/cache.test.mjs` (the rung boundaries,
  a backwards clock included) and `tests/js/badges.test.mjs` (the chrome asks the kernel, the boot
  asks at once, neither classic road re-stamps). The nightly runs it (`SUITES`, its baseline entry
  written by hand rather than re-baselining every suite on a box whose browsers were timing out).
  What this did NOT need: sticky sessions. Workers hold no per-reader state, D1's own bookmark
  cookie already carries read-your-writes, the hub is sticky per member by hash, and the rate
  limiter is per location and permissive by design — pinning readers anywhere would only cost
  latency.

- **A phone's first paint is already the app, so a cold open stops looking like a reload
  (2026-09-17)** — the owner's second report, and the real one: "a flash like an extra page reload
  on a fresh open of the app and a subsequent selection of inbox or community; after it caches it
  loads normally". It was neither the badge nor the database. Held still by blocking `app.js` on a
  390-wide viewport, the first paint was the PLAIN SITE: a centred `h1.home-title`, the desktop
  `<footer>`'s two link rows, no app bar, no tab bar — and then, on a cold cache hundreds of
  milliseconds later (`body` gaining `mc-sb-wide mc-app` at 670 ms against an interactive document
  at 261 ms in one recording), the shell replaced all three at once. The page changed identity in
  front of the reader, which is exactly what a reload looks like; once the bundle was cached the
  same change happened inside a frame and nobody saw it. Now the stylesheet dresses a phone as the
  app until the shell arrives: under `@media (max-width: 600px)`, `body:not(.mc-app)` draws the two
  bars' own surfaces as `::before` / `::after` (the same `--mc-appbar-h` / `--mc-tabbar-h` heights
  whose space `body`'s padding already reserved, `var(--surface)`, `pointer-events: none`) and hides
  the page title and the static footer — the two pieces of furniture the shell takes away in any
  case. The placeholders paint NO text: a guessed title or tab label would be a second lie, and the
  real bars arrive into their own shape. The `?app=0` reader keeps the plain site: `docs/nav.js`
  stamps `html.mc-noapp` the moment it reads the latch, and every rule is gated on
  `html:not(.mc-noapp)`. Nothing in the inline `mc-fout` script changed, so the CSP hashes stand.
  Proven by `webtest/test_first_paint.py` (21 checks: the frozen frame on three tab pages, the
  escape hatch, and the settled page's real chrome) and `tests/css/test_style_css.py`'s new class;
  the phone-footer test there had to loosen its exact-string match, because the minifier now merges
  the new `display:none` selectors into that rule's own list. Not done: baking the bars' real markup
  and labels into the built pages (a true app shell), which would make the first frame
  indistinguishable from the settled one rather than merely the same shape.

- **The two fixed bars ride their own bundle, ahead of the shell (2026-09-17)** — the owner's flash
  had two halves. The stylesheet's pre-shell dress (the passage above) stopped a phone from painting
  the plain site, but the bars themselves still waited for app.js: ~290 KB of shell before a cold
  open had its chrome. They are now their own esbuild ENTRY. `app/chromebits.ts` holds what the bars
  are — `ICON`, `TABS`, `activeTab`, `pageTitle`, the badge readers and the two Lit elements, all
  moved out of `appchrome.ts` — and `app/chrome.ts` is a fifteen-line entry that mounts them;
  `app/app.ts` is the shell's entry, so the shell's output keeps the name every page has always
  loaded. One esbuild invocation builds both with `--splitting`, so Lit and the bar code live in ONE
  shared chunk instead of twice over: `docs/chrome.js` is 216 bytes and its chunk 22 KB, against
  app.js's 270. `docs/nav.js` asks for the bars first (a module, like the shell), primes them into
  the service worker's cache beside app.js and style.css, and the stamper stamps the new key
  (`NAV_ASSETS`). `mountBars` is idempotent and `installChrome` calls it, so whichever bundle lands
  first puts up exactly one pair. The measured lesson: the early bundle must import NOTHING but Lit
  — one `import … from './core.ts'` for the tap kernel put the whole domain layer in the shared
  chunk and took it from 22 KB to 102 KB, so `armTap` stayed in `appchrome.ts` and the shell arms
  the two bars the moment it lands (a press before that follows the link natively, as every other
  link does). `html.mc-bars`, set by `mountBars`, retires the stylesheet's placeholders, so the
  surfaces the first paint drew are replaced in place by the real bars and nothing moves.
  `webtest/test_first_paint.py` now freezes all three states by blocking what has not arrived —
  nothing loaded, the early bundle alone (real bars, six labels, the right tab lit, the page's
  title, one pair), and everything — plus the `?app=0` latch, which the early bundle honours too: 44
  checks, green against a local build of the commit. The budget file gained `chrome.js` and
  `chrome-chunk` (resolved through the early bundle's own import, since the chunk's name carries a
  content hash), and app.js's ceiling came down 291 KB → 275 KB.

## Infrastructure as code (Terraform, 2026-09-08)

**`terraform/` holds the DURABLE infrastructure, adopted by import from what already existed —
Terraform did not create any of it, and the adopting apply changed nothing (`40 to import, 0 to add,
0 to change, 0 to destroy`).** Terraform 1.16, providers `cloudflare/cloudflare ~> 5.24` and
`integrations/github ~> 6.13`, both pinned in a committed `.terraform.lock.hcl`.

**THE BOUNDARY IS THE DEPLOY, and it is the whole design.** `wrangler deploy` rewrites a worker's
**routes, cron triggers, bindings, vars and secrets** on every run. Had Terraform declared those
too, the two tools would fight forever — every `make worker-deploy` would produce a Terraform diff
and every `terraform apply` would produce a wrangler diff. So: **Terraform owns** the zone, three
deliberate zone settings (`ssl=full` — NOT strict, the origin is GitHub Pages; `always_use_https`;
`browser_cache_ttl=0`, which is what makes the `?v=` immutable-asset scheme work), all 18 DNS
records, the three custom rulesets, `bot_management`, the six R2 buckets, the four D1 databases **as
records that they exist**, the two Turnstile widgets, and both GitHub repos. **wrangler keeps**
every worker script/version/binding/var/secret/route/cron and the D1 migrations; **ingest.py keeps**
the librarian rooms' contents and the Vectorize index. Do not move anything across that line.

**Codifying `bot_management` closes a real trap**: its API is a FULL REPLACE, which is exactly how a
partial `PUT` once silently reset `ai_bots_protection`/`ai_training`/`crawler_protection` (see the
zone-bot-systems memory). Terraform always sends the whole object. **And the response-header ruleset
(`e61208bc…` / rule `1dc65168…`) is now IN THE REPO** — the CSP, HSTS, `Permissions-Policy:
microphone=(self)` and `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` that every earlier note called "a dashboard
rule not in the repo" are `terraform/rulesets.tf`. That passage is now history; edit the ruleset
here and plan it.

**The drift the PDF move left is ADOPTED (2026-09-09)**: `cloudflare_r2_bucket.files`,
`cloudflare_dns_record.files` and `cloudflare_ruleset.redirects` (phase
`http_request_dynamic_redirect`, the `/<name>.pdf` → `files.merecatholicity.com` 301 scoped to the
two site hosts) are declared with import blocks in `imports.tf`, alongside the GitHub environments
(`github-pages`, and the new **`terraform-production`** approval gate with its `main`-only branch
policy), the two public-id Actions variables, and **GitHub Pages itself via
`github_repository_pages.site`** (`build_type = "workflow"`, the CNAME — the provider deprecated the
`pages` block inside `github_repository`). The lesson that stands: **infrastructure created by hand
during a migration does not announce itself to Terraform — import it in the same change, or write
down that you did not.**

**TERRAFORM RUNS FROM CI NOW — `.github/workflows/terraform.yml` (2026-09-09).** It fires only when
`terraform/**` (or the workflow/summarizer) changes, plus `workflow_dispatch`. `plan` runs on PRs
and pushes; `apply` runs only from `main` (or a dispatch with `apply=true`) and **waits on the
`terraform-production` environment**, whose required reviewer is the owner
(`prevent_self_review=false`, since the pusher is the sole member; `can_admins_bypass=true`). A
human approves with *Review deployments* on the run; **the AI operator approves with
`scripts/ci_approve.sh <run-id> --approve`** — it downloads the `tf-plan-summary` artifact, prints
it, lists the pending environments and POSTs the same `pending_deployments` review the button does.
Three rules are built in: (a) **this repository is PUBLIC, so plan output is never printed and the
plan file is never uploaded** — the binary plan embeds the prior state (Turnstile secrets);
reviewers see `scripts/tf_plan_summary.py`'s rendering of `terraform show -json` — address, action,
changed attribute NAMES, values only for attributes the provider does not mark sensitive, imports
shown as `import`; (b) **any delete/replace fails the plan** (the standing law) unless run by hand
with `allow_destroy=true`; (c) the apply job **re-plans and refuses if the address/action set
differs from what was reviewed**. Concurrency group `terraform`, never cancelled mid-apply. The R2
state backend's S3 pair is **derived from `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`** (access key = the token's `id`
from `/user/tokens/verify`, secret = SHA-256 of the token value — Cloudflare's documented scheme),
so one secret serves everything; `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` secrets override it if
a dedicated R2 token is ever preferred. Secrets: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (permissions listed in
`terraform/README.md`) and `TF_GITHUB_TOKEN` (the owner's `gh auth token` — scopes `repo, workflow,
read:org` — rotate to a fine-grained PAT any time). A fork's PR sees no secrets and gets `fmt` +
offline `validate` only. **The credentials (2026-09-09).** The CI token is an ACCOUNT-owned
Cloudflare token named `merecatholicity-ci` (id `f994cb0b688fba9afb711fa8c5645041`, no expiry),
**minted through the API** from a temporary 30-day bootstrap token the owner issued — `POST
/accounts/{id}/tokens` with two policies, zone-scoped to merecatholicity.com and account-scoped,
using exactly the permission groups listed in `terraform/README.md` (the real group names, found by
listing `/tokens/permission_groups`: `Zone Transform Rules Write`, `Dynamic URL Redirects Write`,
`Turnstile Sites Write`, not the dashboard's colon-style labels). Its R2 pair is derived (access key
= token id, secret = SHA-256 of the token value — proven against the pair the dashboard shows for
the bootstrap token). Everything lives in **`~/.config/merecatholicity/ci.env`** (mode 600, outside
the repo) and in the four Actions secrets `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`,
`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `TF_GITHUB_TOKEN`; the two public ids are Actions *variables* Terraform
manages. **The bootstrap token should be revoked** once nothing needs it — it was pasted into a
chat. **The security review of 2026-09-09 (findings acted on, in order of weight).** (1) **Secrets
are never present on `pull_request` events, in any workflow** — a same-repo PR could otherwise
publish to R2 and purge from the build job, and a PR's HCL is what `terraform plan` evaluates (an
`http`/`external` data source would read the tokens at plan time, before the gate); PRs now get
build + gates without credentials and `fmt` + offline `validate` for Terraform, and the plan that is
reviewed is always the push's. (2) **One token became three**: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (terraform:
zone/buckets/turnstile/D1-read, cannot purge or deploy), `CLOUDFLARE_SITE_TOKEN` (build.yml + manual
purge: Cache Purge + R2), `CLOUDFLARE_WORKERS_TOKEN` (workers.yml: scripts/routes/D1 write); the
broad `merecatholicity-ci` token was deleted; bucket-scoped R2 item permissions were tried for the
site token and refused for the list endpoint. (3) **Every `uses:` is pinned to a commit SHA** with
its version in a comment, `sha_pinning_required` and `allowed_actions = selected` (GitHub-owned +
verified creators) are declared in Terraform (`github_actions_repository_permissions.site`), and
`.github/dependabot.yml` keeps the pins fresh by weekly PR (which, per rule 1, carries nothing).
**The policy applies to NESTED references too**: GitHub's own `actions/upload-pages-artifact`
composite calls `actions/upload-artifact@v4` by tag inside itself, and the first Build after the
policy went live failed at *Set up job* on exactly that — so build.yml carries the composite's two
steps inline (its `tar` + a pinned `upload-artifact` named `github-pages`, which is all
`deploy-pages` needs). Prefer plain actions over composites here. (4) **`pages: write` + `id-token:
write` live on the deploy job only**; every workflow is `contents: read` at the top. (5) **Secret
scanning, push protection and Dependabot security updates are ON** and declared in `github.tf` (they
had been pinned OFF; all free on a public repository). (6) **`can_admins_bypass = false`** on
`terraform-production`: the gate is the only road. (7) Provider errors on a failed plan/apply print
**summary lines only** — a provider error can echo attribute values, and the log is public. Left
deliberate: no branch protection on `main` (push-is-the-deploy; the gate is the guard on
infrastructure), and the private-shelf repo's scanning stays off (GHAS on a private repo is paid).
**`TF_GITHUB_TOKEN` is a fine-grained PAT since the same day** (no expiry;
Administration/Environments/Variables/Pages read-write + Metadata read on the two repositories
