only), replacing the `gh` CLI token whose `workflow` scope could rewrite workflows; proven by a
local plan and a dispatched CI plan, both `No changes`. GitHub has NO API for minting a PAT of
either kind — the browser form is the one step only the owner can do — and the two org toggles it
needed (*allow fine-grained PATs*, *must expire* unchecked) have neither a Terraform resource nor a
REST endpoint, so they join the exceptions list as hand-set org policy. **Proven the same day:** run
34316447061 planned the adoption (8 imports, 2 creates, 0 changes, 0 destroys), waited on
`terraform-production`, was reviewed and approved with `scripts/ci_approve.sh` after a strict
comparison of the summary artifact against the expected fingerprint, and applied; `terraform plan`
locally answers `No changes`. The Workers workflow's first credentialed dispatch applied the ledger
(nothing pending) and deployed both workers. **Bootstrap order matters and bit once in planning: a
workflow that names an environment GitHub has not seen makes GitHub CREATE it — with NO protection
rules — so the gate was created via the API (reviewer + branch policy) BEFORE any workflow
referenced it, then imported.**

**Four things CANNOT be managed, and the reason is the provider, not a preference:** **Vectorize**
(`merecat-t1`) has no resource at all in the provider (259 resources, zero vectorize);
**`cloudflare_r2_custom_domain`** (the `audio.merecatholicity.com` binding) and
**`cloudflare_calls_turn_app`** (the `merecatholicity-calls` TURN key) exist but support NO import —
declaring either would CREATE a duplicate, and a new TURN app would mint a new id and break
`TURN_KEY_ID` and every voice call; and **`cloudflare_email_routing_settings`** cannot even be READ
by provider 5.24.0 (`Struct defines fields not found in object: support_subaddress` — a provider
decoder bug), so Email Routing stays dashboard-managed while its MX/SPF/DMARC/DKIM records are
managed in `dns.tf`. GitHub **Pages** WAS the fifth exception until 2026-09-09; it is managed now
through `github_repository_pages.site` (see above), adopted as its own import with a no-op plan.

**State lives in R2** (`merecatholicity-tfstate`, S3-compatible backend, `use_lockfile` — R2's
conditional writes make locking work). That bucket is deliberately unmanaged: a state store managed
by its own state cannot be bootstrapped. **STATE IS SECRET** (Turnstile secret keys, among others)
**and this repo is PUBLIC** — `terraform/.gitignore` keeps every `*.tfstate*` out of git; never
defeat it, and never put a credential in a file under `terraform/`. Auth is four environment
variables: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and the
`AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` R2 pair. The wrangler OAuth token in
`~/.config/.wrangler` is NOT usable (short-lived, refresh-based), and the **Cloudflare MCP grant
cannot create or even list API tokens** (`9109: Unauthorized`) — a token comes from the dashboard.

**Blast radius:** the account is shared with four other zones (`adamschaefers.com`, `cypher2k1.net`,
`enchant.games`, `trophybrowns.com`) and carries Turnstile widgets for two of them, which are
deliberately NOT adopted. Zone-scoped resources are pinned to this zone, but **account-scoped ones
(R2, D1, Turnstile) cannot be narrowed by the API**. **NEVER apply a plan showing a destroy or a
replace** — a replaced `cloudflare_d1_database` destroys the comments database, a replaced
`r2_bucket` destroys its objects. `prevent_destroy` guards every stateful resource; reading the plan
is the real defence. Adopting something new is an `import` block + `terraform plan
-generate-config-out=generated.tf`, fold the generated HCL into the topic file, iterate to `No
changes`, then apply. Two gotchas: an import block's `provider =` argument is legal ONLY while
generating config (it errors once the resource is declared), and the generator emits read-only
attributes like `etag` that must be stripped or they show as permanent drift.

- **Every bucket's r2.dev URL is off, and declared (2026-09-17)** — the leak review found the audio
  bucket serving a second public door: its `pub-….r2.dev` URL was enabled beside
  `audio.merecatholicity.com`, and nothing used it (the reader and the dock build
  `https://audio.merecatholicity.com/<book>/<chapter>.mp3`; the `public.r2.dev` in `dns.tf` is the
  custom domains' CNAME target, which the switch does not govern). The owner chose to close it.
  `r2.tf` declares `cloudflare_r2_managed_domain.dev_url` for all seven buckets with `enabled =
  false`, so a door opened by hand is drift in the plan. Provider 5.24.0 has no import for the
  resource (its methods are create/read/update/delete, and the create is a PUT of the switch), so
  adoption is a create: the API read first showed six buckets already off and audio on, the local
  plan showed exactly `7 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy`, and the gated apply is six no-ops and
  the one flip. Before the apply the custom domain served the chapters (206); after it, the API must
  read every switch off while the domain still serves them.

## Cloudflare Workers (dynamic backend)

- **Two of the six leaked credentials are deliberately not rotated, on measured grounds
  (2026-09-18)** — `TURN_KEY_SECRET` and `CF_USAGE_TOKEN` need permissions no CI token holds, and
  the owner, shown what each would take, decided to leave them: recorded as CICD.md §10's
  fourteenth exception rather than re-argued here. Two of the three premises were checked before
  it was accepted, with the workers token, which turns out to read account analytics: **TURN
  relayed egress is 0.000 GB this month and nil on every day of it**, so the leaked key had not
  been used by anyone; the usage token is read-only account analytics, worth little to a holder.
  The third premise — that nobody ever fetched the leaking endpoint — is **not checkable from
  here and was not claimed**: no token on the box reads zone HTTP analytics, and the free plan
  would not retain the six-week window in any case. What carries the decision is the daily usage
  check, not hope: `turn.egress` is metered against the 1,000 GB free pool, so abuse speaks at
  800 GB — still inside the pool, 200 GB before a charge — with the one gap that the check runs
  once at 23:30 UTC. The standing note for a later reader: if that meter ever leaves zero, the
  decision is the thing to revisit first.

- **The Turnstile meter asked for a month of a dataset Cloudflare serves a week at a time
  (2026-09-17)** — the owner's usage page read `Turnstile · Challenges this month · unavailable —
  account "…" cannot request a time range wider than 1w1h, but your query time range spans
  2w3d5h26m…`. `turnstileAdaptiveGroups` was filtered `date_geq: <calendar month start>` like its
  neighbours in `fetchUsageReport`'s query map, but this account serves that dataset at most
  **1 week 1 hour** — so the card was fine for the first week of every month and "unavailable" from
  about the 8th, which is why it had never been noticed on a fresh month. (The other
  month-windowed selects — R2 operations, Vectorize queries, TURN egress — are served over the
  month and are untouched.) The window is now its own pure rule,
  `turnstileWindowStart` in `usagecalc.ts`: six days back from today's 00:00 UTC, so the span to
  now is at most 6 d 23 h 59 m, an hour inside the cap at the worst moment of a day. The row says
  what it is rather than claiming the month — "Challenges in the last 7 days", period `week` (a
  new one, which `alertBody` spells " (the last 7 days)"; the row never alerts, being unmetered) —
  and its note names the reason. `tests/worker/usage.test.mjs` locks three things: that the month
  window at the failing instant really does exceed the cap while the week window does not, at the
  widest moment of a day; that the row carries the week period and label; and that the query in
  `usage.ts` uses the week date and not `monDate`, which is the wiring a type cannot hold. The
  card the error came from was working as designed — one failed dataset costs one "unavailable"
  card and never the page.

**D1 schema changes (comments DB) go through `wrangler d1 migrations` now — NOT a hand-edited
`schema.sql`.** The persistent comments D1 (`merecatholicity-comments`) has a versioned ledger in
`comments-worker/migrations/` (baseline `0000_baseline_comments.sql`, applied; `make migrate` =
`wrangler d1 migrations apply … --remote`, `make migrate-status` = `… list`). A schema change is a
**NEW** `migrations/NNNN_name.sql` (`wrangler d1 migrations create`) — additive, `IF NOT
EXISTS`/`ALTER`/table-swap as before — then `make migrate`. **`comments-worker/schema.sql` is a
GENERATED read-only snapshot** of `migrations/` (`make schema-snapshot`); never hand-edit it (older
notes below saying "mirrored in schema.sql" now mean "add a migration + regenerate the snapshot").
The **three librarian D1s** (`merecat-library`/`-deep`/`-deep2`) are **NOT** under this ledger —
they are derived data, dropped+rebuilt by `librarian/ingest.py`, schema single-sourced in
`schema-librarian.sql` (one origin since the GPU backend's mirror of it retired, 2026-09-10).
Wrangler auto-discovers the default `migrations/` dir (no config key).

The static site delegates its two dynamic features to Workers. Both are **free-tier only** — any new
worker feature must stay within Cloudflare's free plan. Deploy each from its own directory with `npx
wrangler deploy` (wrangler is an npm devDependency, resolved from the repo-root `node_modules/`; the
comments worker goes through `make worker-deploy` = `npm run worker:deploy`, which lints first).
Config is in each directory's `wrangler.jsonc`.

**The worker's sibling imports name `.ts` files, and only `durable.ts` imports `cloudflare:workers`
(2026-09-16).** `lib.ts` and `index.ts` each carried an `import { DurableObject } from
'cloudflare:workers'` that nothing referenced, and the TypeScript siblings were imported by `.js`
names (`./lib.js` for `lib.ts`) — a habit esbuild forgives and Node does not: `import('./lib.ts')`
from a test died on `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_ESM_URL_SCHEME` before it read a line, which is why the worker
tests lifted SQL out of the source by regex. Both are gone — the dead imports deleted, the
specifiers renamed to the files that exist (`quota.ts` had done so since it was written) — so
`lib.ts`, `db.ts`, `quota.ts` and `usage.ts` load in plain Node 24 with no flag, and only `index.ts`
still reaches `cloudflare:workers`, through its re-export of the two Durable Object classes. The
bundle is proven unchanged by **`scripts/worker_bundle_set.sh <outdir>`**: the dry-run bundle
reduced to its sorted function-name set and code-line set, run on the parent commit and on the
change and diffed — the check the 2026-08-01 split prescribed, now a script every worker refactor
runs. This is the floor the behavioural test harness stands on (next passage).

**Worker handlers run in the unit suite (2026-09-16).** `tests/_support/worker.mjs` loads
`comments-worker/src/index.ts` in plain Node — one `module.registerHooks` resolver stubs the single
`cloudflare:workers` import the Durable Object re-export drags in — and drives handlers through
`default.fetch` against a real SQLite behind a D1-shaped shim (`prepare → bind → first | all | run`,
`batch` as one transaction; booleans bind as 1/0 as D1 coerces them, a missing row is `null`), with
every binding a request can touch stubbed or spying: the three rate limiters allow,
`MODERATION_MODE` is `off`, Workers AI and Vectorize throw when reached, R2 is an in-memory bucket
that records every call (and a ledger snapshot at each delete, for the hygiene rules), the hub is an
optional spy whose `viewersOf`/`dmViewing`/`presenceOf` answers are the test's to give, and `fetch`
is a spy that throws — a request that passes has provably touched no network. Turnstile passes for
an established identity with no token (`Domain.Turnstile`, the default), so `establish(db, hash)` —
one `profiles` row — is the whole login. The rules: `index.ts` is imported only through
`loadWorker()` (dynamically — ESM links a file's static graph before any hook runs; `lib.ts`,
`db.ts`, `quota.ts`, `usage.ts` import statically); `resetCaches()` in `beforeEach` (`lib.ts` caches
`app_settings` for five minutes); `node --test` runs each file in its own process, so nothing leaks
between files. **`tests/worker/routes.test.mjs`** holds the `ROUTES` table to a committed snapshot
(`tests/_support/routes.json`, 125 entries, thunk text verbatim) and probes every registered pair
through the loaded worker — a handler left unimported after a move throws on entry and answers the
router's 500, which the probe refuses; the two WebSocket doors, the origin guard and `/@handle` are
probed too. The source-rule locks read the worker through **`tests/_support/worker_src.mjs`**
(`routesSource()` = `routes/*.ts` then `index.ts`, each behind a marker line; `handlerBody(name)`
bounded by the next top-level declaration, export list or file marker), so a handler keeps its lock
wherever it lives — the precondition the 2026-08-01 route-file deferral named. Reading source stays
for laws that ARE textual (the privacy sweep, the Turnstile sweep, the one-fragment unread rule);
behaviour is proven by running the road, and the high-churn lock files are being converted one by
one.

**The handlers live in `routes/*` now (2026-09-16).** The route-file extraction deferred on
2026-08-01 landed in nine commits, each a worker deploy:
`comments-worker/src/routes/{calls,notify,media,wall,profile,dm,merecat,board,admin}.ts`, every
handler moved verbatim with the comment above it, its declaration line unchanged (so the source-rule
locks slice it wherever it lives) and one `export { … }` list at the file's foot; `index.ts` keeps
the `ROUTES` table verbatim and imports what it mounts — 654 lines: imports, `Env`,
`handleConfig`/`handleLive`, the table, `fetch`/`scheduled`. The nine helpers more than one group
shared (the social gate and its refusal, the wall-bell filter and the unread count, the four Discord
announcers) moved into `lib.ts` first, so no route file imports another except `board → merecat`
(the @mention kick), and none imports `index.ts`; `durable.ts` imports only `lib.ts`, as before.
What made it safe this time: **`scripts/worker_bundle_set.sh`** — the dry-run bundle's function-name
set and code-line set were identical before and after every commit (the 2026-08-01 hazard was a
helper reached only through the Durable Object re-export; nothing `durable.ts` imports moved, and
the check would have shown a dropped function) — plus the harness running the handlers and
`tests/worker/routes.test.mjs` holding the table to its snapshot and dispatching every entry, and
after each push `webtest/test_worker_reads.py` (30) and the DM fixture (62) against prod. The tool
that did the moving stayed outside the repo; the commits are the record. `PLAN-TODO.md` (2026-09-09,
the overnight TypeScript/ORM/MVC plan, tracked at the root against the layout rule) is retired: what
shipped of it is in CODEBASE.md's roadmap, and its repository layer is not pursued — with the
handlers runnable in the suite, inline SQL is testable where it sits.

**One UI: the classic twins of the Lit screens are gone (2026-09-16).** Thirteen screens existed
twice — a classic `viewX` in `client/*.ts` and a Lit `<mc-x>` in `app/views/*` — with the classic
body behind `if (window.mcViews && window.mcViews.X) return …`, kept as "the no-bundle fallback".
The fallback rendered for nobody: `docs/nav.js` injects `app.js` unconditionally on every page
(`?app=0` only drops the chrome), the classic boot waits for `window.mcCore` before running and
re-injects the bundle if it is late, so every delegation succeeded for a real user, and along the
one race that reached a classic body it threw at its first kernel call. The 13 bodies (board index
and category, topic, search, the post renderer, profile, inbox, users, notifications, admin home,
the two merecat views, usage) and the four helpers only they used are deleted — ~1,150 lines — and
each door delegates unconditionally, keeping its pre-steps (the back room's key guard before the
category view, the E2E badge above the inbox). The classic client is the write paths, the DM thread
(`viewDm`), merecat and every screen without a Lit twin. Verified: `tsc`, the gate, a double `make
bundle`, and on prod the Lit view sweeps (`webtest/test_board_views.py`, `test_topic_search.py`,
`test_profile_inbox.py`, `test_member_views.py`) with the DM fixture.

**The DM client is six factories (2026-09-16).** `client/dm.ts` (3,015 lines, a 1,307-line `viewDm`
among them) is `dm-crypto.ts` (the nacl loader, base64url, the X25519 pair, E1 and E3, safety
numbers, the badge), `dm-message.ts` (one message: expiry, reactions and the saved mark, the acts,
bubbles and quotes, the reply envelope, E2E media, render/edit/copy/redact/delete), `dm-pickers.ts`
(Forward and Members), `dm-inbox.ts` (the unread cache and badge, the live frame handlers and their
`mc-live` dispatcher in `run()`, the labels and search box, the inbox door), `dm-thread.ts`
(`viewDm` and the call button) and `dm-styles.ts` (the injected stylesheet). Every declaration moved
verbatim with its comment; what one factory takes from another is bound through `B` exactly as the
root's helpers are, so the wiring law (`tests/js/client_modules.test.mjs`: every binding filled and
provided, no var before bind, shared state on `B` alone) covers the six through
`tests/_support/client.mjs`'s list — its `cap()` learned the hyphen (`dm-crypto` →
`installDmCrypto`). The cross-module bindings are typed from the declarations they name (`unknown`
parameters, the declared return), and the header bindings the split would have duplicated as `any`
(`API`, `state`, `section`, `bootSig`, the blob cache, the read pacers) are typed from their root
definitions instead — so the `: any` ratchet reads lower after the split than before it. Source
rules that spanned the old file read the family as one text (`clientDm()`); the ones on a single
concern read that module.

**The bindings are typed and the DM wire shapes have a home (2026-09-16).**
`comments-worker/src/env.ts` is the worker's `Env`: every binding `wrangler.jsonc` declares (D1 ×4,
Vectorize, Workers AI, the two Durable Object namespaces, R2 ×4, the three rate limiters) and every
var and secret as an optional string — the two `{ [key: string]: any }` bags in `index.ts` and
`durable.ts` are gone, `routes/calls.ts` takes `(request: Request, env: Env, ctx: ExecutionContext)`
with a row type per query (`first<Row>()`) as the worked example, and the ChatRoom's librarian reads
carry the same generics; the other route files adopt `Env` one at a time, each step a click of the
`: any` ratchet (`tests/js/any_ratchet.test.mjs`, the count per project held at
`tests/_support/any_baseline.json` and allowed only to fall). `app/wire.ts` is the one home of the
DM wire shapes — `DmMember`, `DmMessage`, `DmThreadPayload`, `DmThreadsRow`/`DmThreadsPayload`,
`DmRosterPayload`, `DmSendBody` — types only and DOM-free, so the worker's tsconfig imports it too:
`routes/dm.ts` builds `/dm/thread`, `/dm/threads` and `/dm/roster` as those types, `app/api.ts`'s
reads are generic and return them, and the Lit inbox and the classic inbox module read their rows as
`DmThreadsRow`. API.md §4.1 is their prose. Both bundles are byte-identical to before (types erase);
the classic DM modules adopt `DmMessage` where a function still takes `m: any`, progressively, under
the same ratchet.

- **`comments-worker/`** — the comments backend. Routed on the zone at `/api/comments*`, so it is
  same-origin with the pages (no CORS). Uses **D1** for storage, **Turnstile** as the spam gate,
  **Workers AI** (Llama Guard) for screening (`MODERATION_MODE` var), rate-limit bindings for per-IP
  throttles, and a monthly cron that dumps D1 to **R2** (`merecatholicity-backups`, 90-day
  retention). Client side is `comments.js`; a page opts in with `<section class="comments"
  data-comments>` plus the script (the book edition gets it via `book-tail.html`). Identity is a
  browser-generated key; the server stores only its SHA-256 and derives a pseudonym from the hash.
  **Live forums (real-time, 2026-07-30):** the worker also hosts a **`BoardHub` Durable Object**
  (one global instance, `env.HUB.getByName('board')`, declared `new_sqlite_classes`) — a read-only
  fan-out over WebSockets so new threads/replies and the index's latest-poster/counts update for
  everyone with no refresh and no polling. Readers connect at **`GET /api/comments/live`** (Upgrade;
  dedicated `CONNECT_LIMIT`), subscribe to scopes (`board:index` | `cat:<key>` | `topic:<id>`, back
  room refused), and the DO broadcasts `new-topic`/`new-reply`/`topic-stats`/`moderation`/`moved`
  events (emitted from
  `handlePost`/`handleModerate`/`handleSelfDelete`/`handleMove`/`handleApprove`, **and
  `merecatInsertComment`** so the bot's @merecat replies and forwarded answers appear live too —
  gated `boardKey && !ADMIN_CAT && status==='live'`). Idle sockets **hibernate** (zero duration;
  free-plan-safe), auto ping/pong via `setWebSocketAutoResponse`; `BoardHub.publish` enumerates
  `ctx.getWebSockets()` (survives hibernation) and matches each socket's
  `deserializeAttachment().subs` — proven on prod that a real trigger delivers over the wire. Client
  is shell-owned **`window.mcLive`** (`app/live.js`): one lifecycle (jittered-backoff reconnect,
  close-on-hidden/idle + reopen-and-resync, degrade) managing the `board` conn and — for merecat — a
  `chat` conn (see the merecat bullet). Lit views merge pushed events into reactive state: `McTopic`
  drops a reply in live on the shown page (highlighted) and, when a reply opens/fills a **later**
  page, grows the pager and raises a "↓ N new replies — go to page K" **pill** (`requestUpdate`
  refreshes the pagers without wiping the static `.comments-list`); `McBoardCat` bumps a topic on
  reply (a light debounced `_refetchCat` when it was on a later page, since a stats event lacks the
  full row) and prepends new topics; `McBoardIndex` patches counts + latest-poster. Posting stays on
  the authenticated Turnstile HTTP path (broadcast is read-only). No WebSocket ⇒ the forum behaves
  exactly as before (manual refresh). All posting/moderation invariants (back room never crosses the
  wire, only `live` posts broadcast) preserved. **The whole chain is proven on prod headless**
  (`scratchpad/test_live_fix.py` 6/6 client merge incl. the later-page pill;
  `test_broadcast_sticky.py` 5/5 real server→socket delivery, self-reverting). NOTE: the
  pseudonym/reserved wordlists — and the faith-declaration codes (the `FAITHS` list in the worker,
  the `FAITH` label map and `FAITH_ORDER` in the client) — are duplicated in `comments.js` and
  `comments-worker/src/index.js` and must stay identical. Every member picks a faith (`nicene` /
  `indo-european` / `seeker`) at signup; it lives on the `profiles` row, is filled from the first
  post (fill-only, never overwriting an edited value), is changed in the profile editor, and shows
  under the author's name on posts and on the profile. **Headless-API hardening (2026-07-30;
  `comments-worker/API.md` is the exhaustive wire contract for a future native iOS/Android
  client):** the platform is a real headless API — identity is a hashed key in the request body (no
  cookies/sessions), a missing `Origin` is deliberately allowed, and every response is a uniform
  `{ok, …}` JSON contract that never leaks SQL/R2. A mobile-readiness audit (its findings folded
  here) shipped the items below; the old "constants live only in the web client" gap is now CLOSED
  at the root by the PureScript kernel (client and worker read one `Domain.*` source). Shipped: (1)
  **security** — the `ChatRoom` (merecat) socket's `#emit` now sends only to `auth:true` sockets and
  `#auth` closes the socket on failure (an unauthed `?chat=N` reader gets nothing), the WS ask
  threads `CF-Connecting-IP` into the DO so IP bans bite there too, `/report` returns an identical
  404 for back-room ids, and every `Upgrade` compare is case-insensitive; (2) **served constants** —
  **`GET /api/comments/config`** (cacheable) serves cats/faiths/ranks/pages/`bot_hash`/bible/emoji +
  `apiVersion` from a new worker
  `CAT_META`/`FAITH_LABELS`/`RANKS`/`BIBLE_SPEC`/`EMOJI_PACKS`/`NAMED_EMOJI` block (the web client
  keeps its inline copies as fallback), and every author-bearing row now carries `assigned` (=
  `displayName(hash)`, via the `withNames` helper) and `rank` (via `rankFor`) where `posts` is
  known; (3) **one broadcast sink** — all board events funnel through `sendToHub` (the single
  privacy choke point via `boardEventPublic`) beneath
  `broadcastBoard`/`publishBoardEvents`/`publishLive`, so the back-room gate lives in ONE place and
  a future webhook/Discord subscriber hooks in there alone; (4) **config into vars** —
  `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`/`SITE` (comments) and `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`/`CONTACT_FROM` (contact) are now env
  vars with source fallbacks (`allowedOrigins(env)`/`siteBase(env)`, `originOk(request, env)`), plus
  a `wrangler.jsonc` `env.staging` stanza with distinct rate-limit namespace ids; (5) **Web Push
  (VAPID) — real OS push, done (branch `vapid`, 2026-08-01):** sent NATIVELY from the worker on
  `crypto.subtle` (no external service, no Node lib). `comments-worker/src/webpush.js` implements
  the VAPID JWT (ES256, RFC 8292) + the RFC 8291/8188 aes128gcm content encryption;
  `deliverPush(env, hashes, payload)` looks up each recipient's `push_tokens` subscriptions and
  sends a **title/body/url** payload (NEVER message/comment text — privacy + E2E), pruning any
  404/410 subscription and never throwing into its caller. Wired into `deliverNotifications`
  (reply/mention, per-kind copy via disjoint `pushMention`/`pushReply` sets), `handleDmSend` (dm —
  gated by the recipient's `notify_dm` pref, since a push is the loudest bell), and
  `deliverWallNotifications` (wall). `GET /api/comments/push/vapid-key` serves the public key;
  `docs/sw.js` (VERSION `mc-shell-v2`) gained `push`/`notificationclick`/`pushsubscriptionchange`;
  the Settings sheet (`app/appchrome.js` → `docs/app.js`, `app.js?v=61`) gained **"Add to Home
  Screen"** (Android/desktop `beforeinstallprompt`, iOS Safari Share-sheet instructions, generic
  fallback) + an **"Enable notifications"** toggle (`Notification.requestPermission` from the tap →
  `pushManager.subscribe` with the served `applicationServerKey` → `push/{register,unregister}`;
  iOS-not-installed and unsupported browsers get guidance, not a dead switch). Gated by
  `PUSH_ENABLED` (flipped to `"true"`) **AND** the VAPID keypair: `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` +
  `VAPID_SUBJECT` vars (in `wrangler.jsonc`, prod + staging) and the `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` **secret**
  (base64url PKCS8 — `cd comments-worker && wrangler secret put VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`; deliverPush
  no-ops safely until it is set). iOS needs the PWA installed to the Home Screen first (Apple rule;
  unavailable in the EU). Crypto proven by `tests/worker/webpush.test.mjs` (independent aes128gcm
  decrypt round-trip + ES256 JWT verify + PKCS8 import). **Remaining owner step: set the
  `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` secret, then deploy.** (**Client inline-constant copies are RETIRED — Phase 5
  single-sourcing, 2026-08-01.** The owner's ruling was to require the bundle, so
  `client/comments.ts` no longer keeps no-bundle-fallback copies of `FAITH`/`FAITH_ORDER`, the
  `RANKS` ladder, `NAMED_EMOJI`, `EMOJI_PACKS`, `CATS`, or the `ADJ`/`NOUN` pseudonym wordlists —
  `faithLabel`/`faithCodes`/`rankFor`/`rankLine`/`displayName`/emoji all read UNCONDITIONALLY from
  `window.mcCore` (the PureScript kernel), which the shell always installs before booting the
  client. The old "wordlists must stay identical in two files" hazard is closed: the kernel is the
  SOLE source, drift is impossible. New shared client/worker constants MUST go in a `Domain.*`
  module and be read via `mcCore`/imported by the worker — never re-inlined.)
- **Moderation is all in-platform** (the comments worker sends no email at all now; the old
  signed-link moderation emails are gone, and its only remaining secret is `TURNSTILE_SECRET`).
  Admins may also **edit or clean any member's profile in place** (`POST
  /api/comments/profile/admin`, admin-keyed, 2026-07-29) — the defense middle ground between doing
  nothing and lock/ban/delete, for removing something offensive at once while sparing the member;
  same field limits + reserved-merecat-name guard as the owner's own save, empty fields clear their
  columns, `clear_avatar` drops the R2 object + column, the bot's profile is exempt, and it logs
  `admin_profile_edit` to the tail; the client shows an **Edit this profile (admin)** affordance on
  any profile that is not your own (courtesy — the server refuses non-admins outright). Admin powers
  live in the per-comment user-fingerprint dropdown: **lock/unlock** an identity (reversible; a
  locked hash is logged out and refused every keyed call), **delete user and all posts**
  (soft-deletes their comments, removes profile+avatar, and locks the hash), and **ban IPs** (every
  address known for the identity, each bannable alone or all at once behind a confirm that flags a
  shared/CGNAT v4). A `blockedReason()` gate on every keyed endpoint (writes + the DM unread poll)
  returns `{blocked}`, and the client logs the user out and redirects to `terms.html` where
  `flash.js` shows a non-fading banner. Public/cached reads are never gated. Three D1 tables:
  `locks` (hash), `ip_bans` (normalized IP key), and `identity_ips` (every IP behind an identity —
  **not a ledger**: the drawer shows only the last 14 days (`IP_SHOW_DAYS`), the monthly backup cron
  deletes unbanned rows idle past 30 days (`IP_KEEP_DAYS`, via `pruneIdentityIps`, first of the
  maintenance prunes that run before `runBackup`), and banned keys are exempt from both so a
  standing ban keeps its drawer handle). **Dual-stack IP capture:** a browser reaches Cloudflare on
  only one address family, so at post time `comments.js` best-effort fetches the other from
  `ipv4/ipv6.icanhazip.com` (Cloudflare-run, CORS-open; both added to the CSP `connect-src`, which
  is a **report-only edge rule not in the repo** — a Response-Header Transform Rule in the
  Cloudflare dashboard) and sends both; the worker's `recordIps()` stores the verified connection IP
  (`seen`) and the reported opposite-family IP (`claimed`). **A family with NO road is remembered
  and skipped for a day** (2026-09-06, `mc-altip-fail:<fam>` in localStorage): a v4-only network
  cannot reach `ipv6.icanhazip.com` at all, and the browser logs that failed load ITSELF, from its
  network stack, before any promise settles — the client's `.catch` cannot silence it, so every
  v6-less reader was taking a doomed DNS+connect on each boot and each post AND leaving a console
  ERROR, which had quietly made the headless console gate useless (eleven suites plus `audit.py`
  failed it on every run from a v4-only dev box). Only a real refusal is remembered, never an abort
  (a slow link must not cost a reader their address for a day); a success clears the mark; blocked
  storage falls back to always trying. `webtest/flows.py` + `audit.py` `BENIGN_CONSOLE` also carry
  **`ipv6.icanhazip.com`** — narrowly, since the suites run on fresh browser profiles where nothing
  is remembered yet, so the first failure of a run still lands; **an `ipv4` failure stays FATAL**
  (that one would mean the CSP `connect-src` or the URL had regressed and alt-IP capture had
  silently died), so never widen it to bare `icanhazip.com`. `ipKey()` normalizes before store and
  match — v4 verbatim, v6 folded to its `/64` (a client's v6 interface id rotates daily, the
  delegated /64 does not) — so `blockedReason` and every ban match on the /64, and neither the
  fallback family nor a rotated address evades a ban. The `/ipban` endpoint takes an array
  (ban-all); the drawer reverse-DNSes each shown IP via the admin-only `/api/comments/rdns`
  (Cloudflare DoH, lazy on drawer-open). **Admin authority is data-driven and flat:** the `admins`
  D1 table (`hash` PK, `added_by`, `created_at`) is the sole source of truth — every admin is an
  equal, removable row, and `isAdminHash()` (now **async** — every call site awaits it) is table
  membership. The `ADMIN_HASHES` env var is only a **bootstrap**: `ensureAdminsSeeded()` fills the
  table from it on first console use, and its hashes count as admin only while the table is empty (a
  fresh/wiped DB), so the board can never be permanently locked out while owners are otherwise
  ordinary removable rows. The client learns its own status from its profile fetch
  (`profile.admin`); the built-in `ADMIN_HASHES` list in `comments.js` is now only a pre-load hint,
  so once the profile lands the server is authoritative and `loadMyProfile` re-renders the board
  whenever the effective answer changed (`isAdmin() !== wasAdmin`), revealing controls for a
  newly-granted admin and stripping them from a revoked one. Admin-only pages all sit behind one
  **Administrative options** link at the foot of the board (`?admin=1`, `viewAdminHome`) — a hub to
  three pages: the **Activity audit** (`?audit=1`), a **moderation console** in three actionable
  sections under a count summary of what is waiting — **Reported** (member-flagged posts,
  most-reported first, each with **Dismiss**/**Delete**/a lazy author-drawer and the full topic
  corner when it is a topic head), **Pending review** (Approve/Delete AI-held comments, the
  replacement for the old email approve link), and **Recent activity** (the last 14 days across
  pages/book/forums, every row now carrying delete + a lazy author drawer + the topic corner for a
  topic); the **IP ban list** (`?ipbans=1`, manual add/remove); and **Add / Remove Admins**
  (`?admins=1`, `viewAdmins`) where `POST /admins` lists the flat roster and `POST /admin`
  grants/revokes any admin (add is by the shared `@`-mention `attachAuthorPicker`, revoke is one
  click behind a confirm — anyone may remove anyone, themselves included, and removing yourself
  drops you back to the board as a plain member; the one guard is that the **last** admin cannot be
  removed, a rule about count not identity, so the board is never left with none). All three views
  share one `adminGate()` that shows a neutral wait, not a false "not for you", while an admin's
  status is still loading (and answers at once for a visitor with no key). **Reporting** is
  community-assisted moderation: a `reports` table (`UNIQUE(comment_id, reporter_hash)` — one flag
  per member), a member `report` link on every post (not your own, not shown to admins who act
  directly), `POST /report` (keyed, `blockedReason`-gated, POST_LIMIT'd, validates the post is
  `live`) that **never changes the post's status** — anti-brigade, a report only surfaces a post, it
  never hides it — and admin-only `POST /report/dismiss` that clears a post's flags without touching
  the post. The per-row topic controls (Move + sticky/lock/delete) are one `topicAdminCorner()`
  client helper shared by the category listing and the console, so a topic is governed identically
  in both. The legacy `bans` table is still checked but no longer written. Each topic on a category
  listing also carries admin **lock/unlock**, **delete**, and **pin/unpin (sticky)** links via the
  `/moderate` endpoint (act `sticky`/`unsticky`); a pinned topic sorts above the rest of its
  category (a `sticky` column on `comments`, `ORDER BY COALESCE(sticky,0) DESC, last DESC`) and
  wears a maroon `(sticky)` mark in the list and at its head. A **Move** dropdown in that same
  corner sends a topic — and all its replies, which each carry their own `page` — to another
  category via the admin-only `/move` endpoint (`UPDATE comments SET page WHERE id=? OR
  parent_id=?`), then DMs the original poster an automated notice, from the moving admin and
  delivered unheld, with a link to its new home. Board counts are computed live from `page`, so a
  move touches nothing else. **The back room (`adminsonly` in `BOARD_CATS`, `ADMIN_CAT =
  'board:adminsonly'`, 2026-07-29)** is a category only admins can see, read, or write — an
  off-shade tile at the foot of the index (inline-styled, **shown/hidden via inline `style.display`,
  NOT the `hidden` attribute — `.board-cat`'s `display:flex` outranks `[hidden]` and once left the
  tile visible to the whole world**; the server the real lock). **Refusals are indistinguishable
  from nonexistence**: the public cat listing answers 'Unknown category.', the topic view 'No such
  topic.', search treats the cat like a bogus name — a prober learns nothing (proven live against
  genuine-bogus probes, byte-identical); a keyed reader's client knocks once on the keyed door for
  ANY failed topic load and the server judges; keyless visitors navigating ?cat=adminsonly bounce
  straight to the index, and a keyed refusal erases the chrome and bounces the same way. EVERY
  public read excludes it outright: the board index window query, `handleBoardCat`/`handleTopicView`
  (uniform cacheable `{admin_only:true}` refusals), search (both the cat filter and the base WHERE),
  `handleAuthorPosts` + `postCountsFor` (a member's public post count and history never betray
  back-room activity), and both feed shapes. Admins read it through keyed `POST /board/admin` {key,
  cat|id, p, find} — the same payloads via the extracted `boardCatPayload`/`topicViewPayload`, never
  cached; the client's viewCat/viewTopic fall through to that door on `admin_only` (topic links,
  pagination, scroll-to-comment all work). Writes demand an admin identity in `handlePost` (topics
  AND replies); `deliverNotifications` filters back-room recipients against the `admins` table so a
  mentioned or watching outsider learns nothing; reports on its posts are refused; a topic **moved
  INTO it sends no courtesy DM** (a retraction from public view, not a move to follow — `/move`
  skips the notice when the target is ADMIN_CAT) while moving out notifies normally;
  `/api/merecat/forward` into a back-room topic requires the forwarder be admin (proven live: the
  bot's forwarded answer posts into the room). Unread counts hide it from non-admin keys and count
  it for admins.
- **Direct messages** ride the same worker: strictly 1v1 threads in D1
  (`dm_threads`/`dms`/`dm_blocks`, canonical-pair unique row, denormalized unread state),
  POST-with-key only (private; no admin visibility by design), Turnstile-gated sends, and
  shadow-blocks: a blocked sender's messages read as delivered to them but are stored `held`,
  invisible to the recipient, and an unblock releases them all with their original timestamps. A
  member may **delete a conversation** (`/dm/delete`, from an inbox row or the open thread):
  per-side `a_cleared_at`/`b_cleared_at` stamps on `dm_threads` (NULL = never cleared, so
  preexisting threads are untouched) hide everything up to the delete from that side alone — a
  **fresh start**, so a later message from the other reappears showing only what came after — and
  once both sides have cleared with no word past the earlier stamp, the thread and every `dms` row
  are purged outright (no FK cascade, so both are deleted explicitly). Every read path
  (`handleDmThreads`/`handleDmThread`/`handleDmUnread`) filters on the viewer's clear stamp. An
  automated move-notice is sent via `sendSystemDm` (the un-gated, always-unheld core of a DM send).
  Client: Inbox at `community.html?inbox=1`, threads at `?dm=<hash>`, unread badge by the Logout
  links throttled by a 90-second localStorage cache, and a member autocomplete fed by one cacheable
  `/dm/directory` fetch with all fuzzy matching done client-side. **DMs are end-to-end encrypted
  (2026-07-31).** Each identity derives an **X25519 keypair deterministically from the secret behind
  its identity hash** (`seed = nacl.hash(utf8("mc/dm/x25519/v1|" + key))[:32]` →
  `nacl.box.keyPair.fromSecretKey`, so nothing new is stored and carrying the key to another browser
  reproduces the same keypair) and publishes only the **public** half — the server holds
  `SHA-256(key)` and cannot derive the private key. A message is sealed with the pair's
  **static-static shared secret** (`nacl.box`, X25519 + XSalsa20-Poly1305): the secret is identical
  from either side, so ONE ciphertext is opened by the recipient AND re-read later by the sender,
  and the worker/D1/**backups** only ever hold the opaque `E1.<b64u nonce>.<b64u ct>` blob. No
  forward secrecy (incompatible with re-reading old messages from one long-term key — inherent, not
  a defect). **Schema (additive):** `dms.enc` marks how a body is stored — `1` = encrypted member
  message, `2` = system/automated plaintext (move-notices, still server-authored, rendered with an
  "Automated notice" label), `NULL`/`0` = legacy pre-E2E plaintext; new `dm_pubkeys(hash PK, pubkey,
  created_at, updated_at)` table. `POST /dm/pubkey {key, pubkey}` (POST_LIMIT'd, idempotent upsert,
  43-char base64url validated); `handleDmSend` accepts `enc` and swaps `MAX_BODY` for `DM_ENC_MAX`
  (24000) on ciphertext; `handleDmThread` returns each message's `enc` **and** the correspondent's
  `pubkey` in the `other` object (both the populated and empty-thread branches — the only place the
  client needs a counterpart key, since the shared secret is per-pair; the cacheable `/dm/directory`
  is untouched); the live `t:'dm'` push carries `enc` + the ciphertext. **Client (`comments.js`):**
  a small crypto layer
  (`ensureNacl`/`myDmKeypair`/`dmEncrypt`/`dmDecrypt`/`dmSafetyNumber`/`ensureMyPubkey`) over
  **vendored `docs/tweetnacl.min.js`** (tweetnacl 1.0.3, byte-exact `sha256 973cc573…`,
  lazy-injected as a same-origin classic script so `script-src 'self'` is satisfied — NOT in
  `vendor/` because it is not bundled; not eslint-linted; `nacl` is whitelisted in
  `eslint.config.js`). `ensureMyPubkey` fires from `enableMemberLive` (every identity-ready path);
  `viewDm` wraps its render in `Promise.all([ensureNacl(), fetch])` so encrypt/decrypt are
  synchronous, encrypts in the send handler (keeping the plaintext for the optimistic echo) and
  decrypts **upstream of `dmMsgNode`** on both the history loop and the live-push path (`dmMsgNode`
  stays byte-identical — the mobile bot's territory); a **keyless recipient** (no published pubkey)
  disables the composer with a "waiting for them to sign in once" notice, never a plaintext
  fallback. **Assurance UI:** a quiet **"🔒 End-to-end encrypted · how it works · verify"** badge via
  a shared `dmE2eBadge()`, prepended in `viewInbox` **before** the Lit `<mc-inbox>` mounts (the
  component renders light-DOM into its own subtree without clearing `section`, so the badge survives
  above the list — **no bundle/`app.js`/`nav.js`/`profile.js` change**, deliberately staying clear
  of the concurrent mobile work). "how it works" opens an honest, non-apologetic explainer
  (encrypted in your browser, we store only ciphertext, open X25519+NaCl, safety number for
  out-of-band verification, and a lost key means unrecoverable history); "verify" reveals the
  per-conversation **safety number** (`dmSafetyNumber`, a short hash of the two ordered pubkeys,
  identical on both sides) with a one-tap "Mark verified" remembered in `mc-dm-verified`. **No PIN**
  (frictionless day-to-day was the owner's choice; a 4-digit PIN is only safe as a local device
  lock, never as server-side recovery). **Honest scope:** genuine E2E — the operator holds only
  ciphertext and cannot read the inbox; the one residual property of any web app (it serves its own
  JS each load) is met by open-source + the safety number, NOT a badge caveat. **DMs are not
  AI-screened** (none ever were — nothing regressed). **Verified on prod:** crypto round-trip (deno,
  13/13), the full worker wire path (publish/serve pubkeys, store+return ciphertext with `enc`,
  plaintext never on the wire, recipient decrypts the wire bytes, 14/14), and a headless browser run
  (14/14: inbox+thread badges, nacl loads, **the browser-derived pubkey byte-for-byte matches the
  deterministic derivation** — which proves the in-browser encrypt path without needing to defeat
  Turnstile — keyless guard, verify link). A full headless SEND is the Turnstile wall, so the send
  path is proven by that pubkey-match plus the identical `dmEncrypt` used everywhere. Migration
  (`ALTER TABLE dms ADD COLUMN enc INTEGER` + `CREATE TABLE dm_pubkeys`) applied to prod D1 and
  mirrored in `schema.sql`. No new cross-file duplicated constant (the domain string is
  client-only). Any `comments.js` change still bumps `comments.js?v=NN`. **Disappearing messages +
  E2E media + platform settings (2026-07-31).** DMs are now transient by default (a privacy strength
  and a free-tier-storage win). **Expiry:** a message vanishes N days AFTER the recipient OPENS it.
  `dms.opened_at` (NULL until the non-sender first reads it) and `dms.expires_at` (a single SHARED
  instant, so both lose it at once) drive it: `handleDmSend` stamps `expires_at = created +
  backstop`; `handleDmThread`'s open path (beside the read-stamp) rebases opened, unsaved messages
  to `opened_at + ttl`; a `dmLive(now)` clause (interpolated server integer, never a bind) on every
  read (`handleDmThread`/`handleDmThreads`/`dmUnreadExists`) hides expired messages INSTANTLY.
  Lifetime is per-conversation `dm_threads.ttl` (24h/7d/30d, default 7d): `POST /dm/ttl` (either
  party, last write wins for both) updates it, rebases opened messages, and broadcasts a live
  `t:'dm-ttl'` event; unopened messages ride a **30-day backstop** (`dm_backstop_days`). Either
  party may **save** a message (`dms.saved`, `POST /dm/save`) → `expires_at = NULL`, exempt for
  both. A **new hourly cron** (`"0 * * * *"`, branched in `scheduled()` on `controller.cron`) runs
  `sweepExpiredDms`: hard-delete expired unsaved rows + their R2 media (batched
  `MEDIA.delete([...])`), prune orphan/dangling media, `sweepDms` empty threads, and refresh the
  media byte total. Block / Delete-conversation unchanged. Client (`comments.js`): `dmExpiryNode`
  (the "⏳ Disappears {ttl} after opening · change" note + 24h/7d/30d chooser, prominent on a new
  conversation), `dmSaveControl` per message, and a live `dm-ttl` handler that updates the header.
  **E2E media (new R2 bucket `merecatholicity-dm-media`, binding `MEDIA`):** a file is encrypted in
  the browser with **WebCrypto AES-256-GCM** (fresh key per file); the key/iv/name/mime/caption ride
  INSIDE the nacl.box message body (server never sees them), and only ciphertext is uploaded via
  `POST /dm/media` (multipart like avatars, keyed + `POST_LIMIT` + `media_enabled` + per-upload size
  + 9 GB storage-cap gated) under a **random opaque object key** `dm/<32hex>` with NO uploader in
  the key or R2 metadata (the bucket cannot be traced to who uploaded what). `handleDmSend` accepts
  `data.media_key`, validates the unlinked `dm_media` row, stores `dms.media_key`/`media_size` and
  links `dm_media.msg_id`; the `t:'dm'` push carries `media_key`. Recipients stream the ciphertext
  from `POST /dm/media/get` (membership-checked against the live, unexpired message; a stranger or
  expired ref → indistinguishable 404) and AES-decrypt client-side;
  `dmMediaNode`/`loadDmMedia`/`dmMediaDecrypt` render `<img>/<video>/<audio>` from a `blob:` URL
  cached per key (own-echo seeds the cache from the local file). Media expires WITH its message —
  `sweepExpiredDms` and `handleDmDelete`'s both-sides purge delete the R2 object (SELECT `media_key`
  before the row delete; D1 can't cascade to R2). New `dm_media(key PK, size, created_at, msg_id)`
  table = lifecycle + byte accounting + orphan pruning (unlinked >1h, or msg gone);
  `enforceMediaCap` recomputes `SUM(size)` each sweep, caches it in `app_settings.dm_media_bytes`,
  and near the 10 GB wall LRU-prunes the oldest + nulls their pointers. **Best-practice
  E2E-at-rest:** the R2 blob is client-encrypted with a key the server never holds — stronger than
  server-side at-rest encryption. **`app_settings(k PK, v, updated_at, updated_by)`** is a NEW admin
  key/value store (cached `getAppSettings`, mirrors the librarian's `config`): `media_enabled`,
  `media_max_bytes` (25 MB default), `dm_default_ttl`, `dm_backstop_days`, `dm_media_bytes`. Admin
  page `?settings=1` (`viewPlatformSettings`, linked from `viewAdminHome`) → `POST /admin/settings`
  (get/set with clamps) and a confirmed **Purge all DM media** button (`POST /dm/media/purge`,
  cursor-listed + batch-deleted, requireAdmin). **Discord webhooks (2026-08-01):** two OPTIONAL
  `app_settings` keys — `discord_forum_webhook` and `discord_feed_webhook` (full Discord webhook
  URLs, empty = off) — are set in that same admin Settings page. When set, a fresh LIVE forum post
  (topics AND replies, **never the back room** — `boardKey(page) && page !== ADMIN_CAT`) fires
  `notifyDiscordForum`, and a fresh LIVE feed/wall post fires `notifyDiscordFeed`, both in
  `ctx.waitUntil` (deferred, fire-and-forget). `sendDiscord` (lib.ts) posts a maroon embed and
  **never throws into its caller** (5s AbortController timeout). Safety: `isDiscordWebhook`
  (pure.js, unit-tested in `tests/worker/discord.test.mjs`) is the ONE gate — only a genuine
  `https://discord(app).com/api/webhooks/<id>/<token>` URL is ever accepted/POSTed (no SSRF to
  arbitrary hosts), member text rides ONLY in an embed with `allowed_mentions:{parse:[]}` (no post
  body can @everyone/@here), and `discordSnippet` strips the STX/ETX highlight sentinels + caps
  length. `handleAdminSettings` validates the URL on save (rejects non-Discord). No new duplicated
  constant; no client `?v=` implication beyond the settings UI (COMMENTS_V bumped for the two
  webhook input fields). **Free-tier fit (verified against Cloudflare limits):** cron 5/acct (1
  monthly + 1 hourly), request body 100 MB (25 MB cap), R2 10 GB + **egress free**, the worker only
  STREAMS ciphertext (no worker crypto lib, no CPU/3 MB-size pressure), the sweep batches R2/D1
  deletes under the 50-subrequest cap. New CSS for all of this rides an injected `mc-dm-css` block
  (never a shared stylesheet, staying clear of the concurrent CSS/mobile bot). **OWNER ACTION
  (report-only CSP dashboard rule, not in repo):** add `blob:` to `img-src` and `media-src` on rule
  `1dc65168…` in ruleset `e61208bc…` so the policy stays clean — report-only ⇒ media already renders
  without it (proven), and the ciphertext fetch is same-origin so `connect-src 'self'` already
  covers it. **Fresh start:** the pre-E2E plaintext DMs were `DELETE`d (owner-confirmed, no real
  users); `dm_pubkeys`/`dm_blocks` kept. New endpoints: `/dm/ttl`, `/dm/save`, `/dm/media`,
  `/dm/media/get`, `/dm/media/purge`, `/admin/settings`. Schema migration (`dm_threads.ttl`;
  `dms.opened_at/expires_at/saved/media_key/media_size` + `dms_expires_idx`; `dm_media`;
  `app_settings`) applied to prod D1 and mirrored in `schema.sql`. `DM_TTLS` (86400/604800/2592000)
  is duplicated client+worker (keep in step). **Verified on prod:** expiry engine 17/17 wire
  (open-stamp, rebase, symmetric read-filter, inbox drop-off, save-exempt, sweep), media+admin 15/15
  wire (upload/serve/membership/AES round-trip, settings clamps, purge), and a full headless run
  12/12 (an A→B encrypted **image** decrypts nacl.box→AES-GCM, streams from R2, and renders as a
  decoded blob `<img>` with `naturalWidth≥1`, plus the note/chooser/save/attach/settings page).
  `comments.js` v=164.
- **The member media platform (2026-08-02): voice notes, board attachments, per-type/per-context
  limits, client-side image compression.** The settled posture, verified against 2026 Cloudflare
  docs before building: **compression is always the CLIENT's job** (free Workers get 10 ms CPU;
  Image Transformations' 5k free monthly ops optimize *delivery*, not storage, and can never touch
  E2E DM ciphertext — kept in the back pocket for feed thumbnails, deliberately unused); **video is
  never transcoded** (no free Stream tier; Media Transformations bills video/audio output per second
  — cap + pass through, the Discord/Signal posture); **voice notes record natively and store MP3**.
  All shared rules live in **`Domain.Media`** (defaults, clamp ranges, kinds parse/serialize, the
  strict `wall/<i|v|a>/<64hex>` key parser, the exact mime whitelists — video mp4/quicktime/webm,
  audio mpeg/mp3/mp4/x-m4a/aac/webm/ogg/wav; spec in `tests/purescript/media.test.mjs`), read by the
  worker via a small lib.ts membrane
  (`mediaKindOfKey`/`mediaKindMax`/`mediaKindsFor`/`mediaMaxAcross` — the ONE place worker-side
  Maybe is erased) and by the client via `mcCore.media*`. **Settings matrix** (`app_settings`, admin
  `?settings=1` "Media & uploads" section, clamps in `handleAdminSettings` via the kernel):
  `media_image/video/audio_max_bytes` (10/15/5 MB defaults; `media_max_bytes` stays the absolute
  per-file CEILING — effective cap = min(per-kind, ceiling), so raising a kind past 25 MB needs both
  knobs), `media_kinds_dm/wall/board` (CSV masks; board defaults `image,audio` — video is one admin
  toggle away; empty = off), `media_audio_max_seconds` (180, clamp 30–600 — CLIENT-ADVISORY, the
  server cannot decode audio; bytes are its wall), `media_image_autocompress`, and the store budgets
  `media_cap_dm_bytes` (2 GB) + `media_cap_wall_bytes` (3 GB) — sized to the MEASURED R2 reality
  (3.55 GB KJV audio is fixed; ~6.3 GB member headroom under the account-wide 10 GB free tier; the
  old code gated DM media against a fantasy 10 GB and the wall had NO byte accounting at all). `GET
  /config` serves the whole policy as `media:{…}`; **composers gate from the served config, never a
  literal** (closed a live bug: client gated at 60 MB, server refused at 25). **Upload hardening
  (the un-Turnstiled `/wall/media`+`/board/media` were a live hazard — one IP could fill the account
  in minutes and kill the backup cron's R2 writes):** live `SELECT SUM(size)` cap checks at upload
  time (a sweep-maintained counter is up to ~65 min stale — meaningless against a flood; refuse at
  90%), an **established-identity gate** (`isEstablished`: a profiles row, a comment, or a wall post
  — i.e. has passed Turnstile at least once), unlinked-orphan sweeps tightened 1 h → **15 min**
  (dm_media + wall_media both), per-kind caps + kinds mask enforced at upload AND at claim time
  (`wallClaimMedia(env, key, allowedKinds, settings)` parses the kind letter from the key — an
  upload cannot know its destination, so claim-time is the real wall), and **no silent LRU for
  public media** (content, not cache): refusal is the policy; `enforceWallMediaCap` (hourly, after
  `sweepWallOrphanMedia`) only refreshes `wall_media_bytes` and past 95% evicts oldest-first while
  stamping **`media_expired`** on the parent (comments/wall_posts/wall_comments — migration 0005) so
  the client renders an honest placeholder; DM media keeps its LRU (ephemeral by contract). **Board
  attachments (migration `0005_board_attachments.sql`)**: `comments.media_key/media_size` + the
  three `media_expired` columns; board media rides the WALL pipeline — same WALLMEDIA bucket, same
  `wall_media` table, **`ref_type = 'board'`** (NEVER `'comment'`, which means a WALL comment — the
  id spaces are unrelated and the sweep would purge or cross-wire every board attachment; locked by
  `tests/worker/media.test.mjs`, which builds the real schema from the migration ledger and proves
  sweep semantics). `handleWallMediaUpload` generalized to `mediaUpload(request, env, ctx)` with
  routes `/wall/media` + `/board/media`; `handlePost` claims+links (board topics/replies ONLY —
  article pages refuse, **the back room refuses outright**, which is what keeps the public media GET
  keyless; empty body allowed under a valid claim, screened as `'(media post)'`; link = `UPDATE …
  WHERE ref_id IS NULL` closes the double-claim race); `media_key` rides the POST response, the
  new-reply broadcast, `handleApprove`'s broadcast, topic payloads (`topicViewPayload` + both topic
  SELECTs), and the pending/reported/recent admin queries; Discord embeds say `(shared an
  attachment)`. **Lifecycle = purge at soft-delete** (retraction semantics — the bytes never outlive
  public visibility): `handleSelfDelete` (RETURNING media_key), `handleModerate` delete (head +
  replies), `handleDeleteUser`, and **a move INTO the back room purges the thread's media
  outright**; the hourly `sweepWallOrphanMedia` board OR-branch (spares live AND pending — the admin
  queue keeps its evidence) is the backstop, and `pruneComments` needs no change. **Serving**: the
  public GET gained `caches.default` (honest rationale: saves R2 reads/CPU/latency, NOT worker
  invocations — a route's worker always runs in front of the cache; the request budget's protector
  is the browser cache at max-age=86400) + `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, and a
  defense-in-depth back-room gate (a board-linked key whose comment sits in `ADMIN_CAT` answers the
  byte-identical 404; only gate-passing 2xx are ever cache.put). **Client (`comments.js`)**:
  `mediaCfg()` (promise-cached `/config` read, kernel-defaults fallback), `compressImage()` (~40
  hand lines, NO library: `createImageBitmap` — EXIF auto-applied everywhere since 2021 — → ≤2048 px
  long edge → `toBlob('image/jpeg', 0.8)`, one retry q0.65; **JPEG, not WebP — Safari still cannot
  encode WebP from canvas**; a 4–6 MB iPhone photo lands ~250–600 KB; iOS hands the picker's HEIC as
  JPEG automatically when `accept="image/*"`, and raw HEIC on non-Apple browsers fails the decode →
  polite refusal), applied on wall + board + DM (pre-encrypt) attach paths (avatars keep
  `pushAvatar`); the **voice recorder** (🎙 beside every 📎 in the DM/wall/board composers;
  feature-detected with an `<input accept="audio/*" capture>` fallback for iOS-PWA quirks):
  `getUserMedia` on tap → MediaRecorder (`isTypeSupported` fall-through, `audio/mp4` AAC on
  Safari/Chrome 126+, opus on Firefox) → `start(1000)` with elapsed/remaining vs
  `media_audio_max_seconds` + a byte counter → hard stop → **preview `<audio>` + Re-record + Use**
  (the owner's better-takes/preserve-resources flow) → **lamejs MP3 encode** (mono 64 kbps ≈ 0.5
  MB/min; sizes are predictable from the seconds cap) → the File rides the composer's normal attach
  path; encode failure falls back to the raw recorder blob, labeled, with a download link where
  `canPlayType` refuses. **`docs/lamejs.min.js`** is vendored like tweetnacl (@breezystack/lamejs
  1.2.7 IIFE, global `lamejs`, `new lamejs.Mp3Encoder(1, rate, 64)`; LGPL-3.0 — compliant precisely
  because it stays a separate unmodified same-origin lazy script, NEVER bundled into app.js; header
  carries the license + source URL; **sha256
  `29d51691af0a2e3a70cdf41b96e9ea55e4f84750e672d6af187e752afaa1ed9e`**; `lamejs` whitelisted in
  eslint.config.js; MP3 frame output proven in Node and headless). Board comments render media via
  the existing `wallMediaNode(key, null)` in BOTH paths (classic `commentNode` + Lit
  `app/views/post.ts` through a new `kit.wallMediaNode` export — helper stays singular per the mcKit
  law). **v1 non-goals (deliberate, one knob away)**: article-page/book attachments, journal media,
  📎 topic-list markers, attach-on-edit (media survives a body edit; a media-only post is not
  editable), RSS/search indicators. **Laws**: new media rules go in `Domain.Media` (never
  re-inlined), WALLMEDIA/MEDIA stay deliberately OUTSIDE `runBackup()` (public/ephemeral media —
  retention is the prunes/caps, not the backup), and the `?v=` law applies as always. **Per-section
  media administration (2026-08-02, second wave — the fine-grained split).** Everything above now
  resolves PER SECTION (`dm` = Inbox, `wall` = Feed+walls, `board` = forum): the key grammar lives
  in Domain.Media's `section*Key` builders (`sectionScanKey('dm')` is `Nothing` BY CONSTRUCTION —
  E2E ciphertext is unscannable, the law in the type), read through the lib.ts membrane
  (`mediaKindMax(s,kind,ctx?)` with a per-section→legacy-global→kernel fallback chain,
  `mediaScanEnabled`/`mediaVoiceEnabled`/`mediaAudioSeconds`/`mediaRetentionDays`). New app_settings
  keys: `media_scan_wall/board` ('1' default — the LLaVA image screen in `mediaUpload` is now a
  per-section toggle; avatars stay unconditional), `media_voice_dm/wall/board` (client-advisory 🎙
  flags), the 9 override keys `media_<ctx>_<kind>_max_bytes` + 3 `media_audio_max_seconds_<ctx>`
  (deliberately NOT seeded in APP_SETTING_DEFAULTS — absence = inherit; an EMPTY value in
  `/admin/settings` DELETES the override row), `media_cap_board_bytes` (1 GB default — the forum's
  own budget, split out of `media_cap_wall_bytes` which is now feed-only; counter
  `board_media_bytes`), and media-only age retention `media_wall/board_retention_days` (0 = forever)
  + `media_dm_retention_days` (1–90, replacing the hardcoded `Dm.mediaMaxSeconds` cap in
  `sweepExpiredDms`). **Migration `0006_media_sections.sql`**: `wall_media.ctx` ('wall'|'board',
  backfilled from ref_type, stamped at upload from the route, RE-STAMPED at claim so ctx ⇔ ref_type
  for linked rows — accounting follows reality; every reader COALESCEs to 'wall'); the per-ctx live
  SUM gates uploads, `enforceWallMediaCap` loops both sections (95%→90% valve each, shared
  `stampWallMediaExpired` helper), and the NEW hourly `sweepMediaRetention` (between orphan sweep
  and cap valve in BOTH cron chains) purges aged public media — deliberately NOT sparing `pending`
  (media-only; the held TEXT keeps the queue's evidence, the parent gets `media_expired`). The wall
  post/comment link UPDATEs gained the `ref_id IS NULL` double-claim guard the board path had (a
  real pre-existing race, fixed). **New endpoints**: `POST /wall/media/purge` + `/board/media/purge`
  (per-section purge-all, sibling of `/dm/media/purge` — R2+rows deleted, parents stamped, counter
  zeroed). **The wall-pending hole is CLOSED**: `handlePending` now also returns `pending_wall`
  (held wall_posts/wall_comments, a SEPARATE array — merging would cross id spaces) and
  `handleApprove` takes `kind:'wall-post'|'wall-comment'` (count bump + broadcast only when
  non-shadowbanned, mirroring the posting path); before this a text-screen-held feed post was stored
  pending but visible NOWHERE. `/config` serves `media.sections.{dm,wall,board}` (dm has NO `scan`
  field — the absence is the statement) beside the legacy flat fields (kept one deploy for cached
  clients). **Client**: `mediaCfgNormalize` (one shape whatever the server age), `mediaGateFile`
  takes the section object, all three composers read their own section (accept derived per-section —
  the wall/DM hardcodes are gone; 📎 hidden when a section is off; 🎙 behind `voice` AND
  audio-in-kinds), the pending queue renders held media inline (`wallMediaNode`) and the
  `pending_wall` rows with approve/delete, and `?settings=1` is per-section panels: Media platform
  (global master/ceiling/autocompress/honest-AI notes) → Feed & member walls → Community forum →
  Inbox (its scan checkbox rendered disabled+unchecked with the honest E2E note) → Journal, each
  public panel carrying its own purge dangerBox. **Board video shipped ON**
  (`Media.defaults.kindsBoard = image,video,audio`). **Voice recorder robustness**: the live
  "Microphone access was refused" bug was the EDGE — the dashboard Response-Header Transform rule
  (ruleset `e61208bc…`, rule `1dc65168…`) served `Permissions-Policy: microphone=()`, denying the
  mic before any prompt; FIXED live 2026-08-02 to `microphone=(self)` via the API. The client now
  preflights `navigator.permissions.query({name:'microphone'})` (try/catch — Safari-safe), branches
  honest copy per `e.name` (blocked/no-mic/busy/security), and EVERY failure offers an idempotent
  `.mc-voice-fallback` row ("Record with your device instead") riding the `<input accept=audio/*
  capture>` OS-layer path — a voice note always has a road. `voiceMime()` verified
  same-browser-decode-safe (invariant comment added). Webtests: `webtest/flows.py` grew a `mic` flag
  ('fake'/'deny'), `webtest/test_voice.py` covers the denied path + recorder→lamejs primitives + the
  full composer flow, `webtest/test_admin_reads.py` asserts the new settings panels. **The media
  stash (2026-08-03, born of a live loss: a recorded voice note rode a new-topic composer, the page
  died at Post — an iOS engine reload no script can prevent — and the recording, already uploaded
  but its `media_key` held only in JS memory, was orphaned and swept).** An attached/recorded file
  now survives any reload, the attachDraft covenant extended to media: an IndexedDB store
  (`mc-media-stash`, `mediaStash` in comments.ts) keeps one record per composer place ({blob, name,
  type, size, key, at}; places reuse the draft key grammar
  `topic:<cat>`/`reply:<id>`/`wall:<path>`/`wallc:<post>`). The board composer stashes on upload
  success (key + bytes; on rebuild it re-adopts the key while the server's 15-min unlinked-orphan
  window can still hold the row, else re-uploads the kept blob through the normal gate); the
  wall/feed composer stashes the gated file at attach (it uploads at post time). Cleared by ✕, by a
  successful post, and by 24 h age; no IndexedDB = the old behavior. The DM composer is deliberately
  out (its media rides the E2E envelope). nav.js's heal-reload `typing()` guard also counts a filled
  topic title or a visible attach chip as mid-use (a media post can carry an empty body). Proven
  headless 5/5 (attach → reload → chip restored → ✕ → stays cleared; session scratchpad
  `test_media_stash.py`).
- **Perceived speed: the tap arrives before the network does (2026-09-06/07).** Measured first on
  prod at 150ms RTT: the document was never the problem (5–15ms, SW-cached); `softNav`'s entire
  synchronous output was a 2px bar and a 300ms timer, and the tab highlight, title, art and `<main>`
  all waited for `boots()`, which runs last. Tap, nothing, jump. Four layers now: (1) **INSTANT
  NAV** — the seven platform documents are the same thin shell differing only in the `<h1>`, so
  `app/shell.ts` builds the destination LOCALLY inside the click event (`PLATFORM_PAGES` +
  `instantMain`), `chrome.pending(path)` lights the destination tab and cross-fades `body[data-art]`
  (`activeTab(path?)`/`themeArt(path?)` took a path parameter), and the already-loaded client boots
  into it so its data fetches start in that same tick. Measured 6–12ms, prod. The document is still
  fetched but ONLY to reconcile `<title>`; **`<main>` is never re-swapped once a client has booted
  into it.** Three load-bearing details: the URL moves WITH the paint (a locally painted page pushes
  its own history entry; a document-driven page still pushes at its swap, so a hop that never
  rendered leaves no entry); the instant path is **gated on `loadedScripts['comments.js']`**, so the
  first hop off Home (which loads `index.js`) takes the document road as it must; and mid-navigation
  the rail's current item stops being the non-navigating `<span>` and becomes a link again (two lit
  rails reads as broken, and `pending` is only ever painted — promoting it would destroy the element
  under the finger). Doc-driven routes arm a skeleton at **120ms** so a fast hop never flashes one.
  Guarded by `tests/js/shell_template.test.mjs`, which parses `PLATFORM_PAGES` out of the shell and
  asserts the served pages still match. (2) **SKELETONS** — there were none anywhere; ~20 views
  hand-wrote "Loading…" in three spellings, and `viewTopic`/`viewDm` rendered NOTHING until their
  round trip. `skelTpl()` (`app/views/util.ts`) + `skeleton()`/`skelInto()` (comments.ts). Admin
  GATE waits keep plain text on purpose — a permission check is not content arriving. Cold start is
  covered with **no JavaScript**: `section.comments.board:empty` matches the served-but-empty board
  section and stops matching the instant the client renders, with a 150ms animation delay so a
  one-frame empty never flashes. (3) **PERSISTED STALE-WHILE-REVALIDATE** — `app/store.ts` gained a
  disk tier and the missing primitive, a SYNCHRONOUS **`peek()`**; views seed their first render
  from it (`kit.peekJson`). The policy is **`Domain.Cache`**, not the store, because two of its
  rules are not caching questions: `persistable` refuses every `/dm/` read BY PREFIX (end-to-end
  plaintext must never reach localStorage, and a DM endpoint added later is excluded the day it is
  written), and `classify` has three rungs — fresh / stale-but-worth-showing / expired past 24h,
  where a placeholder is truer than rows presented as current. Keyed by identity, `hydrate(hash)`
  from `enableMemberLive`, `forget()` on logout. (4) **PULL TO REFRESH** (`app/ptr.ts`, installed by
  the shell on every page, touch only): an ordinary pull refetches in place keeping scroll; **three
  pulls in ~6s escalate to a document reload**, announced before it fires (the owner's rule).
  Arithmetic in **`Domain.Ptr`** — the first resistance curve needed ~250px of finger to arm, nearly
  half a screen: correct arithmetic, wrong in the hand; ~130px now.
  `html,body{overscroll-behavior-y:contain}` or Chrome Android fires its own pull alongside ours.
  **Also removed: two API calls from EVERY navigation** — `mcBoot()` is the whole client body, so
  `_pubkeyFor`/prefs markers were rebuilt each hop and `/dm/pubkey` (an idempotent WRITE) + `/prefs`
  fired ~190ms each every time; the markers now live outside the boot (page-scoped, so a lost pubkey
  row still heals on the next load). **Settings → Troubleshooting → Clear app cache** (all users):
  empties Cache Storage + the store + derived scraps and reloads, from an **explicit allowlist** —
  the identity key is unrecoverable, so anything unlisted survives, including drafts, reading
  positions and preferences (`tests/js/clear_cache.test.mjs` locks that). **The launch splash**
  (`scripts/inject_social.py`): the cross rising into a maroon glow with the wordmark settling,
  drawn from the ROOT element's `::before`/`::after` so it needs no DOM and no `<main>` swap can
  disturb it; **Home only** (start_url, so every launch of the installed app; anywhere else is a
  resume). It fades once the app has rendered, never before 500ms, unconditionally by 2.2s, clears
  on any throw, and both pseudo-elements are `pointer-events:none` so a stuck splash could never
  trap a reader — proven by blocking app.js+comments.js outright and watching it still clear at
  ~2.65s (`tests/py/test_boot_splash.py`, which also `node --check`s the generated script since a
  stray quote would ship a syntax error into all 272 pages). Regression gates: `webtest/test_ptr.py`
  (8/8), `tests/purescript/{cache,ptr}.test.mjs`, `tests/js/store.test.mjs`. **The fifth layer, and
  the one that answered the live "white flash and a hard total page reload" report (2026-09-07): the
  app was reloading ITSELF.** The shell has had soft navigation for a year but no programmatic door
  — only a click handler — so **21 in-app hops in `client/comments.ts` plus 3 in the Lit views moved
  the app with a bare `location.href =`**, which is a full document load: white flash, lost scroll,
  dropped live socket, every boot re-run. It fired after posting a topic or reply, from the member
  picker, from the **profile's Send a Direct Message button**, on submitting a search, on opening a
  feed card, after deleting a conversation. The door now exists — **`window.mcNav(href, replace?)`**
  in shell.ts (falls through to a real load for cross-origin / non-`.html` / the away interstitial),
  reached as **`go()`** in the classic client and **`goto()`** in `app/views/util.ts`. Compounding
  it, two LIVE links still named **retired urls** — the Lit post renderer's DM link
  (`community.html?dm=`) and the bell's wall rows (`community.html?post=`): those resolve, because
  `route()`'s legacy switch redirects them, but the redirect was itself a full load, so **one tap
  cost two documents**; that switch is soft-and-replacing now, and the links name their real homes.
  Three hops stay full loads ON PURPOSE, each saying so in place: the blocked-identity redirect to
  terms.html and Settings' logout (the identity the page was built around is gone — only a fresh
  document is sure to leave nothing keyed to it standing) and the onboarding fallback taken when the
  client is not loaded at all. **`tests/js/softnav.test.mjs`** holds both classes plus a
  `HARD_ON_PURPOSE` allowlist a new full load must argue its way onto. **Second-order finding:**
  instant navigation makes it real for a Lit update to still be queued when the shell swaps
  `<main>`, so `updated()`/`firstUpdated()` can run on a DETACHED element — `this.parentElement` is
  null and the topic/board composer mounts threw on it the moment the DM links stopped being full
  page loads (a full load had hidden it: the document died before Lit got there). Both hooks return
  when detached, and the `as HTMLElement`/`!` casts that had been asserting a parent are gone. Any
  view that mounts machinery into its parent needs that check — locked in the same test file.
  **Third-order, and the one that was actually killing the installed app (2026-09-08):** a reader's
  crumb ring showed `load(navigate)` to a DM thread with **no pagehide, no beforeunload and `errors:
  none`** — by the ring's own contract (both = the page went somewhere; pagehide alone = something
  took it) **neither means the document was destroyed without the web platform being told, i.e. an
  iOS jetsam**, which is why chasing navigations never ended it. Cause: **decrypted DM attachments
  become blob URLs, a blob URL pins its bytes until revoked, and `_mediaCache` was declared INSIDE
  `mcBoot()` — which is the whole client (lines 45–11197) and re-runs on EVERY soft navigation**, so
  each hop replaced it with a fresh `{}` and orphaned every URL without revoking one; nothing in the
  DM path had ever called `revokeObjectURL`. `dmMediaNode` also fetched and AES-decrypted **every**
  attachment on the page the moment the thread drew, giving each a live `<audio>`/`<video>` — on iOS
  a media decoder apiece. **Making navigation soft made this worse**: the document now survives the
  hops that used to reset it. Fixed by a **page-scoped LRU above mcBoot** (`mcDmBlobs`, count AND
  byte budget, eviction revokes, the newest entry never evicted so one huge attachment stays
  viewable, and an element whose blob was reclaimed re-requests once), **`whenNear()`** so an
  attachment decrypts on approach, and **`preload='none'`** on both media tags. The same class one
  level up: **yesterday's draft loss-guard bound a capture-phase `pointerdown` to the document with
  no `bootSig`**, so every navigation left another live listener holding its own `draftLive` —
  proven on prod at **1 guard firing after six hops** (six before). `tests/js/dmblobs.test.mjs`
  locks all of it, including **"no `createObjectURL` in the DM path may bypass the store"** (one
  that does is a leak by construction) and a swept rule that every document/window listener `mcBoot`
  installs carries the boot signal, with an anti-vacuum count. The crumb ring now also records what
  a DM page weighs (`dm thread: N msgs, N attachments, N blobs held`), since a killed web view
  leaves nothing else to read. **And that crumb immediately disproved the memory theory** — the next
  report read `dm thread: 3 msgs, 0 attachments, 0 blobs held` one second before a full
  `load(navigate)` of the URL already on screen. **The actual cause was WHERE the Turnstile
  challenge was being run, not when.** `viewDm` called `loadTurnstile()` **unconditionally** as its
  composer mounted — and in render mode the mount IS the challenge — into a host styled
  `position:fixed;left:-9999px;opacity:0;pointer-events:none`. That is the one thing a challenge
  container must never be: the widget hides ITSELF via `appearance:'interaction-only'`, and hiding
  the host as well puts a cross-origin challenge iframe where an installed iOS web view will never
  composite it — the document was taken away ~1s after every mount, with no pagehide and no error.
  **The three failed fixes before it (drop `execute()`, warm on focus, one widget per document) all
  moved WHEN the challenge ran and the symptom moved with it — submit, then typing, then opening a
  view — which was the evidence, misread three times as a new bug.** Now: the host is a real
  on-screen, opaque, interactable element with nothing in it until the widget says otherwise;
  opening a view mounts nothing (the focus net and an unwarmed press are the only two callers); and
  **the mount itself is crumbed** (`turnstile: mounting the widget`) so a death is either adjacent
  to a mount or is not Turnstile's. Locked in `tests/js/turnstile_warm.test.mjs` — the container may
  not go off-screen/transparent/untouchable/`display:none`, and `viewDm` may not mount on open. It
  recurred WITH one — `turnstile: mounting the widget` at 17:39:44, `load(navigate)` to the same URL
  at 17:39:46 — which named the challenge itself, not its timing. **The sixth fix stopped relocating
  it and contained it instead, plus two findings outside this repo.** **(a) THE ZONE was running
  FOUR overlapping Cloudflare bot systems on the same pages**: Bot Fight Mode, **JavaScript
  Detections** (which injects a hidden 1×1 iframe + `/cdn-cgi/challenge-platform/…/jsd/main.js` into
  every HTML response — visible in the served markup), Browser Integrity Check, and our own
  Turnstile widget. **A `cf_clearance` conflict between Turnstile and JS Detections IS documented,
  but it did NOT apply here and this was a WRONG CALL, corrected 2026-09-08**: that conflict is
  specifically about Turnstile **pre-clearance**, and this zone's widget is `clearance_level:
  "no_clearance"` (check it with `GET /accounts/<acct>/challenges/widgets`) — it issues no such
  cookie, so there was nothing to overwrite. The evidence settled it: both were switched OFF for
  hours and **the kill still happened twice more**, on two different pages. `fight_mode` and
  `enable_js` are **back ON**. The lasting lesson is the API trap, not the theory: **`PUT
  /zones/<zone>/bot_management` is a FULL REPLACE, not a patch** — a partial body silently reset
  `ai_bots_protection`/`ai_training`/`crawler_protection`, so always GET first and send the whole
  object. **(b) THE WIDGET was re-challenging on a timer forever** — `refresh-expired` defaults to
  `'auto'`, and the ring proved it (two `token ready` 292 s apart with nobody touching the screen);
  both widgets now set `refresh-expired:'never'` + `retry:'never'`, and `getToken()` owns freshness
  at the moment of the press. **(c) THE CHALLENGE NOW RUNS IN ITS OWN BROWSING CONTEXT**: new hand
  page **`docs/turnstile.html`** (standalone — no nav.js, no shell, deliberately NOT in `nav.py`'s
  PAGES, referenced as `turnstile.html?v=N` and hand-bumped since it carries no stamp) holds the
  widget and returns the token over `postMessage`, **origin AND source checked**; the parent never
  loads Cloudflare's script at all, and a frame that fails to report ready in 6 s falls back to the
  old in-page widget so no branch is worse than before. **Edge: `X-Frame-Options` was `DENY`
  site-wide and is now `SAMEORIGIN`** (it would have blocked our own iframe; CSP `frame-ancestors`
  moved `'none'`→`'self'` and `frame-src` gained `'self'` to match). Proven on prod: opening a
  thread mounts nothing; focus gives `isolating frame requested → frame ready`; parent has no
