  `mc-ts-script` and no `window.turnstile`; nothing injected by Cloudflare. Locked in
  `tests/js/turnstile_warm.test.mjs` (15 checks). **NONE OF THAT STOPPED IT, and the seventh attempt
  is the one that matters (2026-09-08).** The ring finally separated the cases: on a HARD-loaded
  page the challenge completed (`frame ready` → `token ready`, page lived 10s more); on a
  SOFT-navigated one, in the very same iframe, `frame ready` → **`load(navigate)` 1s later, no
  token, no pagehide**. Containment does not contain it. **So the gate moved instead of the widget:
  `Domain.Turnstile` — `required = not (skipEstablished && established)`** (app_settings
  **`turnstile_skip_established`**, '1' default, admin row "Verification" in `?settings=1`).
  `isEstablished` IS the record that an identity has already passed a challenge (profile row,
  comment, or wall post — none exist without one), so it is not asked again on every write; the
  CONTINUOUS guards are untouched and all still run (identity key, lock/ban/block, per-IP POST
  limits, AI screen, and the established fence on uploads). `verifyTurnstile` skips **only when no
  token was offered** (a presented token is still siteverified), resolves establishment from the
  HASH — **the rule takes two booleans, neither describing the client, so no browser can claim an
  exemption** — and **fails closed** if the settings read or the probe throws. `/config` carries
  `turnstile:{skip_established}` purely so the client knows not to bother: **a spared reader mounts
  NOTHING** (no frame, no Cloudflare script, no `window.turnstile`), which is the only thing that
  reliably stops the installed app being taken down. Specs: `tests/purescript/turnstile.test.mjs` +
  `tests/worker/turnstile.test.mjs`. Proven on prod: an unestablished identity with no token is
  still refused 403; a member focusing a DM composer mounts nothing and crumbs `turnstile: spared,
  nothing mounted`. **The meta-lesson, worth more than the fix: the symptom moved with every one of
  the six relocations, and that movement was the evidence — see the `moving-a-symptom-is-evidence`
  memory.**
- **The eighth Turnstile finding: four views still mounted the challenge on open, and the profile
  showed the frame (2026-09-09).** The owner's report the day after the sparing shipped: a fresh
  open of the app, a tap on Profile, "an out-of-place white box", sometimes the full white-flash
  reload with it; the community flashed too; the DM view did not. The DM view was the ONLY one whose
  mount-on-open had been removed — `armBoardForm()` (community + topic), `viewProfile` and the Lit
  `<mc-profile>`, `wallComposer()` and the page-comment `start()` all still called `loadTurnstile()`
  unconditionally as they opened, and `loadTurnstile()` itself knew nothing of the sparing (only
  `warmToken()` checked), so an established member mounted the challenge on arrival and met the
  known kill. The box was the frame ITSELF, visible for three stacked reasons: the Lit profile
  rendered a `.mc-ts-host` of its own inside `<main>`, which `tsHost()`'s bare
  `querySelector('.mc-ts-host')` found first, so the frame mounted inside the view; the host's CSS
  (`.mc-ts-frame{height:0}`) lived in `ensureDmStyles()`, which the profile never injects, so the
  iframe stood at the 300×150 default; and `docs/turnstile.html` declared no `color-scheme`, so
  under the dark theme the browser painted its canvas opaque white (a child document whose used
  colour scheme differs from its embedder's is rendered opaque — CSS Color Adjustment). Then the
  next `<main>` swap destroyed that frame while `mcTsFrame` stayed set, so it was never rebuilt —
  which is why the second visit looked "fixed", and why an unspared reader on that document would
  have waited 10 s for a token from nothing. **Now:** the sparing gate is in `loadTurnstile()`, the
  one road every mount takes (a spared identity mounts nothing whoever asks; an unknown one asks
  `/config` first and mounts only on a "no"); the four on-open calls are gone and
  `kit.loadTurnstile` no longer exists, so a view CANNOT mount — the `.ts-slot` marker plus the
  focus net, or `editProfile`'s explicit warm, are the only ways in; the host has its own stylesheet
  (`ensureTsStyles()`, injected by `tsHost()`, so the element cannot exist unstyled); `tsHost()`
  accepts only `body > .mc-ts-host[data-mc-app]`; `tsEnsureFrame()` rebuilds a frame that is no
  longer `isConnected` and `tsMounted()` counts only a live one; the frame drops `aria-hidden` while
  a human check is up; and `turnstile.html` declares `color-scheme: light dark`, so its canvas stays
  transparent and the widget follows the app's theme on the rare occasion a check does show. Also
  corrected in place: the frame's `?v=` was never hand-bumped — `scripts/stamp_versions.py` writes
  its content hash into nav.js's `MC_ASSETS` and `asset('turnstile.html')` reads it, so an edit
  re-keys itself on the next build (CLAUDE.md and the page's own header said otherwise).
  `tests/js/turnstile_warm.test.mjs` now SWEEPS every `loadTurnstile()` call site (only `warmToken`,
  `rawToken` and the gate's own re-entry may), forbids the kit export and any
  `loadTurnstile`/`.mc-ts-host` in a Lit view, and locks the stylesheet, the strict host, the
  rebuild and the colour scheme. The lesson under the lesson: the DM fix of 2026-09-08 was written
  as "viewDm may not mount on open" — one view, named — when the rule was "no view may"; a test that
  names the one place the bug was seen is a per-site list, and the four other places were already
  there. **The ninth (2026-09-09, the same day): the contact page was still the 2025 design.**
  `docs/contact.html` carried Cloudflare's `api.js` in its markup — the implicit render, which
  mounts the widget the moment the page opens — and on a soft arrival `contact.js` rendered it again
  on boot; the owner saw "a Turnstile white flash and reload on the contact page", which is the
  mount-on-open kill in its purest form. It has its own sitekey and its own worker
  (`contact-worker`, siteverify with its own secret), and nobody to spare: a visitor has no
  identity, so the challenge must run somewhere. The postmortem's one solid fact is that the
  challenge COMPLETES on a hard-loaded document and took soft-navigated ones down, so `contact.html`
  is now a **document page** in `app/shell.ts` (`DOCUMENT_PAGES` + `documentPage()`), consulted by
  all three doors — the click interceptor, `mcNav`, and the history walk (`popstate` reloads into
  it) — so no road arrives softly; a deliberate load is the same document the reader got after the
  kill, minus the kill. And the page mounts nothing on arrival: the script tag is gone from the
  markup, the slot is `.contact-ts` (not `cf-turnstile`, which an implicit render would scan), and
  `contact.js` loads `api.js?render=explicit` and mounts on the first focus of a real field or on
  the Send press, `refresh-expired:'never'` + `retry:'never'` like the other two widgets, with
  `reset()` earning a fresh token at the press when the old one aged out while the message was being
  written; the mount and the script load are crumbed (`turnstile(contact): …`). The widget stays
  visible by design — on a contact form the reader should see the check happen. Locked in
  `tests/js/turnstile_warm.test.mjs` (the contact rules) and `tests/js/softnav.test.mjs` (the
  document page and its three doors); `webtest/test_contact_turnstile.py` proves it on prod.
- **The social layer's global kill switch (2026-09-06).** The Feed and member walls are one feature
  behind one admin toggle: `app_settings` **`social_enabled`** ('1' default), set in `?settings=1`'s
  new FIRST panel **"Social (Feed & member walls)"**. Off, the whole social half is inaccessible
  **to everyone, admins included** — the owner's ruling, so there is no admin-bypass branch to audit
  — and **nothing is deleted**: every
  `wall_posts`/`wall_comments`/`wall_likes`/`wall_comment_likes`/`bookmarks`/`notifications` row
  survives and returns exactly as it was when the switch goes back on (the flag is a plain k/v row,
  **no migration**). **Polarity is single-sourced in `Domain.Wall`** (`enabledDefault`, `enabledFrom
  v = v /= "0"`) because the codebase carries both idioms (`calls_enabled !== '0'` beside
  `media_enabled === '1'`) and reading this one as `=== '1'` would dark the feed on any database
  lacking the row — worker membrane `socialEnabled(s)` in lib.ts, client membrane `wallEnabledFrom`
  in app/core.ts (the kernel's first client-side Wall use), spec in
  `tests/purescript/wall.test.mjs`. **Refusals are indistinguishable from nonexistence** (the back
  room's posture, the owner's choice): `/wall/feed`, `/wall`, `/wall/post`, `/wall/comment`,
  `/wall/edit`, `/wall/like`, `/wall/comment/like` and `POST /wall/media` answer 404 `'No such
  page.'` via the single `socialOff(env)`/`noSuchPage()` pair; **`/wall/post/get` answers the
  byte-identical 404 `'That post is gone.'` a deleted post gives**, so a shared
  `feed.html?post=<id>` cannot tell "switched off" from "taken down"; `/wall/likers` answers the
  empty likers list an unknown post already gives. **Deliberately STILL OPEN when off** (each one a
  real trap): **`/wall/delete`** (an author or admin must always be able to retract, and the admin
  queue discards held wall rows through it), **`GET /wall/media`** (it serves **forum** attachments
  too — `ref_type='board'`; gating it breaks board media), the admin **`/pending`+`/approve`** wall
  branches (held content must never be stranded), and every cron sweep. The wall UPLOAD gate rides
  the **ROUTE arrow**, not `mediaUpload`, because `POST /board/media` shares that handler.
  **Cross-surface residue is filtered, not deleted**: `kind IN ('wall','wall-like')` notifications
  drop out of the list and **both** unread counts (one `notifUnreadCount` helper now — there were
  two copies of that query, and fixing one is exactly how a badge sticks), saved feed posts drop out
  of `/bookmarks`, and `kind:'wall'` stops being a valid `/bookmark` kind (falling into the
  pre-existing `'Bad request.'`). **Client**: `socialCfg()` in comments.ts reads `/config`'s new
  `social:{enabled}` through `cachedJson` — which **shares mcStore's per-URL cache with
  `mediaCfg()`/`callsCfg()`, so it costs no extra request** — and mirrors the answer into
  **`localStorage['mc-social']`** ('0' = off, absent = on) dispatching `mc-social-change`;
  `app/appchrome.ts`'s `visibleTabs()` reads that mirror SYNCHRONOUSLY to drop the Feed tab from
  both the mobile tab bar and the desktop rail (a `/config` fetch from the shell would put API
  traffic on all ~270 corpus pages and break the free-tier budget law), and both components
  re-render on the event. `feed.html` (listing AND `?post=`) renders `viewNoSuchPage()`;
  `renderProfileWall` early-returns so a profile keeps identity/bio/links/rank/Recent Community
  Posts and simply has no wall. The notification/saved renderers are **untouched** — the server
  stops returning those rows — which is what makes re-enabling a pure switch flip. **Propagation is
  ~5 min** (per-isolate `getAppSettings` cache 300 s + `/config` edge cache 300 s), stated in the
  admin copy. Drift-guarded in `tests/worker/social.test.mjs` (allowlist, coercion, every guard by
  handler name, the indistinguishable wordings, and the three must-stay-open surfaces) — which also
  closes a standing gap: nothing had ever guarded the `/admin/settings` `allowed` map, so a mistyped
  key was a silent no-op. `webtest/test_zoomproof.py` and `webtest/test_voice.py` are now flag-aware
  (they drove the wall composer and would have failed, or passed vacuously, the moment the switch
  was flipped).
- **1v1 voice calls (2026-08-03, free-tier, E2E).** Members call each other from the DM thread
  header (📞), Signal-style: media is peer-to-peer **DTLS-SRTP** (genuinely E2E — the worker relays
  only setup metadata, and even the TURN leg carries opaque ciphertext). **Architecture: NO new
  Durable Object** — call state lives in the two clients under the pure **`Domain.Call`** kernel
  state machine (Idle|Outgoing|Incoming|Connecting|Active|Ended(reason); Ended absorbing; Timeout =
  ring timer 30s / setup watchdog 20s / **NO-OP in Active** so a stale timer can never kill a live
  call; `glareWins` = lower hash's offer wins simultaneous calls; spec swept exhaustively in
  `tests/purescript/call.test.mjs`). **Setup rides two gated POSTs** (`/api/comments/call/offer` +
  `/call/answer` — keyedGated POST_LIMIT + **isEstablished** (the media-upload drive-by fence),
  refuse self/bot, and `dm_blocks` → **fake `{ok:true}`**, the caller rings out to silence,
  indistinguishable); **transient signaling rides a `t:'call-sig'` BoardHub relay branch** cloned
  from `t:'typing'` (durable.ts — ICE batches + end/decline/busy/taken, ≤4 KB, kind-whitelisted, no
  storage; SDP never rides the relay; 'taken' to one's OWN hash hushes other tabs). **TURN**:
  `/call/turn` (READ_LIMIT + established) mints short-TTL credentials from **Cloudflare Realtime
  TURN** (key `merecatholicity-calls`, uid in wrangler.jsonc `TURN_KEY_ID`, secret `TURN_KEY_SECRET`
  via wrangler secret; created 2026-08-03, NO card-on-file was required) — free pool 1,000 GB/month
  (~1–2M relayed audio-minutes; ~15–20% of calls need relay), $0.05/GB past it with NO spend cap;
  any failure/absence serves the STUN-only fallback (`stun.cloudflare.com` free/unlimited) and calls
  still mostly connect. **Admin settings (`?settings=1` "Voice calls" panel): `calls_enabled`**
  (global kill switch — refuses every /call/* server-side + hides the 📞 via `/config`
  `calls:{enabled}`), **`calls_turn`** (off = STUN-only = zero billing exposure), **and the silence
  watch (2026-08-03): `calls_idle_hangup` + `calls_idle_seconds`** — an Active call where NEITHER
  side clears the voice floor for the window ends itself. Client-run in app/call.ts's
  `startIdleWatch` off `/config` `calls:{idle_hangup, idle_seconds}`: `pc.getStats()` already
  carries per-second `audioLevel` (0..1) for the local mic (`media-source`) and the remote track
  (`inbound-rtp`) — no audio graph, nothing leaves the device; either side over
  `Domain.Call.voiceFloor` (0.01) resets the clock, a browser that never reports a numeric
  audioLevel DISARMS the watch (missing data must never hang up a call), and the end flows through
  the kernel's `IdleHangUp` event (legal ONLY in Active → `Ended "idle"`, the stale-timer
  discipline). Default on/60s; clamp 15–600 single-sourced in `Domain.Call.idleClampSecs` (settings
  save + /config + client all apply it). Proven live by `webtest/test_call.py idle`: sound holds a
  15s-window call open, muting both sides ends it with reason `idle`. Ring-while-away: coalesced
  `'call'` notification (migration **0009**, the 0008 table-swap idiom; drift-guarded in
  `tests/worker/call.test.mjs`) + Web-Push nudge only when `presenceOf` says offline (no names in
  push; the nudge lands on the missed-call record, never an answerable ring); `/call/answer`
  read-marks it TARGETED (kind+actor — never the nuke-all `/notifications/read`). **Client**
  (`client/comments.ts`): the `window.__mcCall` singleton (soft-nav epoch bumps never orphan a live
  pc; full reload = call ends, the honest story), `#mc-call-ui` fixed on `<body>` + injected
  `mc-call-css`, every transition through `mcCore.callStep`; global incoming banner on any
  member-live page; glare/busy auto-decline; ICE batched 250 ms over `mcLive.member.callSig`
  (live.ts, typing's sibling; buffer + re-flush on `mc-live-resync`); Active bar = timer/Mute/Hang
  up + the iOS honesty line ("keep this screen open — locking the phone pauses the call"; iOS
  suspends WebRTC on lock/background, worse in the installed PWA — no CallKit exists for the web).
  **Socket close NEVER feeds the kernel** — an Active call rides through the live layer's
  hidden/idle socket closes (remote hangup during a gap surfaces as pc failure within ~10s grace;
  ICE restart deliberately v1-out-of-scope). Verified on prod by **`webtest/test_call.py`**
  (fake-mic LiveUsers): a REAL P2P call end-to-end (both sides Active, `inbound-rtp` audio packets
  both directions, clean hangup), the STUN-only degraded path, block-indistinguishability at the
  wire, in-call busy auto-decline, and the multi-tab 'taken' hush. v1 non-goals: video, group calls,
  ringtones, answer-from-push, ICE restart, call-log DM lines (metadata minimization).
  **Ring-anywhere + member privacy (2026-08-03, second wave).** The call ENGINE moved out of
  comments.js into the shell bundle — **`app/call.ts`**, installed by shell.ts right after
  `installLive()` — because a receiver must ring on ANY page: comments.js boots only on the platform
  pages and its listeners die with each soft-nav boot cycle. app/call.ts binds its
  `mc-live`/`mc-live-resync`/`pagehide` listeners ONCE for the page's life, owns the
  singleton/banner/bar, and **self-enables the member's live socket wherever `mc-comment-key`
  exists** (rechecked on pageshow/focus — `member.enable` opens and holds the socket on any page,
  the "badges everywhere" contract). comments.js keeps only `callsCfg` + `callButton`, delegating to
  **`window.mcCall.place(other, label)`**; the 📞 moved from the DM thread HEADER into the **composer
  button row beside 🎙** (reachable from the bottom of a long thread; the header keeps just the
  presence dot + name). `Window.mcLive.member` gained `callSig` in `globals.d.ts` (the canonical
  declaration — never redeclare it per-module). **Member privacy switch**: the Settings gear's
  Privacy & safety gained **Voice calls** on/off (`_setPref({calls})` in appchrome's McSettings) —
  stored as **`profiles.calls_ok`** (migration **0010**; NULL/1 = takes calls) via `handlePrefs`'
  new `calls` key, and enforced in `handleCallOffer` with the SAME fake `{ok:true}` a dm_block gets
  ("not taking calls" is indistinguishable from "did not pick up"); drift-guarded in
  `tests/worker/call.test.mjs`, wire-proven in `webtest/test_call.py`'s block scenario.
  `notifyCall`'s presence check fixed to `presenceOf`'s array return (the push nudge now truly fires
  only when the callee is away). The crown-jewel webtest's receiver now sits on **library.html — a
  bundle-only page with no comments.js** — proving the shell engine rings where the classic client
  never loads.
- **In-app notifications** ride the same worker — the retention backbone now that email is gone —
  reusing the DM unread machinery. Two D1 tables: `notifications` (one row per event for one
  recipient: `kind` `reply`/`mention`, `topic_id`/`comment_id` to jump to, `actor_hash`, `read_at`
  NULL = unread, the DM stamp idiom; index on `(recipient_hash, id)`) and `watches` (`PRIMARY KEY
  (hash, topic_id)`). `deliverNotifications()` runs in `handlePost`'s `ctx.waitUntil` **only for a
  `live` board post** (a held/pending post notifies no one, though the author still auto-watches):
  the author **auto-watches** the thread (topic or reply), each client-supplied `mentions` hash
  (validated 64-hex, capped at 10) gets a `mention`, and a reply **fans out** to the topic author
  and every watcher save the replier and anyone already mentioned — one dedup'd `db.batch`;
  `handlePost`'s reply SELECT was widened to pull the topic `author_hash`. @mention resolution is
  **the same `/dm/directory` + `dmScore` fuzzy search as DM send**, sprung by an `@` token in the
  reply box (`attachMentions`) and carrying the picked member's own hash so a one-way pseudonym is
  never reversed; `boardPost` sends the `mentions` whose token still stands in the body. Keyed POST
  endpoints, all `blockedReason`-gated like the DM ones: `/notifications/unread` (badge count via
  the 90-second-cached `notifUnreadCheck`, a Notifications link beside the Inbox in
  `renderIdentity`), `/notifications` (list at `community.html?notifications=1` =
  `viewNotifications`, each row linking `?topic=T#comment-C`), `/notifications/read` (opening the
  list stamps all read), and `/watch` (`act` `status`/`watch`/`unwatch`, the manual toggle in the
  topic header). The kind codes `reply`/`mention`/**`dm`** are a server `CHECK` plus a client label
  map — keep them in step. **A direct message is also a notification (2026-07-31), not only an inbox
  badge**: `handleDmSend` (unheld) and `sendSystemDm` call `notifyDm`, which inserts a **coalesced**
  `dm` notification (one UNREAD row per recipient+sender, `topic_id`/`comment_id` 0, jumps to
  `?dm=<actor>`) and publishes a live `notification` event so the bell rings too; the client renders
  "X sent you a message". Adding `dm` to the live `kind` CHECK was a **table-swap migration**
  (SQLite can't ALTER a CHECK; `schema.sql` carries the new constraint, all rows preserved).
  `pruneNotifications` joins the cron chain (below): read rows past `NOTIFICATIONS_KEEP_DAYS` (30)
  go, plus any notification whose post is gone (the orphan sweep spares `dm` rows, which carry no
  comment) or watch whose thread is gone; unread rows are kept however old.
- **Unread threads, mute, and profile post-history** ride the same worker (a retention/UX trio).
  **New since last visit:** a `thread_reads` table (`PRIMARY KEY (hash, topic_id)`; `topic_id = 0`
  is a per-user *read-all floor* set lazily on the first `/board/unread` call, so a newcomer starts
  all-read instead of facing a wall of "new") drives four keyed, `blockedReason`-gated POSTs —
  `/board/unread` (total + per-category new counts; a topic is new when its `last_at` beats the
  reader's per-thread `read_at`, or the floor), `/board/reads` (the unread topic ids for one
  category, so `viewCat` marks them `● new`), `/board/read` (upsert `read_at=now` on opening a
  topic), and `/board/read-all` (reset the floor to now and drop the now-redundant per-thread rows —
  the **Mark all read** action on the board index). A poster has seen their own post, so
  `handlePost` also advances the author's `thread_reads.read_at` to their new comment's `created_at`
  (the thread's fresh `last_at`) for a **live** board post — without this a member's own reply reads
  back as one unread when they return to the index, since posting raises `last_at` past the
  `read_at` that opening the thread set. **BLOCK is the ONE member-facing control (the 2026-08-03
  unification — the owner's ruling: "User can block. User can unblock. that is it.")**:
  `setBlock(hash, on)` in comments.ts drives BOTH halves in one act — the DM shadow-block
  (`/dm/block`, server) and the hide-list (`mc-muted` localStorage, server-synced via `/prefs`
  `muted`; `isMuted`/`toggleMute` survive as the underlying machinery and the kernel's `Domain.Mute`
  is unchanged). A blocked member's posts are **fully hidden** (a display:none stub — no collapse,
  no "show"): comment renderer (classic + Lit post.ts), wall/feed post + comment builders, board
  topic rows (classic + Lit), the index latest-post line, and search results; their **profile is
  closed** (`renderProfile` gates with "You have blocked this member" + Unblock — both the classic
  and Lit profile paths funnel through it). Block affordances: the post ⋯ menu (block/unblock,
  confirm-gated), the profile card, the DM thread; Settings' Privacy panel shows ONE **Blocked
  members** list (the server dm_blocks roster unioned with the local hide-list; Unblock undoes both
  halves) — the separate "Muted members" panel is gone. The bot cannot be blocked (isMuted's guard).
  **Admin moderation (locks, IP bans, shadow bans, delete) is a separate, untouched world.**
  Contract locked by `webtest/test_post.py` (blocked post = hidden stub) +
  `scratchpad/test_block_local.py` (5/5). **Post-history:** `GET /board/author?hash=&p=`
  (READ_LIMIT, `cacheHeader`) returns a member's own live forum posts, newest-first and paginated,
  rendered as a **Recent posts** list on their profile — deferred behind a *Show recent posts* link
  so a profile view makes no post-history call until the reader asks (a worker-load saving).
- **Post count and rank.** Every forum post, article-page comment, and profile carries its author's
  total live-forum post count — `postCountsFor(env, hashes)`, one grouped query per page over the
  authors actually present (the same "topics always, replies only under a live topic" definition
  `handleAuthorPosts` totals), backed by the new `comments_author_idx`. The client turns the count
  into a scriptorium **rank** (`RANKS`/`rankFor` in `comments.js`: Novice 0, Apprentice 10,
  Scriptorium Hand 50, Copyist 100, Scribe 250, Illuminator 500, Master Scribe 1,000, Keeper of
  Scrolls 2,500, Treasury of Wisdom 5,000) shown as a muted "Rank · N posts" line under the author
  on each post (`authorNode`'s new `posts` arg, reusing the `comment-faith` style) and on the
  profile header. **No new table** — counts are computed, not denormalized, so nothing to keep in
  sync; the rank thresholds live only client-side.
- **Profiles and avatars** ride the same worker. Profiles (nick/bio/signature) live in the D1
  `profiles` table, AI-screened like comments. Avatars are 400x400 JPEG only, 500 KB cap,
  magic-byte-sniffed server side, screened by a Workers AI vision model (LLaVA), and stored in R2
  **`merecatholicity-avatars`** under one fixed key per identity (upload = overwrite, so no
  orphans). **Preset avatars** are a `comments.js`-only alternative to uploading — **no worker
  change**: the profile editor's *Choose from the gallery* toggle opens a tabbed, searchable panel
  (the emoji-picker chrome reused) of ready-made art carried into `avatars/presets/<pack>/` as
  static Pages-served images and grouped by a generated `avatars/presets/index.json` manifest
  (fetched lazily on first open, like `emoji/emoji-data.json`). Picking one draws that
  **same-origin** image onto the very same 400x400 canvas an upload uses — `contain`-fit onto a
  `#faf6ee` matte (the tile color) since JPEG carries no alpha — and pushes the resulting JPEG
  through the identical `/avatar` endpoint, so storage, serving, backup, and the AI screen are all
  unchanged. Regenerate the packs + manifest from source emoji folders with
  `scratchpad/import_presets.py`. The monthly backup cron dumps the whole database
  schema-agnostically — excluding the search index's FTS5 shadow tables (`comments_fts%`, derived
  data) but appending its virtual table, triggers, and a `('rebuild')` so the one file restores
  search on its own — and mirrors the avatar objects into the backups bucket; **any new state store
  outside D1 must be added to `runBackup()`'s mirror or it is not backed up**. A mid-month snapshot
  can be forced through the admin-keyed `POST /api/comments/backup`. Before each dump the cron runs
  a housekeeping chain — `pruneIdentityIps` → `pruneComments` → `sweepDms` → `pruneNotifications` →
  `pruneMerecatChats` → `runBackup` — that also hard-removes soft-deleted comments older than 30
  days (`DELETED_KEEP_DAYS`, since comments are only ever soft-deleted) and any live replies a topic
  delete left orphaned (topic delete doesn't cascade to its replies), sweeps stray DM rows (messages
  whose thread is gone, threads with no messages), and clears read notifications past their month,
  any notification/watch left pointing at a vanished post or thread, and the orphaned `thread_reads`
  and `reports` rows whose thread or post is gone (both swept inside `pruneNotifications`);
  **`pending` comments, shadow-`held` DMs, and unread notifications are deliberately left alone**,
  and each prune is self-contained so one failure never stops the backup. Cloudflare's CSAM Scanning
  Tool is enabled on the zone.
- **Forum full-text search** rides the same worker over an FTS5 **external-content** virtual table
  `comments_fts(title, body)` (`schema.sql`), kept in lockstep with `comments` by three `AFTER
  INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` triggers (`comments_ai/ad/au`); `porter unicode61` tokenizer so "conclude"
  finds "concluded". The index mirrors ALL comments — the **forum-only, live-only** restriction is
  applied at query time (`page LIKE 'board:%' AND status = 'live' AND (parent_id IS NULL OR parent
  is live)`), so no conditional triggers are needed. `GET /api/comments/search` (READ_LIMIT,
  `cacheHeader`-cached) takes `q`, `cat` (a `BOARD_CATS` key), `author` (64-hex hash), `sort` (`new`
  = recency, else bm25 relevance), `p`. `buildMatch()` turns the query into a **safe** FTS5 MATCH by
  double-quoting every token/phrase — so `"exact phrase"` is an adjacency match and no FTS5 operator
  (`* - : ^ NEAR AND OR`) can be injected — and `snippet()` wraps matched terms in STX/ETX control
  chars (a body can never contain them, per `CONTROL_RE`) which the client splits into `<mark>`
  spans (never innerHTML). Client `viewSearch()` at `community.html?q=<query>` (+
  `&cat=&author=&sort=&p=`, all URL-driven and shareable): a search box on the board index and a
  **Search** link in `renderIdentity`, a category `<select>`, an `@`-author filter reusing the DM
  directory + `dmScore` (`attachAuthorPicker`), highlighted result rows linking to
  `community.html?topic=<root>#comment-<id>`. **Only the forum is searched** — never article-page
  comments or the book. After creating the vtable on an existing DB, run `INSERT INTO
  comments_fts(comments_fts) VALUES('rebuild')` once to index prior rows; the monthly backup carries
  the vtable + triggers + rebuild (see the backup note above), and `pruneComments`'s hard deletes
  fire the delete trigger, so nothing extra is needed on the cron.
- **Emoji** are a `comments.js`-only feature — **no worker change**: bodies stay plain text and the
  images are static assets served by Pages. Standard Unicode emoji insert and store as characters;
  the two image packs (**memes** = a memes + a cat-memes pack, **pepe** = two pepe packs, all from
  emoji.gg, each source image optimized to a 128px **WebP** under `emoji/memes/` and `emoji/pepe/`,
  ~1.3 MB total) insert and store as `:shortcode:`. The renderer (`appendRich`, still
  createElement/textContent only, never innerHTML) turns a `:code:` into a same-origin `<img
  class="mc-emoji">` **only** when the code is on the `CUSTOM_EMOJI` whitelist (derived from the
  inline `EMOJI_PACKS`), a common `NAMED_EMOJI` alias into its character, and anything else stays
  literal text — so a body can never name an arbitrary image source. The full standard set (~1900,
  grouped, with search keywords) is generated from GitHub's **gemoji** into `emoji/emoji-data.json`
  and fetched **lazily** on first use, so it never rides the initial page load (the inline
  `STANDARD_EMOJI`/`NAMED_EMOJI` are the pre-load fallback). Three ways in, all attached to every
  composer through `mdEditor`: the **😊 picker** button (a tabbed, internally-scrolling panel — a
  search box, then Emoji/Memes/Pepe tabs, search focused on touch), the **`:` autocomplete** (the
  sibling of `attachMentions`: `@` picks a member, `:` picks an emoji, narrowing as you type on
  desktop and mobile), and hand-typed `:shortcode:`. Picker and `.mc-emoji` CSS is injected once as
  `<style id="mc-emoji-css">` matched to the site palette (`--rule`/`--maroon`/`--faint`), **not**
  added to `style.css`; the old markdown syntax legend under the toolbar was dropped. Regenerate the
  packs with `scratchpad/build_emoji.sh` (ImageMagick) and the data file from gemoji; the two
  duplicated wordlists caveat does not apply here (the standard data lives only client-side).
  Same-origin emoji images are unaffected by the report-only CSP.
- **Post preview and local drafts** are `comments.js`-only — **no worker change**. Every composer
  (the page/book comment box, the board new-topic form with its title, the topic reply box, the DM
  composer, and the edit-own-post box) carries a **Preview** button beside its Post/Send/Save
  (`previewButton`; the toggle state lives on the textarea, set by `mdEditor`, so the
  identity-driven button-row rebuilds keep the label right): it swaps the whole editor for the post
  rendered by the very `fillBody` that draws published comments (a topic's title rides atop),
  relabels to **Edit** for the way back, and never touches the value, so posting works from either
  side. The same composers autosave to a per-place localStorage slot (`attachDraft`; keys
  `mc-draft:page:<path>` / `topic:<cat>` / `reply:<topicId>` / `dm:<hash>` / `edit:<commentId>`),
  debounced on input and flushed on blur/hidden/pagehide, so any number of half-written posts
  survive a crashed browser or a dead phone and each restores to its own spot; a successful post
  calls `ta.mcDraftDone()` (clears the slot and holds saves until the next keystroke, so a teardown
  flush on a redirect cannot resurrect what was just sent), cancelling an edit clears its slot, an
  emptied box removes its slot, and slots untouched for 30 days are swept on load. Programmatic
  inserts (toolbar, both pickers, the quote button) dispatch `input` so drafts and autocompletes
  track them; preview/draft CSS rides the injected `mc-emoji-css` block, not `style.css`. The
  once-unscoped `.comment-text` lookups in the post paths are now scoped to `.comment-form` (an open
  edit box wears the same class and must not win).
- **merecat, the librarian bot** rides the comments worker (routes
  `merecatholicity.com/api/merecat*` + www, same worker so it shares
  `sha256hex`/`blockedReason`/`requireAdmin`/rate limits/the AI binding). **TRANSPORT — READ THIS
  FIRST (2026-07-30): merecat generation is now a WebSocket state machine in a per-conversation
  Durable Object (`ChatRoom`), NOT the old HTTP stream.** A member asks over one socket and receives
  `hello`/`state`/`meta`/`tokens` frames back; the DO owns the whole generation (local `serve.py`
  via `merecatLocalFetch` with failover to `env.AI`, retrieval via
  `merecatPrompt`/`merecatRetrieve`, `merecatFold`) and is the SINGLE D1 writer to
  `chats`/`chat_msgs` (`done=0`→`done=1`), so disconnect-survival is structural — a dropped socket
  reconnects and the DO's `hello` replays phase + answer-so-far (that frame IS the
  resume/reconcile/recover, all now gone). Flow: **`POST /api/merecat/ask-init`** {key, chat?, q}
  mints/verifies the thread + returns `{chatId, backend, used}` (client adopts
  `?merecat=1&chat=<id>` BEFORE connecting), then **`GET /api/merecat/live?chat=<id>`** upgrades to
  the `ChatRoom` DO (auth = member key in the auth frame, never the URL; `CONNECT_LIMIT`). Client:
  `app/live.js` `window.mcLive.chat(chatId,key,onFrame)` (a chat conn beside the forum `board` conn,
  one shell-owned idle/reconnect lifecycle), driven by **`askWs`** (new ask) and **`resumeWs`**
  (reopen a live thread) in `viewMerecat`. **RETIRED and DELETED** once proven live: the client's
  `ask()` HTTP stream + `resumeAsk`/reconcile/recover/stall-watchdog, and the worker's
  `handleMerecatAsk` (`/api/merecat/ask` now 404s), `merecatPump`, `merecatPumpProxy`,
  `merecatCloudInto`, `merecatAskLocal`, `claimRow`; `handleMerecatStore` (`/store`) is a
  `{ok:true}` no-op kept for one-deploy safety. There is **no HTTP fallback** — a browser with no
  WebSocket gets a plain note. **`serve.py` cleaned 2026-07-30** to match: `store_answer`,
  `flush_partial`, the `_flusher` thread, the `chat`/`msg` request params and the `urllib` import
  are gone (the DO calls serve.py WITHOUT chat/msg per "Option B", so the callback never fired); the
  streaming heart is untouched (queue + place-in-line heartbeat, retrieval, STX keepalive, token
  stream, engine-fault disclosure, ETX completion mark, reranker robustness, sd_notify watchdog).
  Verified: py_compile clean, `merecat-local` restarted, `/health` ready+rerank ok, a full WS ask
  streamed end-to-end, `test_preflight.py` 4/4. **@merecat mentions ride the `ChatRoom` DO too since
  2026-08-03** (a dedicated `mention:<comment id>` instance per summons, internal `POST /mention`
  lane, alarm keep-alive; `merecatMentionKick` in index.ts is the kicker from both `handlePost` and
  the admin `/api/merecat/mention` lever, which now returns `{queued}` instead of holding the
  connection). The generation itself is still the one-shot `merecatMentionReply`
  (`merecatLocalRead`/`merecatRetrieve`) — only its RUNTIME moved, because the old home was
  structurally broken: it ran inside handlePost's `ctx.waitUntil`, which the runtime cancels ~30s
  after the response, so every local-backend mention (minutes of generation) was silently lost — the
  box generated the whole answer and the dead worker threw it away (comment 226 postmortem, found
  via the Workers Logs warn "waitUntil() tasks did not complete within the allowed time";
  observability is enabled on the worker, and the telemetry query API can read those logs days later
  — use it before speculating). **Never put a long generation (or any minutes-long await) inside a
  stateless invocation's `waitUntil` — kick it into a DO**, whose floating promises + a 30s
  self-rearming alarm run it to completion (the ChatRoom's own #generate pattern). Forward is
  unchanged (it posts an already-generated answer, no model call). The rest of this bullet describes
  behavior (caps, retrieval legs, personas, rooms, admin-observe, chats/threads schema) that is
  INTACT; where it narrates the OLD HTTP disconnect contract (`merecatPump` write races, `/store`
  callback, client stall-watchdog/reconciler/resume polling, the `?probe` completion-mark plumbing),
  that machinery is superseded by the DO state machine above — treat those passages as history. See
  [[realtime-layer]]. It is a members-only RAG chat at `community.html?merecat=1` (`viewMerecat` in
  `comments.js`, a `🐈 merecat` link in `renderIdentity`, strict `state.key && state.myHash` gate
  like board search). **Data is decoupled**: its own D1 database `merecat-library` (binding `LIBDB`;
  schema in `comments-worker/schema-librarian.sql`: `works`, `chunks`, external-content `chunks_fts`
  FTS5 + triggers, `config`, `usage`, `user_usage`) and its own Vectorize index `merecat-t1`
  (binding `MERECAT_INDEX`, 1024-dim cosine, bge-m3; the free plan stores ~4,880 vectors total.
  **Vectorized: band 1 entire (~1,088) plus the worldview shelf's political-theological core** —
  four Faheys (mystical-body 420, kingship-jewish 244, kingship-naturalism 179, money-manip 122;
  **rulers-russ and secret-societies ceded their 257 vectors 2026-07-28 so Russell's Germanization
  of Early Medieval Christianity could be vectorized whole at 146**, the owner's ruling: keep
  mystical-body, drop other Faheys as needed — the de-vectorized two stay fully BM25-served),
  crusades + inquisition, the Indo-European research recut at `chunk_words: 1000`, and Russell
  (**moved to band 5, the deep shelf, by the owner's 2026-07-29 ruling — beside Schaff's History and
  the Second Temple sources, his content not a boost carrying Germanization questions; vectors kept,
  vectorization being band-agnostic**) — **3,863 of 4,882 used, ~1,019 headroom since the 2026-07-29
  removal of six Fish Eaters works** (jewish-question, liturgical-year, liturgy-sacraments,
  morality-social, solafide, solascriptura — dropped entirely at the owner's word: files rm'd from
  the private repo, manifest pruned, both engines swept, persona's shelf inventory trimmed to
  astronomy/crusades/inquisition; local gained `build_index.py --drop` for manifest removals).
  **`deleteByIds` has a LOW per-call id cap** (257 ids fails outright, 50 succeeds): the ingest
  sweep batches at 50 since 2026-07-28 — its old 1000-per-call batching made every real-sized vector
  sweep fail silently into the catch, which is how a `vectorize` flag-flip left stale vectors until
  repaired via `wrangler vectorize delete-vectors` in 50-id batches. After any vectorize-flag
  change, verify with `wrangler vectorize info merecat-t1` that the count actually moved (mutations
  are async — allow a minute). The semantic leg hydrates matches from ALL rooms (RV 15), so any
  room's work may carry `vectorize: true`; the rest of the corpus is BM25-only, and `chunk_words` in
  works.yml coarsens a work's chunks to spend fewer vectors). **Everything in LIBDB is derived
  data** rebuilt by `librarian/ingest.py`, which is why `runBackup()` deliberately does not mirror
  it, and questions are never stored (usage tables are counters only). **`librarian/` is the bot's
  whole mind**: `works.yml` (manifest of ~209 works with `tier` **1–7**, `vectorize` flags, and
  `store: deep` routing), `persona.md` (system prompt), `config.yml` (model + caps + topk), `extra/`
  (drop-in AI-only txt/md), `ingest.py`, `README.md` (the expansion path). **The nine weight bands
  are the owner's standing weighting policy** (works.yml header documents it; treat this as law when
  adding works — note the LADDER ORDER is not the tier-number order): 1 = the site's works +
  catechetical core incl. Cyril's Catechetical Lectures, Gregory of Nyssa's Great Catechism,
  Augustine's Enchiridion, the prayer pages, and about.html (vectorized, 1.6 boost), 2 = the KJV
  (1.45), **6 = Newman entire (1.4 — promoted to sit directly beneath the Scriptures as the
  interpretive companion the site reads the Fathers with**; the persona carries the site's whole
  reading of him: friend in both his ages, development notes with the book's three added tests
  overlaid, the book's ch. 4 authority model prevailing, cite him often, bring his reading of the
  Fathers alongside the Fathers' own words, weave Scripture + Fathers + councils + Newman into one
  argument), 3 = the named Fathers' works + the Catena + Fathers singles like City of
  God/Confessions/On Christian Doctrine/On the Incarnation (1.35), 4 = councils + schism documents
  (1.25), 5 = the deep shelf: Schaff sets incl. the History of the Christian Church and Creeds of
  Christendom volumes, the Summa, Trent, and the modern apologetics shelf (Chesterton, Gibbons)
  (1.0), 9 = the private scholars' shelf (1.3, between the Fathers and the councils — see below), 8
  = the private worldview shelf (**1.55 — the owner's settled 2026-07-29 ruling after a brief 1.6
  experiment: its own dedicated band on the second rung, above the Scriptures, with band 1 kept the
  sole highest**; the weight lives only in the worker CASE, one line to move), 7 = the Roman world
  (Gibbon, Cassius Dio, Tacitus, Suetonius, Ammianus, Zosimus, Bury — 0.9, the bottom of the totem
  by the owner's explicit word: background for the classics, never atop a doctrine answer). **The
  worldview shelf (band 8) is private**: `librarian/private/worldview/` (git-ignored, never hosted,
  never committed) holds traditional Catholic worldview material that shapes the bot's outlook on
  history, other religions, the social order, philosophy, and science — Fish Eaters topical essays
  (each topic directory concatenated into one work with per-page `#` headings; **since 2026-07-29
  only astronomy-zodiac, crusades, and inquisition remain** — the owner dropped jewish-question,
  liturgical-year, liturgy-sacraments, morality-social, solafide, and solascriptura entirely) and
  named books (Fahey, Copleston, Ripperger, Wolfgang Smith, Woods, Marshall, Adler). Provenance
  heuristic from the owner: an unlabeled txt is fisheaters.com (the files open with the Ignatius
  where-the-bishop-is quote); books identify themselves in the filename or opening. Explicitly
  papal-maximalist ITEMS are pulled entirely before ingest (the two Chair-of-St-Peter pages went);
  incidental maximalism stays and the persona's worldview rules make the book's first-millennium
  authority model correct it, and keep the shelf as the site's OUTLOOK, never 'the record'. These
  works carry `url: ""` — an url-less work cites by plain title everywhere (the /ask and mention
  source builders pass url '' through, `merecatFinishAnswer` writes the bare label instead of a
  markdown link, and the client's `srcFooter` renders a span), and **the transparency panel lists
  the private shelves in its full-shelf section under the heading “Additional works available to
  merecat that are not served in our Library” — plain titles with passage counts, no links (the
  owner's standing word of 2026-07-28, REVERSING the earlier omission rule)**. The listing is
  automatic: /about returns every works row and the client groups url-less ones under that heading,
  so future private works appear without any extra step — keep it that way. **The private shelves
  live in the THIRD room (`store: deep2`) since 2026-07-28**, when their re-push wedged the deep
  room's second 500 MB wall (the migration healed it 500 → 425 MB, the third proof that D1 reclaims
  on delete). Converted texts are scrubbed at ingest (`scrub_text` in ingest.py: tags, entities,
  base64 image debris — the epub conversions had carried literal `<span>`/SVG soup into Beale,
  Sanders, and the research file, which surfaced as garbled sources footers), and the worker
  (`merecatScrub`) plus the client footer scrub the same debris out of labels and prompt windows so
  even chats stored dirty display clean. **The scholars' shelf (band 9,
  `librarian/private/scholars/`) is the site's modern research authorities and carries standing
  doctrine in the persona**: N. T. Wright is the PRIORITIZED LENS on Paul, justification,
  Trent-and-Luther read together, Sanders, Second Temple Judaism, and the Roman/Hellenistic world
  (the site's research hope: NPP is compatible with a Tridentine account, Trent and Luther argued
  word games over generally compatible views, reconciliation via Wright may be possible), and the
  FIERCE FIRST AUTHORITY on resurrection/miracles/NT-contradiction apologetics; Fitzmyer (Anchor
  Romans) is the exegetical fallback; **the last things are settled by Beale's Revelation + City of
  God Book XX: amillennial partial preterism, refute hyper-preterism and premillennialism, tolerate
  postmillennialism, prefer Beale wherever he speaks**; Hahn, Pitre, and Marshall are side voices
  whose papal maximalism is not defended. Conversion tooling for private acquisitions: epub →
  `pandoc -f epub -t gfm` (headings survive as `#` for build_text), pdf → `pdftotext`, mobi (PalmDoc
  compression 2) → a ~40-line stdlib LZ77 extractor feeding pandoc html→gfm — no calibre on this
  machine. **`librarian/private/` is an INDEPENDENT nested git repo** — a second clone of the
  PRIVATE repo `merecatholicity/private-shelf` into that path, git-IGNORED by the parent
  (`.gitignore`), never a submodule. It was briefly a submodule on 2026-07-28 and reverted the same
  day (the "submodule withdraws as fast as it arrived" commit): **the GitHub Pages Jekyll builder
  refuses to build a tree naming a private submodule it cannot fetch**, which takes the whole site
  down — do not try it again. A fresh machine restores it with `git clone
  git@github.com:merecatholicity/private-shelf.git librarian/private` (`--recurse-submodules` on the
  parent does NOTHING now). After changing private texts: commit+push INSIDE `librarian/private/` —
  there is no gitlink sha in the parent to bump. **NEVER commit the texts into the parent itself and
  never flip private-shelf public** — the parent repo is PUBLIC and its history is published
  forever; infringing copies there would also expose the site's own repo to DMCA takedown. Second
  backup: the dedicated R2 bucket `merecatholicity-private-shelf` holds date-stamped tarballs
  (first: private-shelf-2026-07-28.tar.gz, 19 MB; NOT the 90-day backups bucket; refresh manually
  after a wave). Originals stay in ~/Documents and ~/Downloads. **Retrieval is five-legged**
  (hard-won, see `merecatRetrieve`): Vectorize semantic over band 1, a tier-WEIGHTED BM25 leg (the
  ladder above), a RAW unweighted BM25 leg (so a verbatim deep-shelf hit can never be crowded out by
  boosted works quoting the same words), a PHRASE leg (`merecatPhrases` slides 6-token windows over
  the question so a quotation in the question finds the text that IS the quote), and a VERSE leg
  (`merecatVerseSeats`: a chapter:verse reference written in the question fetches that verse's own
  chunk by anchor from every Bible on the shelf and seats it with phrase strength — BM25 ranks
  essays ABOUT a passage above the passage itself, and the model once answered a Douay-Rheims
  rendering question wrongly from memory; the accepted book spellings are `MERECAT_BIBLE` in the
  worker, which MUST stay in step with the client's `BIBLE` autolink table — the wordlists
  discipline — plus worker-only Vulgate namings and the deuterocanon, with `MERECAT_KJV2DR`
  translating the 23 divergent slugs, since the DR's 1–4 Kings are not the KJV's) — merged, then
  reranked. Questions are translated by `merecatMatch` (stopwords dropped, ≤16 informative tokens,
  **OR-joined**) NOT the forum's `buildMatch` (10 ANDed tokens — right for terse searches, fatal for
  natural questions). **Three databases** since the classics overflow: `merecat-library` holds bands
  1–6 plus config/usage/chats via binding LIBDB; `merecat-library-deep` holds the great sets, the
  private shelves, and the original Roman world via binding LIBDB2, routed by `store: deep`; and
  `merecat-library-deep2` holds the Latin and Greek classics with the Indo-European shelf via
  binding LIBDB3, id 98e30c6d…, routed by `store: deep2` — opened 2026-07-28 when the deep room hit
  its 500 MB wall mid-classics; the 36 flipped classics migrate on the daily runs, each re-push
  sweeping its old deep-room copy so the wedged room heals as they move, and new Second Temple or
  classics arrivals belong in deep2. Rooms are purely a space decision (the `store:` flag routes a
  work; ingest passes `store` on begin/append/end, and BOTH `begin` and `delete` sweep the work's
  rows and vectors from BOTH rooms, so flipping a work's `store` flag never leaves a stale twin
  behind — same cids would shadow the fresh text out of the retrieval pool and the /works union
  would carry two hashes for one id, re-pushing it forever; proven live with a throwaway work
  through the full flip lifecycle. `vectorize` may ride any room since RV 15's three-room semantic
  hydration; the only wall is the ~4,880-vector budget, which ingest warns on). **CLAMP POSTMORTEM
  (2026-07-29): `handleMerecatIngest` clamped stored tiers to `Math.min(7, …)`, so bands 8 and 9
  were silently stored as tier 7 (weight 0.9) from their creation — every weight ruling on the
  private shelves, including the 1.6 experiment, never actually applied until the clamp was fixed
  (now 9) and the deep2 rows repaired by direct UPDATE. After ANY band-scheme change, verify STORED
  tiers with `SELECT tier, COUNT(*) FROM works GROUP BY tier` in every room — the manifest is not
  the truth, the rows are.** Retrieval runs the keyword legs and the verse leg across ALL rooms and
  merges — a chunk carries its band wherever it lives, so the ladder weights identically across
  databases and the reranker judges the merged pool blind to the room. `/works` and `/about` union
  both rosters; the ingest projection prints per-room MB. **Content hashes are over PARSED CHUNKS,
  not file bytes** — a nav/footer edit rewrites every built page's bytes without changing one chunk,
  and byte-hashing once turned one menu line into a full-corpus re-ingest storm (ingest now builds
  every work each push to compare; ~4 min of local parsing per run is the price of storm-immunity).
  **The merecat page is open to everyone** (`?merecat=1`, a nav entry "Ask Merecat AI" — a nav
  change means `python nav.py` + full `make html` for the pandoc navs): visitors see the intro, the
  open-book panel, and a disabled composer with the board's own one-click identity drawer right
  above it; asking, threads, quota, and forwarding stay key-gated (server-side as always).
  **Forwarding**: a member may forward any single private answer to a live unlocked topic
  (`/api/merecat/forward` {chat, msg: id|'last', topic}, strictly owner-keyed) — it posts under the
  BOT's name as "Forwarded from the librarian's desk by <nick>", question quoted,
  `merecatFinishAnswer` (the shared renumber+footer helper) applied, trimmed to MAX_BODY
  footer-last; the client affordance is a per-answer "forward to the board" link opening an inline
  **drill-down picker** (2026-07-29, replacing the old pasted-link prompt): category first — only
  rooms the member may post into, the back room offered to admins alone (`allowedCats`; the server
  enforces regardless) — then the topic, then one confirm naming topic and room. The topic step
  narrows **server-side** as you type: the board cat listings grew an optional `q` title filter
  (`boardCatPayload` — up to five ANDed, LIKE-escaped, case-insensitive substrings; same param on
  public `GET /board/cat` and the keyed `/board/admin` door), so a two-topic room and a
  two-thousand-topic room both cost one twenty-row page, with "show more"/bottom-scroll appending
  pages and a 300 ms debounce + request-sequence guard on the box; locked topics render disabled.
  The persona is **live-editable in the merecat administration page** (`?merecatadmin=1`, textarea →
  `/config`): a dashboard save is live in ~5 min and STANDS until `librarian/persona.md` is itself
  next edited — ingest pushes the file only when its sha256 differs from the stored
  `persona_file_hash` config key (returned by `/works`), so the file and the dashboard reconcile by
  whichever was touched. The Scripture band carries an `apocrypha` work (`kind: bible-deutero` in
  ingest.py — the DR books the KJV lacks + Greek Daniel/Esther chapters, anchored into the DR
  reader). The phrase leg is a **stride-1 five-gram comb** (any strided comb leaves gaps — it twice
  straddled a quoted phrase) with a deep LIMIT 20 (bm25 ranks heavy quoters of a phrase above the
  text that says it once; the reranker and guaranteed phrase seats sort the pool). The `/ask`
  preamble carries `rv` (`MERECAT_RV`), the retrieval build number — bump it when retrieval changes,
  and **check it when live-testing: fresh isolates lag deploys by minutes and byte-identical results
  across "different" configs mean a stale isolate answered**. **The owner's intent in one line:
  everything is read through the book's lens plus Newman ("else it is a search engine with manners"
  — the persona's identity paragraph), the site's own voice outranks all, and the rest of the
  Library serves as evidence beneath it.** New shelf arrivals default to the band their kind implies
  (Fathers single → 3, deep set → 5) and the per-run audit names anything unplaced. **The Second
  Temple shelf (2026-07-28 ruling)**: Josephus (antiquities/wars/josephus-minor), Philo ×4, Enoch,
  Jubilees, and the Testaments sit at band 5 in the deep2 room — primary sources that dominate
  NT-world questions by CONTENT, not boost (Josephus was weighed for a higher band and kept at 5);
  the persona carries the emphasis instead: the Wars is the eyewitness record partial preterism
  reads the Olivet discourse against, and Wright is the shelf's appointed interpreter. Phaedo rides
  band 7 deep2 with its classics siblings. **Capacity, final two-room accounting (2026-07-27
  rebuild)**: 209 works / 136,965 chunks total — room one (`merecat-library`) 136 works / 41,933
  chunks / 314 MB holding bands 1–4, 6, the band-5 singles, and all config/usage/chat state; the
  deep room (`merecat-library-deep`) 73 works / 95,032 chunks / 346 MB holding the 52 great sets
  (Summa, ANF/NPNF, Schaff's History, Creeds) and the 21-work Roman world — each room comfortably
  inside its own 500 MB cap (the DB runs ~2.09× stored text, verified live). Room one once wedged at
  exactly 500 MB mid-push; **D1 reclaims file space on DELETE** (500 → 316 MB after the sets moved
  out), so a full room is recovered by marking works `store: deep` and re-pushing, never by starting
  over. Per-question cost never grows with the corpus (always top-8 chunks), so a bigger shelf costs
  storage and ingest writes only. **The whole Douay-Rheims is on the shelf since the space opened
  (2026-07-27)**: the deutero books at band 2 (`bible-deutero`), the protocanonical rest at band 5
  in the deep room (`bible-dr-proto` in ingest.py, the exact complement of the deutero cut —
  disjoint by construction, union = the whole DR, 2,975 + 408 chunks), so the KJV remains the
  quoting Scripture and a DR-specific or Vulgate-tradition question still finds Challoner's text
  directly. Citations are **proportional**, not capped: few for a simple question, a dozen when the
  question truly spans the shelf (persona + both SOURCES headers). `make librarian` / the daily
  `python ingest.py --push` pushes incrementally (per-work content hash on the server is the resume
  state; works removed from the manifest are pruned on unfiltered runs; **sources not yet built are
  skipped with a note and picked up by a later run** — how the still-arriving Newman corpus flows
  in; **every run audits works.yml against library.html** and names anything on the shelf the
  manifest lacks; `--budget-rows` keeps a big push under D1's ~100k rows-written/day free cap; the
  admin key comes from `MC_ADMIN_KEY` or the git-ignored `librarian/.key` — a dedicated
  `merecat-ingest` admin identity exists in the `admins` table for this, removable in the Admins
  console like any admin). **Ask flow** (`handleMerecatAsk`): keyed + `blockedReason`-gated +
  `POST_LIMIT`; per-member (10/day) and global (150/day) caps from config; hybrid retrieval = bge-m3
  embed → Vectorize topK + `buildMatch` BM25 over `chunks_fts` with tier-weighted rank (bm25 is
  negative-better, so a >1 multiplier boosts) → `bge-reranker-base` → top ~8 chunks; the answer
  streams as `text/plain`: one JSON line `{chat:<id>, sources:[{n,title,heading,url}]}` (deep-anchor
  URLs), a blank line, then tokens — JSON responses are refusals (`resting`/`capped`/blocked), and
  the resting message covers any Workers AI failure or spent budget. **Conversations persist as
  threads** (the DM idiom, tables `chats`/`chat_msgs` in LIBDB): `/ask` without a `chat` id mints a
  thread once the model accepts the question (the preamble carries the id back and the client adopts
  it into the URL), with an id it continues the caller's own thread (ownership-checked); threads
  keep **30 days from last message** (`MERECAT_CHAT_DAYS`) — **unless saved: `chats.saved = 1`
  (owner-set via keyed `/chat/save` {id, save}) exempts a thread from BOTH expiry sweeps
  permanently** until unsaved or deleted (unsaving one already past the cut lets the next sweep take
  it; the client warns). The client's Past-conversations panel splits into "Saved conversations
  (kept permanently)" and "Kept thirty days", save/unsave beside delete on every row. Note saved ≠
  backed up: the merecat DB stays outside `runBackup()` by design, so "permanent" means
  exempt-from-pruning, not disaster-proof. Owner-only via `/api/merecat/chats` (list, which also
  opportunistically prunes the caller's expired unsaved threads), `/chat` (read), `/chat/save`,
  `/chat/delete`. **Admin observation (2026-07-29, REVERSING the old no-admin-read design now that
  the terms disclose review-for-improvement)**: `POST /api/merecat/admin/threads` (paged list of ALL
  threads, who asked resolved from the comments-DB profiles, newest first) and
  `/api/merecat/admin/thread` {id} (one thread's full messages) — both `requireAdmin`, strictly
  READ-ONLY (SELECT only, no prune/write), **bounded to a rolling `MERECAT_CHAT_DAYS` (30-day)
  window on `last_at` — list, count, AND detail — so even a SAVED thread (exempt from expiry, kept
  in its owner's list) ages off this admin view past 30 days, and a deleted thread is gone from
  `chats` so it never shows here (the owner's word: a bird's eye view of recent use, not a keep)**,
  client views `?merecatthreads=1` (`viewMerecatThreads`) + `?merecatthread=<id>`
  (`viewMerecatThread`, no composer/forward — pure observe), linked from the admin home.
  `pruneMerecatChats` joins the monthly cron chain (before `runBackup`), likewise sparing saved
  rows. **Thread memory is whole-thread at bounded cost**: the newest `MERECAT_WINDOW` (10) turns
  ride each ask verbatim (the client never supplies history) plus a running condensed
  `chats.summary`, maintained by `merecatFold` — one cheap same-model call made in the pump's
  `waitUntil` *after* the answer streams (never adds latency), folding turns that aged past the
  window (`summarized_to` is the fold cursor; a failed fold just waits for the next turn). NOTE the
  chats tables are the one **non-derived** part of LIBDB and are still deliberately unbacked —
  ephemeral by contract, the 30-day clock is the promise. `/api/merecat/about` (POST, key optional,
  READ_LIMIT) returns model id, caps, the persona verbatim, the whole works roster with chunk
  counts, today's community usage, and the caller's own count + admin flag. **The public "How
  merecat works" transparency panel was RETIRED by the owner's word (2026-07-28)** — the merecat
  page shows only a single introduction paragraph (identity + domains of expertise + the ecumenical
  reach + the Newman-plus-the-book's-three-tests lens, reduced to one paragraph at the owner's word)
  and the quota line; /about remains server-side (the merecatadmin persona editor reads it), and
  every earlier keep-the-panel-updated rule is void. Do not resurrect the panel unasked. **Admins
  are never capped** (`isAdminHash` bypasses both the per-member and global checks; their use still
  counts in every tally, so the page shows an over-the-line 12/10 plainly — the true wall for them
  is the free budget itself). The always-visible **quota line** under the composer feeds from the
  light `/api/merecat/usage` POST on open and from each answer's preamble
  (`used:{you,cap,today,gcap,admin}` rides the first JSON line); reset times are spoken in the
  reader's local clock, computed client-side only. Per-work chunk counts are **stamped on the works
  row at ingest `end`** (`works.chunks`) so roster reads never scan the chunk store. **@merecat in
  public**: the librarian is also a pseudo-member — the fixed `MERECAT_BOT` hash (constant in both
  worker and `comments.js`; its random preimage was discarded, so no key can produce it and nobody
  can post as the bot). A **live** post/comment by a real identity containing `@merecat` triggers
  `merecatMentionReply` from `handlePost`'s `waitUntil`: brief = where the thread lives + the
  topic/last ~12 board replies (or last ~10 page comments) + the asking comment, retrieval supplies
  the shelf (the page's own text is in the corpus, so it is never inlined), and the answer posts as
  a fresh live comment by the bot (board replies bump the topic and fan out notifications; sources
  ride as markdown links). The mention charges the mentioner's daily count (admins uncapped); at a
  cap the bot still replies with a zero-cost note pointing at the merecat page for the renewal time
  (never naming a timezone publicly). Held-then-approved posts don't auto-trigger — the admin
  `/api/merecat/mention` lever re-summons on any comment id. A mention standing only inside quoted
  `>` lines never summons — `merecatMentioned` tests unquoted text in both the auto-trigger and the
  lever, so quoting a summons cannot resummon (nor charge the quoter). Guards: nick containing
  "merecat" is refused in `handleProfileSave`, `handleDmSend` refuses the bot as recipient,
  `handleDmDirectory` omits it, `deliverNotifications` never notifies or auto-watches it, the client
  hides DM affordances on its posts/profile, and the mention picker pins a "merecat · AI BOT 🐈"
  entry that inserts the literal `@merecat` token (no hash). The bot's profile (nick `merecat 🐈 AI
  BOT`, faith nicene, bio, signature) is **hardcoded in `merecatEnsureProfile`** and re-upserted on
  every reply; its 🐈 avatar is a 400×400 JPEG in the avatars R2 bucket under its hash. Chat model is
  `@cf/qwen/qwen3-30b-a3b-fp8` (~48 neurons/question ≈ 200 q/day free, shared with Llama Guard +
  LLaVA); **switchable via `config.yml` with no redeploy** (worker config cache is per-isolate,
  5-min TTL). Two hard-won stream gotchas live in `merecatPump`: purely numeric tokens arrive as
  JSON **numbers** (coerce with `String()`, or years and `[n]` citation digits vanish), and
  `merecatThinkStripper` strips `<think>…</think>` spans across chunk borders (qwen3 reasons;
  `/no_think` is appended to the system prompt but the filter is the guarantee). **Anchor
  invariant**: `ingest.py`'s pandoc walk must mirror `deeplink.js`'s id assignment
  (`h1.unnumbered,h2–h6,p` order, `paraN` reset per heading → `<heading-id>__pN`, `unique()` +
  `toc-` collision) — change them together; hand pages chunk by their semantic ids (each objection
  is its own `#oN`), Bibles chunk from `kjv.json`/`dr.json` with `slug-ch-v` anchors, and ingest
  validates every anchor before pushing. Client answers render through `fillBody` (so `John 6:53`
  autolinks into the KJV reader) and the sources footer is built as plain same-site links.
- **merecat-local, the GPU backend (retired 2026-09-10).** For fourteen months the librarian had an
  offline twin on the owner's RX 9070 XT: `local/serve.py` behind a Tailscale Funnel, the same shelf
  and five legs with the whole corpus vectorized locally, a routing switch (`config.backend`) with
  failover, a reader-facing reasoning selector (off…max) that appended a depth directive to the
  system prompt, `{queue}` heartbeats, STX/ETX marks and a reranker canary. The owner retired it on
  2026-09-10 — Cloudflare Workers AI is the only backend from now on — and everything that existed
  for it left in the same change: the `local/` tree, `merecatLocalFetch`/`merecatLocalRead` and the
  mention's local leg in `lib.ts`, the DO's local branch and `#relayLocal` in `durable.ts`, the
  `/api/merecat/backends` health probe (the route stays, cloud-only), `MERECAT_LOCAL_URL` from
  `wrangler.jsonc`, the `backend`/`failover` config keys, the admin routing switch and the client's
  local-only copy, `tests/py/test_serve_thinkstrip.py` and `test_llm_tokens.py`. Two things survived
  on purpose: the caps now apply unconditionally (they were gated on strict-Cloudflare mode), and
  the think-stripper — moved to `pure.js` so plain Node can test it — kept the GPU twin's one extra
  guarantee: reasoning that arrives with no opening tag and a bare `</think>` (what a chat template
  that pre-opens the think block produces) is swallowed, and before anything visible has started the
  reasoning buffered ahead of it is dropped (`tests/worker/merecat_think.test.mjs`, the Python cases
  ported). `backend: 'cloudflare'` rides `/usage`, `/about`, `/ask-init` and the ask preamble for
  one deploy so clients built before the retirement still read it. Owner's hands, outside the repo:
  `wrangler secret delete MERECAT_LOCAL_KEY`; `DELETE FROM config WHERE k IN ('backend','failover')`
  in `merecat-library`; the three systemd units and the Funnel on the box. The full account of the
  design stays in git history (this bullet before 2026-09-10) and in the reasoning-dials passage
  that replaced its one lasting idea.
- **The reasoning dials (2026-09-10).** The one lasting idea of the GPU twin was its reasoning
  ladder — off · low · medium · high · xhigh · max — a reader's choice that became a plain directive
  in the system prompt. Workers AI's Qwen3 reasons too: the prompt's closing token tells it whether
  it may (`/think` or `/no_think`; the site had always sent the latter) and its thinking streams
  inside `<think>` spans that `merecatThinkStripper` removes. So the same ladder now drives the
  cloud model, and it is a kernel module, **`Domain.Merecat`** (the 28th): the ladder and its order,
  `effortParse` (lenient, `instant` folds into `off`, garbage into a caller's fallback),
  `effortClamp`, the per-level directive, the per-level output **headroom** (thinking and answer
  share one `max_tokens`, so `low` adds 512 tokens, `max` 8,192 — without it a long think would
  truncate the answer it was meant to improve), the labels, the default-OFF `reasoning_on` switch,
  the resting values, the sampling `temperature` (the 0.35 the worker had hardcoded for a year, now
  a config key clamped 0–1) and the **nine band weights** (the hardcoded `CASE` in the weighted
  search leg, now `band_weights`, a CSV of nine decimals clamped 0.1–3.0 — the wrong count or one
  unreadable entry yields the default ladder whole, because a half-read ladder would silently
  re-rank the shelf); `parseDecimal` is written by hand because the kernel carries no `numbers`
  package. **The admin holds four dials over every reader's choice** (the merecat admin page, saved
  through `/api/merecat/config` like the caps): the switch (`reasoning_on`, off = exactly what the
  site did before), the level a new reader starts at (`reasoning_default`, `low`), the highest any
  reader may pick (`reasoning_max`, `high`), and the level a thread mention reasons at
  (`mention_effort`, `high`). **The server decides**: `merecatEffortFor(cfg, asked)` in `lib.ts`
  applies the switch first, then parses the reader's request against the default and clamps it to
  the ceiling; the `ChatRoom` stores THAT level, builds the prompt with it (`merecatPrompt` takes
  `effort`; `merecatThinkSuffix` closes the system message), spends `cfg.max_tokens + headroom` at
  `cfg.temperature`, and names the level that actually ran in the `meta` frame. Mentions run through
  the same gate at `mention_effort`. The reader's selector under the composer is a courtesy copy: it
  appears only while the switch is on, offers nothing above the ceiling (labels from the kernel
  through `mcCore`), remembers the choice on the device and sends it as a request. **The config
  chain grew up with it**: the write endpoint's allowlist is a map with a coercion per key
  (`MERECAT_CONFIG_KEYS` in `index.ts` — every value lands in the table only in the shape the reader
  would produce from it), `merecatConfig` reads every dial through the kernel, `MERECAT_DEFAULTS`
  takes its values from `Domain.Merecat` rather than literals, and `/usage` and `/backends` carry
  `reasoning:{on,default,max,mention,ladder}` so the client knows what to offer.
  `librarian/config.yml` owns `temperature` and (commented, ready) `band_weights` — tuning the
  shelf's ranking is an edit and a push, no worker deploy — while the four reasoning dials are
  dashboard-owned; a key present in the file overwrites the dashboard on every push, which the file
  says in so many words. `MERECAT_RV` is 16. Specs: `tests/purescript/merecat.test.mjs` (the ladder,
  clamp, headroom, temperature, the band ladder and its SQL arm) and `tests/worker/merecat.test.mjs`
  (defaults from the kernel, reads through the kernel, the allowlist map, the DO's clamp, the
  prompt's closing token, no hardcoded temperature, the client's selector). Cost is the owner's to
  watch: every level above Off spends more output tokens and more neurons from the shared
  10,000-neuron day, per model on the Platform usage page.
- **merecat is built by the pipeline (2026-09-10).** The owner's rule: every iteration of the
  librarian ships the way everything else does — edit its inputs, commit, push — and only when those
  inputs changed, incrementally, because a full rebuild is slow. What the exploration found first:
  the ingest (`librarian/ingest.py --push`) was ALREADY incremental server-side (`works.hash` over
  parsed chunks; a `--budget-rows` stop exits 0 and resumes next run) and needed no Cloudflare
  credential at all — an admin key over HTTPS, the BUILT `docs/` tree and the private shelf clone.
  What kept it a hand job was the edge: Bot Fight Mode turns a GitHub runner away, and on the Free
  plan it cannot be skipped by any rule (Cloudflare's docs, verified). **The road**:
  `.github/workflows/merecat.yml` (CICD.md §2.5) fires on a push to `main` that touches
  `librarian/**` or the library's sources (`content/`, `resources/`, `book/`, `partials/`, the two
  generators), daily at 04:10 UTC, and on dispatch; waits for this commit's Build; restores its
  `site-2-<sha>` cache (an EXACT hit — a fallback would ingest an older site); clones the private
  shelf with a read-only deploy key (absent → skipped, and skipping never prunes); and runs the
  ingest against **the worker's workers.dev hostname** — `wrangler.jsonc` now declares `workers_dev:
  true`, and `index.ts` answers 404 on that hostname for every path but
  `/api/merecat/{works,config,ingest}`, each of which demands **`MERECAT_INGEST_KEY`**, a new worker
  secret honoured by `requireIngest()` and by nothing else, so a leaked runner secret could at worst
  rewrite the shelf. The private shelf's own pushes arrive by a one-line workflow in that repository
  (`gh workflow run merecat.yml` with a PAT scoped to *Actions: write* on the site repo). **What
  makes it cheap**: the parse ledger (`librarian/.ledger.json`, git-ignored, cached between runs as
  `merecat-ledger-*`, and used by `make librarian` too) remembers each source's signature — parser
  version + manifest entry + source bytes — so an unchanged work is not parsed again (the full parse
  is ~4 min; a private-shelf-only push is seconds); the server's hash means an unchanged work is not
  pushed again; the run writes a markdown job summary (pushed / unchanged / parse-skipped / waiting
  / pruned / budget state) and stamps `last_ingest` + `last_ingest_by` into the config table, which
  the merecat admin page shows. The ledger answers "was this parsed", never "was this pushed": a
  remembered work the server lacks is still pushed (`tests/py/test_ingest_ledger.py`). **Declared,
  not clicked**: `github_repository_deploy_key.private_shelf_ci` in `terraform/github.tf`, present
  once the public half rides the Actions variable `PRIVATE_SHELF_DEPLOY_PUBLIC_KEY`
  (`TF_VAR_private_shelf_deploy_key` in `terraform.yml`). The credentials themselves are minted by
  hand, as all credentials are (CICD.md §10.2): `wrangler secret put MERECAT_INGEST_KEY` + `gh
  secret set MC_INGEST_KEY`, `ssh-keygen` for the shelf key (private half →
  `PRIVATE_SHELF_DEPLOY_KEY`), the private repo's `SITE_DISPATCH_TOKEN`. Until `MC_INGEST_KEY`
  exists the workflow exits 0 with a notice and the shelf stays a hand job. The first pipeline run
  of a changed corpus is multi-day by design — the D1 free write budget is ~100k rows a day and the
  whole shelf is ~685k estimated rows — and the daily schedule is what finishes it; every run is
  idempotent and resumable because the server hash is the state. Worker code (retrieval legs, the
  prompt, the ChatRoom, the band CASE) still ships through `workers.yml`; a change to the band
  SCHEME touches both (`works.yml` tiers and `band_weights`).
 **The completion mark (v125, retired with the GPU backend 2026-09-10):** every whole answer from
`serve.py` ended in ETX `\u0003` so a relay could tell a clean end from a death mid-stream; the
cloud path never needed it — the DO owns the generation and writes `done=1` itself — and the mark
left with the backend.
- **The Cloudflare free-tier usage monitor (2026-08-03).** `admin.html?usage=1` (the "Platform
  usage" door; Lit `mc-usage` in `app/views/admin.ts`; route `RUsage` in `Domain.Route` — a new
  admin param means Route.purs + the core.ts membrane record + classicRoute + the dispatch, all
  four, plus `tests/purescript/route.test.mjs`) draws live health bars for EVERY free-plan meter the
  platform rides: Workers requests/day (ACCOUNT-wide — other workers on the account share the pool
  and show in the per-script detail), Workers AI neurons/day, D1 rows read+written/day + storage
  (per-DB detail barred against the empirical 500 MB free wall), R2 Class A/B ops/month + storage
  (per bucket, 10 GB), DO requests/duration/rows/SQLite bytes, Vectorize queried dims/month + stored
  dims (the ~4,880-vector budget reads ~79% by construction), Realtime TURN egress (1,000 GB/mo),
  Turnstile count (unmetered, context), cron slots. Server: `comments-worker/src/usage.ts` queries
  the **GraphQL Analytics API** per product under `Promise.allSettled` — one failed dataset costs
  one "unavailable" card, never the page — via **`CF_USAGE_TOKEN`** (a READ-ONLY API token, single
  scope **"Account Analytics: Read"**, set with `wrangler secret put CF_USAGE_TOKEN`; the dataset
  field names — `totalNeurons`, `storedVectorDimensions`, `egressBytes`, `activeTime` µs → GB-s ×
  0.125 — were probed live against the real account before the queries were written, not guessed)
  beside the `CF_ACCOUNT_ID` var; until both stand the page serves the setup steps and everything
  no-ops. All decidable logic is PURE in `usagecalc.ts` (the `FREE` limits table stamped `asOf` — a
  Cloudflare plan change edits that ONE table; R2 op classification with unknown ops counted as
  Class A so novelty can only read worse; UTC day/month windows; bands ok<60≤watch<80≤hot<100≤over),
  spec in `tests/worker/usage.test.mjs`. Endpoint `POST /api/comments/admin/usage` (requireAdmin;
  API.md §8). **The daily alert**: a THIRD cron `30 23 * * *` (late UTC day so day-quota meters read
  near-complete; 3 of the account's 5 free slots — a new cron needs its own `event.cron` branch in
  `scheduled()` or it runs the MONTHLY chain) reruns the report and **system-DMs every admin as
  merecat** when any meter crosses 80%/100% — escalations at once, standing warnings weekly, sub-80
  forgotten so a re-cross speaks fresh (`foldUsageAlerts`; state JSON in
  `app_settings.usage_alert_state`, read/written directly, never through the 5-min settings cache).
  `webtest/test_admin_reads.py` covers the page in both the configured and setup states plus the
  visitor gate.
- **The AI budget guard (2026-09-10).** The question caps ration the librarian by COUNT
  (`global_daily`, `user_daily`); nothing watched the METER the count was a proxy for — the
  account's Workers AI day (10,000 neurons, shared with the Llama Guard screening, the LLaVA avatar
  checks, embeddings and reranking), which the free plan refuses past the line and a paid plan
  bills. The owner's rule: merecat must never be the reason the account crosses it. So the worker
  now reads the meter itself before every question and every @merecat mention — the same
  `aiInferenceAdaptiveGroups` select the Platform usage page draws (`aiNeuronsSelect` in
  `comments-worker/src/quota.ts`, which `usage.ts` imports so there is ONE copy; the GraphQL glue
  moved to `analytics.ts` so both can share it without a lib import) aggregated by the monitor's own
  `buildReport`, so the guard and the health bar can never disagree — and rests the librarian when
  the day's spend reaches the admin's line. **Kernel** (`Domain.Merecat`): `quotaGuardDefaults` (ON,
  95), `quotaGuardOnFrom` (the social switch's default-ON polarity: only a literal "0" is off,
  because an absent row must mean guarded), `quotaGuardPctFrom` (a whole percent 10–99, anything
  else the default; 100 is the wall, not a line), `quotaTripped` (used·100 ≥ limit·pct; no limit, no
  trip), `hoursUntilUtcMidnight` (whole hours rounded up, 24 at midnight itself, never 0) and
  `restingNote` (what a resting librarian says: "come back in about N hours, when it renews at
  midnight UTC", "in under an hour" for the last — now the QUESTION cap's refusal too, in the
  ChatRoom and the mention, so every collective limit names the hours). **Worker** (`quota.ts`, no
  lib import, Node-tested with a stubbed fetch in `tests/worker/quota.test.mjs`; the worker tsconfig
  gained `allowImportingTsExtensions` so its sibling imports carry `.ts` and Node runs it as-is):
  `merecatQuota(env, cfg)` → `{on, pct, configured, used, limit, meter_pct, resting, reset_in_h,
