  note, read_at, stale, unread}`; `quotaPublic` is the member slice (no raw counts, no age). Reading
  discipline: one select reused a minute per isolate (each ChatRoom and each worker isolate keeps
  its own — a handful a minute at the busiest; the guard's select has a 5 s timeout, not the
  monitor's 12), trusted fifteen minutes more when a fresh read fails, a failed read not retried for
  a minute (a dead analytics API costs one timeout, not one per ask), and **with no reading at all
  the guard STANDS OPEN** and logs `merecat_quota_unread` — a monitor outage must never take the
  librarian down; the usage token missing (`CF_USAGE_TOKEN` / `CF_ACCOUNT_ID`) means no meter, no
  guard, and the admin page says so in words. **Where it binds**: `ChatRoom.#ask` after the count
  caps and BEFORE the thread is minted (a refusal leaves no empty conversation) → `{t:'state',
  phase:'error', resting:true, quota:true, reset_in_h, error}`; `handleMerecatAskInit` before
  minting → **503** `{ok:false, resting:true, quota:true, reset_in_h, error}` with `Retry-After` in
  seconds (the client's `refuse` path shows it; a native client keys on the status);
  `merecatMentionReply` → the no-cost resting note plus the see-when link. **Admins are held to it**
  — the caps ration, the guard protects the account — and the admin page is where it is switched
  off; every refusal logs `merecat_quota_rest` with the meter's share and the line. **Why 95 and not
  99**: the line is a margin, not the wall — the adaptive analytics run a minute or two behind the
  spend, a plain qwen3 question is ~48 neurons (~0.5% of the day) and one at Max reasoning several
  times that (8,192 tokens of headroom at output prices), a cached reading can be a minute old, and
  the board's screening keeps spending after the librarian rests; 500 neurons covers all of that at
  this site's traffic, and the admin can move the line anywhere in 10–99. **Config chain**:
  `quota_guard_on` / `quota_guard_pct` in `MERECAT_CONFIG_KEYS` (the switch's coercion stores '0'
  only for an explicit 0/false — the YAML `false` trap), `merecatConfig` reads both through the
  kernel, `MERECAT_DEFAULTS` takes them from `Domain.Merecat`; dashboard-owned like the reasoning
  dials (config.yml documents them commented, ready to pin). **Wire**: `used.quota` (the public
  slice) rides every `used` — ask-init, `hello`, `state thinking`, `meta`, `/usage`; `/backends`
  carries the full view for the admin page (API.md §2.4, §5.2, §5.3). **Client**: `applyGuard` in
  `viewMerecat` closes the composer while resting (a member's box only — the identity gate owns it
  until then) and reopens it when a later `used` says the day renewed; the quota line shows the note
  plus the local renewal time while resting, and "the day's AI budget is N% spent; merecat rests at
  P%" otherwise; the admin dials block (switch, line, and a status line that names the four states:
  no meter, off, unread, the reading) sits under Reasoning; `mcCore` exposes
  `merecatQuotaGuardDefaults` / `merecatQuotaGuardPctFrom`. Specs:
  `tests/purescript/merecat.test.mjs` (the six rules), `tests/worker/quota.test.mjs` (cache, stale,
  backoff, fail-open, the member slice, one dataset), `tests/worker/merecat.test.mjs` (the chain,
  the three gates, the wire, the page), `tests/worker/usage.test.mjs` (a neurons-only raw is a legal
  partial); `webtest/test_worker_reads.py` asserts the `quota` shape on `/usage` against prod. NOT
  guarded, on purpose: the board's own Workers AI spend (screening fails open by its own rule) and
  the Vectorize monthly meter merecat alone queries (30 M dims a month at ~10 k a question — nowhere
  near); if either ever needs a wall, `merecatQuota` is where a second meter joins.
- **Comments sections are admin-switched, per page and per journal article (2026-09-10).** The
  owner's ruling: the sections under the book and the articles are OFF, and the admin opens them one
  by one; comments live only under the site's OWN writings, never under a work hosted in the
  library; and one more switch gives every journal article a section of its own, made with the
  article and retired with it. **Kernel** (`Domain.Comments`, the 29th module): `commentablePages` —
  the seven own writings (`/book.html` + the six content pages with `comments: true`), MOVED here
  from the worker's `PAGES` (now `Comments.commentablePaths`, single-sourced;
  `tests/js/commentable_pages.test.mjs` holds the build's widget pages and this list to the same
  set, over the sources, and refuses the tail partial anywhere in `resources/`);
  `parseEnabledPages`/`serializeEnabledPages`/`pageEnabled` (the `comments_pages` CSV — only
  commentable paths survive, deduped, canonical order, the `Media.parseKinds` idiom);
  `journalEnabledFrom` (the `comments_journal` switch — **only a literal '1' opens it, the OPPOSITE
  polarity of `Domain.Wall`/`Domain.Turnstile`, deliberately**: those default ON because getting
  them wrong darkens a feature that was always on; these default OFF because the owner switched the
  sections off and a fresh database must open none); `journalKey`/`journalKeyId` (the
  `journal:<article id>` page key, strict and canonical so one article has one key string —
  `journal:012` is nothing); `pageHref` (a journal key → `/journal.html?a=<id>`, for permalinks, the
  audit's jump links and `viewLink`). **Worker**: `commentsPageKey(env, raw)` is the ONE resolver
  `handleGet`, `handlePost`, `handleEdit` and `handleFeed` share — an unknown path, a commentable
  page switched off, or a journal key whose switch is off or whose article is not live in the
  standing journal all answer null, and every caller then refuses exactly as it refuses an unknown
  page (`400 'Unknown page.'`; the edit gives the missing-row `403 'Not yours, or already gone.'`),
  so a closed section is indistinguishable from a page that never had one.
  `journalTopic`/`journalArticleLive` are the predicate `handleJournal` and the gate share (journal
  on; a live board topic outside the back room by an unmuted author; the article its head or one of
  its live unmuted replies). Deliberately NOT gated: `/delete` (retraction), `/meta` (admin-keyed,
  and it accepts journal keys so the fingerprint drawer works there), `/pending` + `/approve`, every
  sweep. `/config` carries `comments:{pages:[…open], journal}` (the client's courtesy copy);
  `handleJournal` carries `comments` on both shapes. **Cleanup**: `sweepJournalComments(env,
  deletedTopicId?)` — one idempotent UPDATE whose SQL is exported (`JOURNAL_SWEEP_SQL`,
  `JOURNAL_SWEEP_THREAD_SQL`) so the spec runs the real statement against the real migrations in
  `node:sqlite` — soft-deletes every `journal:<id>` row whose article is deleted or gone (a HELD
  article keeps its comments, so an approval never finds them missing); the thread form (the
  journal's own topic deleted) retires every article's, since the reply rows stay as live orphans
  exactly as a topic delete always left them. Called from `handleSelfDelete` (a reply of the journal
  topic → the plain sweep; the head → the thread form), `handleModerate` delete (the head),
  `handleDeleteUser`, and the monthly cron right after `pruneComments` (which may just have
  hard-pruned the articles); the soft-deleted rows then ride the ordinary 30-day hard prune.
  Re-pointing `journal_topic` deletes NOTHING — those rows merely become unreachable and return if
  it is pointed back (the social switch's rule: a switch deletes nothing). **Client**:
  `pageKey()`/`pageHref()` replace `pagePath()` at every API and permalink site (boot-scoped
  `COMMENTS_KEY`/`COMMENTS_HREF`, null on an article page where the two are one); `start()` asks
  `commentsCfg()` (the shared, cached `/config` read — no extra request) and mounts NOTHING for a
  page not listed: no widget, no comments read, and unknown (config unreachable, an older worker)
  reads as closed; `mountComments(host)` is the widget split out of `start()` so
  `viewJournalArticle` mounts one under an entry (`div.journal-comments`, `styles/main.css`) when
  the read said `comments:true`; the audit's page rows jump through `commentsPageHref`; the settings
  page gains **Comments on our own writings** (one checkbox per `mcCore.commentablePages`, right
  after the social switch, grouped Books / Articles / The Journal — the journal's switch sits THERE
  with the others, not down in the Journal panel, which governs the page itself; it was first put in
  that panel and the owner could not find it), saved as the two keys; the admin hub's door text
  names them. Propagation is the usual ~5 minutes (the per-isolate settings cache + the edge-cached
  `/config`). Specs: `tests/purescript/comments.test.mjs` (the rules and the polarity),
  `tests/worker/comments_switches.test.mjs` (allowlist, coercion, the one resolver by handler name,
  the byte-identical refusals, what stays open, the sweep's SQL on fixtures, no migration),
  `tests/js/commentable_pages.test.mjs` (build ↔ kernel parity), `tests/js/core.test.mjs` (the
  membrane's coercions); `webtest/test_settings_page.py` expects the eighth heading. **Adding an own
  page**: frontmatter `comments: true` (or the tail partial) AND a row in `commentablePages`, in one
  change — the parity test refuses half of it; a path is a storage key, so never rename one. Not
  supported, on purpose: a `journal:<id>` feed as a Discord subscription scope (`parseFeedScope`
  takes site paths only; the RSS feed itself works).
- **The commentable pages are DETECTED, not listed (2026-09-10, the same day).** The first cut kept
  `Domain.Comments.commentablePages` by hand, so a new article or book would have needed a kernel
  edit before its switch appeared — the owner's ruling is that the console must find our writings
  itself. Now `scripts/writings.py` reads the SOURCES: every page built from `content/` (an ordinary
  page needs no key; `comments: false` in its frontmatter opts a utility page out — terms, privacy,
  the Library and Resources catalogs, the Logos download page and the Hours are out today) and every
  book the root Makefile builds with pandoc into `docs/<name>.html` (titled by its `--metadata
  title`; the memorandum `bishop-presbyter.html` now takes `book-tail.html` and is a book with a
  switch), and writes them — books first, then articles by slug — as the generated
  `purescript/src/Domain/Writings.purs` (`writings :: Array {path, title, kind}`), which
  `Domain.Comments` reads. The module is git-IGNORED and regenerated by `npm run build:ps`, so every
  `make psbuild`, every `make tests`/`jscheck`/`bundle` and every CI gate is current by construction
  — nothing to commit, nothing to go stale; `make writings` is the hand road. The ONE rule for "this
  page carries the widget" is `content.carries_comments(fm)` (`fm.get('comments') is not False`):
  content.py stamps the mount by it and the detector lists by it, so a listed page always has its
  section to open; a book built without the tail partial is REFUSED by the detector rather than
  given a dead switch. The console groups the switches under Books and Articles (`kind`). Guards:
  `tests/py/test_writings.py` (the rules, the refusals, the byte-stable escaped emission, the rule
  applied to this tree), `tests/js/commentable_pages.test.mjs` (a second, independent derivation
  over the sources held equal to the kernel; every book target built with the tail; the library
  never), `tests/purescript/comments.test.mjs` (the shape and the anchors). Not detected, on
  purpose: the hand pages `nav.py` still owns (`where-to-begin.html`, `the-book.html`, the home
  page) — move a page into `content/` to make it a writing.
- **The DM press-and-hold surface (2026-09-10): a message's acts on ONE surface, WhatsApp-shaped,
  with reactions beyond a heart, a quoted reply the server cannot see, and a saved message that
  looks saved.** The owner's ask, with a WhatsApp screenshot: the inbox should be disciplined and
  usable the way WhatsApp is — press and hold a message for a reaction and its acts — without losing
  what is ours. What stood before: a ♡ toggle on received bubbles and a passive ❤ chip on mine
  (2026-08-03), and a ⋯ `postMenu` in each bubble's head with save/edit/delete as link rows; a
  `comment-head` author line ("You" / the name) with the time, forum-style. **The bubble is
  WhatsApp-shaped now** (`dmBubble`, the one frame every text, media, expired-media and redacted
  message shares): no author line — the side and the fill say who; the optional system label (`⚙️
  Automated notice`); the quote block of what it answers; the body; and a meta row at the foot
  (`.dm-meta`: `edited`, the saved ★, the time, and on mine the ticks — `✓` delivered, `✓✓` seen,
  colored, titled, no words). Day chips (`dmDayNode`: Today / Yesterday / Mon, Sep 8 / Sep 8, 2025)
  stand over the first bubble of each day, on the history, the live drop-in and my own echo alike
  (`placeMsg`), so a bubble carries only the time. **The surface** (`dmOpenActions`): a
  press-and-hold on touch (430 ms, cancelled by 8 px of movement — a press that moves is a scroll;
  the click that follows a hold is swallowed so a link under the finger does not also navigate;
  `navigator.vibrate` where it exists), a right-click, the ⌄ that appears on hover (`.dm-more`,
  hidden under `(hover:none)`), or a tap on the bubble's reaction pill. Over it: **the reaction
  bar** — `Domain.Dm.quickReactions` (👍 ❤️ 😂 😮 😢 🙏), my current one lit and added when it is not
  among the six, tapping mine again withdraws it, and a **+** that swaps in the whole
  `buildEmojiPanel` (now taking an `onPick` callback beside its textarea use) — the standard set AND
  our own memes/pepe packs, the reaction WhatsApp cannot offer, rendered as the pack image by
  `dmReactionNode`; **the bubble itself, untouched, lit in a hole between four pieces of scrim** (no
  clone: the real node keeps its blob media, its styles, its receipts); and **the menu** — Reply ·
  Copy (text, or a media caption) · Edit (mine, text, not a system notice) · Save/Unsave (★ lit when
  saved) · Delete (mine) — only the acts that apply; no Forward, Star or More (the owner's ruling
  from the screenshot). On a phone the page is first scrolled just enough for bar + bubble + menu to
  fit (a bubble taller than the room takes the bar over its head and the menu over its foot),
  measured against `visualViewport` so a raised keyboard does not hide the menu, then the document
  is locked through **`window.mcSheet.lock()`/`unlock()`** — `lockDocument` now returns `true` only
  when THIS call took the lock, and the surface releases only a lock it took, so a surface over an
  open sheet cannot free the sheet's — and the scrim pieces are `touch-action:none;
  overscroll-behavior:contain`: the three layers every overlay here keeps (the sheet passage above).
  On desktop it is a popover at the pointer (`.dm-act-pop`) dismissed by an outside click, a scroll
  outside it, Escape, or a resize; focus goes to the first item and returns on close. One at a time
  (`dmActOpen`); the boot's abort tears it down (`bootSig`); a redacted bubble (`node.mcDead`) opens
  nothing, and a surface over a bubble that gets redacted live closes. **A swipe to the right
  replies** (touch: engage past 24 px with the movement mostly horizontal, follow to 72 px, arm at
  48 px with a maroon edge, fire on release) — the bubble travels by transform, so nothing widens
  the page; `touch-action: pan-y pinch-zoom` on bubbles leaves vertical panning and pinch to the
  browser; under `(hover:none)` bubbles are `-webkit-touch-callout:none; user-select:none` so a hold
  is a hold, not a selection (the edit textarea re-enables selection). **Reactions**: ONE emoji per
  side per message, `react_a`/`react_b` TEXT on `dms` (**migration `0012_dm_reactions.sql`**, which
  carries every `liked_a`/`liked_b` = 1 forward as the `❤️` reaction — U+2764 U+FE0F, the quick
  bar's own bytes — and leaves the old flags unread; the next migration is 0013); `POST /dm/react
  {key, with, id, emoji}` (`''` withdraws) validated through **`Domain.Dm.normalizeReaction`** on
  BOTH ends — the worker's `dmReaction` membrane in lib.ts and the client's `mcCore.dmReaction`
  before the wire; exactly one emoji in the RGI shape (a regional-indicator pair, a keycap, or a
  pictographic base with its optional presentation selector / skin tone / tag sequence and any
  number of ZWJ-joined bases — two emoji side by side are two reactions and are refused) or one of
  our pack `:tokens:` (checked against `Domain.Emoji.packs`, lower-cased) under a 32-unit cap;
  `/dm/like` is an alias (`like:1` = ❤️) and the thread payload carries `react_me`/`react_other`
  (plus derived `liked_me`/`liked_other`) for one deploy of cached clients; the live frame is
  `dm-react` (`dm-like` is no more). The same visibility gate as the like had (pair, not held from
  me, not expired, not redacted, after my own clear stamp). The pill (`.dm-react-pill`) hangs off
  the bubble's bottom corner — theirs at the left of theirs, mine at the right of mine — both glyphs
  when we differ, "❤️ 2" when we agree; `main.css` gives `.dm-msg` `position:relative` for it and
  the ⌄ (locked by the CSS test). **Reply quotes ride INSIDE the E2E plaintext** — the server never
  learns what answers what, and there is no `reply_to` column (the js test forbids the string): the
  plaintext is `Domain.Dm.replySentinel` (U+0001 — no keyboard produces it; `CONTROL_RE` refuses it
  in any plaintext the worker can read) + JSON `{v:1, text, reply:{id, from, kind, text}}`
  (`dmWrapText`/`dmParseText`, proven by round trip in `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs`); plaintext
  without the sentinel is the bare message it always was, so every older message still reads; a
  media reply rides in the media envelope (`env.reply`); the excerpt is `Domain.Dm.replyExcerpt`
  (whitespace folded, ≤160 code points + `…`, counted in code points so an emoji is never split); an
  edit re-wraps the same quote; the quote block (`dmQuoteNode`) names You or them, says `📷 Photo` /
  `🎞️ Video` / `🎤 Voice note` / `📎 File` for media, and a tap jumps to the original when it is on
  the page and flashes it (else a word of feedback — pages are 20 messages). The composer shows
  "Replying to …" with a ✕ (`.dm-reply-bar`) while a reply is armed; Reply focuses the box (the
  focusin net warms Turnstile as always). **A saved message is lit for both** (`dm-saved`: a 2 px
  gold ring and the ★ in the meta — the Snapchat convention the owner asked for), and `POST
  /dm/save` now publishes `dm-save` to the other side so their bubble lights live. **Retired**:
  `dmLikeControl`, `dmSaveControl`, `dmAppendControls`, the DM bubble's `postMenu` and
  `comment-head`, the `(edited)` head marker, the `dm-like` frame, the worded receipts. **Copy** was
  added though the owner's list did not name it: the hold now owns the long press, so without it no
  text on a phone could be copied at all. Guards: `tests/purescript/dm.test.mjs` (the grammar —
  every shape accepted, every non-emoji refused, every pack code passes, the sentinel, the excerpt
  rule), `tests/worker/dm_reactions.test.mjs` (the ledger builds through 0012 on a pre-0012 database
  and backfills exactly the hearts; the handler validates through the kernel, never an inline regex;
  the alias route; the visibility gate; the live frames), `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs` (the three
  overlay layers, the lock taken-and-released discipline, one surface that dies with the boot,
  passive gestures and the swallowed click, the mount styles, the envelope round trip, no
  `reply_to`), `tests/js/core.test.mjs` (the membrane coercions), `tests/css/test_style_css.py` (the
  built bubble rules). **Proven on prod over fixtures** (`webtest/test_dm_actions.py`, the same
  day): a page-level `fetch` stub installed through chromedriver's CDP endpoint
  (`Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument`) answers the thread read with four synthetic bubbles and
  swallows every DM write, so the surface is exercised end to end — bubbles, chips, ticks, pills,
  the saved ring, the quote jump, the popover, the menus per side, the picker with a pepe reaction,
  Reply/✕, the live hooks, Escape, the phone hole + lock + swallowed click — with nothing sent,
  reacted or saved and the owner's own inbox never opened (a DM round trip proper needs two
  identities behind Turnstile, and real conversations are not a test bed): 31/31. The one thing it
  found: Chrome refuses (and logs an intervention for) `navigator.vibrate` before the frame's first
  real tap, so the buzz asks `navigator.userActivation.hasBeenActive` first (`dmBuzz`).
- **The conversation is a chat screen (2026-09-11, the owner's second look).** Three findings from
  the phone: the saved ring and gold star were noise ("indicate it more subtly, like Snapchat"); the
  thread did not feel like WhatsApp — the header with the call buttons and the bottom bar must
  always be in view; and the "Replying to" strip stood empty above the box before any reply was
  armed. **The thread is a screen now**, not a page with a form at its foot. A **sticky header**
  (`.dm-head`, under the app bar on phones and the desktop bar on desktop): the correspondent's
  avatar (their `/avatar`, or an initial), the name (hidden on phones under the shell, where the app
  bar already carries it), a subtitle that reads **Online** / **typing…** / **🔒 End-to-end
  encrypted**, the **📞** call button (the `callsCfg` + WebRTC gate as before, moved up from the
  composer) and **ⓘ**. Tapping the header text, the ⓘ, or the ⏳ chip at the top of the messages
  opens the **conversation sheet** (`infoNode`, through `mcSheet.open`; inline under the header
  without the shell): the avatar card with *View profile*, **End-to-end encrypted** with *How it
  works* and *Verify safety number* (`dmVerifyPanel`), **Disappearing messages** with the 24h/7d/30d
  chooser (`dmExpiryNode` grew an `onChange` so the chip follows; a live `dm-ttl` repaints both),
  and the danger rows **Block** / **Delete conversation** — everything that is not a message left
  the thread: the `board-topic-head` title line, the `dm-e2e` badge line, the prominent expiry note,
  the typing line and the block/delete links at the foot are gone. A **fixed composer**
  (`.dm-composer`, `position:fixed`; on phones above the tab bar and, under `body.mc-kb-open`, above
  the soft keyboard exactly as the merecat composer rides — the same `--mc-kb` the shell publishes;
  on desktop aligned to the content column by measurement, since the sidebar shifts it; a
  `.dm-c-space` spacer reserves its height under the last bubble, remeasured by a ResizeObserver as
  the bar changes — **fixed, never sticky**: the first cut was sticky, and the owner's next
  screenshot showed it floating a gap above the tab bar on open — a thread opens at the document's
  end, where a sticky bar sits in its natural flow place, above the body's tab-bar reservation and
  the footer, until a scroll re-sticks it; the battery now asserts the bar flush with the tab bar at
  390px before any scroll): the reply strip and the attach chip (hidden until they have something),
  `[+]` attach, the rounded field with a 1-row **auto-growing** textarea (to ~6 lines) and a **😊**
  that toggles the site's emoji panel (our packs included, rendered above the row), and the **mic
  that becomes Send** the moment there is text or an attachment (WhatsApp's swap; without voice,
  Send stands dimmed when idle). **Enter sends** where there is a keyboard and a fine pointer
  (Shift+Enter breaks the line; a `:` or `@` picker that already took the key keeps it); on a phone
  Enter is a new line. The composer is not wrapped by `mdEditor` (no B/I/quote toolbar, no scripture
  picker, no Preview — a chat is not a post; markdown typed by hand still renders): it calls
  `warmOnFocus(ta)` itself and carries the `.ts-slot`, so the Turnstile focus net is unchanged;
  drafts, the `:` emoji and `@` mention autocompletes, the typing signal, the media gate and the
  voice recorder (its row lands inside the composer) all ride as before. Opening lands at the foot
  (`scrollToEnd`, instant); a live arrival scrolls only when the reader was already near the foot.
  **Chrome resets the scroll position at the window `load` event** (its restoration for a fresh
  entry): on a real network the thread renders before `load`, so the first cut opened at the foot on
  a local serve (where `load` had long fired) and at the top on prod — found by the 390px battery
  and a probe that instrumented every scroll road (no JS call moved it; the reset followed `load` by
  7 ms). When `load` is still to come, the view re-lands the foot for a beat after it. Line icons
  (`mcIcon`: plus, send, mic, phone, info, smile — Feather-shaped, hand-drawn paths) replace the
  emoji glyph buttons on this screen. **The saved mark**, settled after two looks: the ring shouted,
  then a faint-ink star whispered ('a bit too subtle'); now a gold ★ in the meta row and the
  bubble's own 1px border tinted the same gold — no ring, no shadow (the menu still says
  Save/Unsave, the star lights there). **The picker and the keyboard never share a phone screen**
  (the third look: both up at once was crammed): `buildEmojiPanel`'s touch autofocus of its search
  box — what raised the keyboard under the picker — now applies only to the forum toolbar's own
  picker (a caller with `onPick` owns the keyboard); the composer's 😊 blurs the field before
  opening, becomes a keyboard button while the picker stands (`setEmojiFace`), a pick inserts the
  emoji, closes the picker on touch and refocuses the field so the keyboard returns, and a tap into
  the field closes the picker; on desktop the picker stays open beside the field, as the forum's
  does. The battery's fixture now publishes a (fake) key so the composer is live, and the 390px flow
  proves the handoff. **The hidden bug**: `replyBar.hidden = true` had no effect because
  `.dm-reply-bar{display:flex}` beats the UA's `[hidden]` — the same trap sat under the bar and the
  menu behind the picker; one rule
  `.dm-reply-bar[hidden],.dm-act-bar[hidden],…{display:none!important}` closes the class, and the
  fixture battery now asserts computed display, not the attribute. Guards:
  `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs` (the quiet mark, the hidden rule, the sticky header/composer and
  keyboard rule, the focus net by hand, the mic/Send swap, Enter's gate) and
  `webtest/test_dm_actions.py` (the header, the sheet's rows and chooser, the sticky composer as the
  section's last child, the old chrome gone, computed visibility everywhere).
- **Phones show no footer except on the home tab, and Settings → About is a themed dialog carrying
  the footer's information (2026-09-11, the owner's fourth look).** The report: a DM thread opened
  onto the footer. With the fixed composer, the thread's document still ended in the shell footer
  (`mc-footer`, appended to `<body>` by `installChrome`; the pages' own static `<footer>` is already
  hidden under `body.mc-app`), and `scrollToEnd` scrolled to the DOCUMENT's end, so the footer stood
  between the last bubble and the bar. The ruling, generalized: footers stay as they are on desktop;
  on phones there is no footer anywhere but the home tab; and since the footer's links must live
  somewhere on a phone, the gear's About becomes the place — helpful, in the theme, copyable,
  dismissable, not the gray inline block it was. **The footer**: `syncThemeArt` stamps
  `document.body.dataset.mcTab = activeTab() || 'page'` beside the art on every navigation, and one
  phone rule in main.css hides it: `body.mc-app:not([data-mc-tab="home"]) mc-footer{display:none}`
  (the base `mc-footer{display:block}` keeps desktop as it was; without the shell the static footer
  shows as before). **The thread's foot**: `scrollToEnd`/`nearEnd` measure the spacer's top against
  the fixed bar's top (`endGap`), never the document's end — so on desktop, where the footer follows
  the thread, it stays below the bar until the reader scrolls for it. **The dialog**
  (`mcDialog({title, body, actions})` in appchrome, exposed as `window.mcDialog`): a scrim and a
  centered card in the site's palette (`--surface`, `--rule`, `--maroon`, `--shadow-2`), `z-index`
  above the sheet, dismissable by ✕, Close, the scrim or Escape — Escape taken on the WINDOW in
  capture with `stopImmediatePropagation`, before the sheet's own document listener, so the settings
  stay under it; the body `user-select:text`; the overlay's three layers (scrim `touch-action:none`
  + `overscroll-behavior:contain`, the card contained, the document lock taken through
  `lockDocument()` only when free and released only when taken — a sheet usually holds it); a link
  inside it is a navigation, so it closes itself and any sheet under it. **About**
  (`McSettings.openAbout`): the state line (up to date / older version / checking), the device facts
  `<pre>` in the theme's inks (`--cream-2` on `--ink`, no longer `--faint` gray), and **the footer's
  information** — `FOOTER_LINKS` (Home, Library, Community, About the project, Contact, Terms &
  Conditions, Privacy — the union of the shell footer's short form and the static partial's) as
  links under the © line — with *Reload to update* when stale and **Copy**, which hands over the
  facts AND the doors as absolute URLs (`_aboutCopyText`). The inline `_aboutPanel` is gone. Guards:
  `tests/js/footer_about.test.mjs` (the phone rule and the desktop base rule, the tab stamp on the
  navigation road, the dialog's three layers and lock discipline, Escape on the window, About as the
  dialog with every door the partial names, Copy's text), `tests/css/test_style_css.py` (both rules
  survive the build), `webtest/test_about_dialog.py` (desktop: footer on Community, About over the
  settings with the seven doors, selectable and themed, Escape and the scrim leave the settings;
  phone at 390px: no footer on Community, the dialog fits over the sheet, Close keeps the sheet's
  lock, the home tab keeps its footer), and `webtest/test_dm_actions.py` (the thread opens at its
  own foot; desktop's footer stays below the bar; the phone shows none).
- **The merecat ask row is the DM composer's shape, flush on the tab bar (2026-09-11, the owner's
  fifth look: "bulky, and not perfectly flush").** Two defects, one lesson. main.css fixed
  `.merecat-form` above the tab bar with `margin: 0`, and the injected `#mc-merecat-css` — appended
  to `<head>` later, equal specificity, so it wins — gave it `margin: .8rem 0 .2rem`: for a fixed
  element `bottom` places the MARGIN edge, so the bar floated .2rem above the tab bar. The same
  injected block stacked the field and a full-width Ask button in a column on phones
  (`flex-direction:column`), which is what read as bulky. Now the row is the DM composer's: one
  rounded field (`rows=1`, `q.mcGrow` to ~6 lines, shrinking back when the box is cleared after an
  ask) and a round send button with the paper-plane icon (`mcIcon('send')`), its word "Ask" shown on
  desktop only (`.merecat-ask-word`); the injected block carries `@media
  (max-width:600px){.merecat-form{flex-direction:row;align-items:flex-end;margin:0}}` so the two
  stylesheets agree where it matters (the law: an injected block must agree with the stylesheet,
  since it wins). Guards: `tests/js/merecat_composer.test.mjs` (the phone agreement — row, margin 0,
  no column anywhere; the round button; the one-row field and its shrink-back) and
  `webtest/test_merecat_composer.py` (at 390px: one fixed row with no bottom margin, flush on the
  tab bar before any scroll, the round icon-only button, a one-row field that grows and shrinks, no
  footer; on desktop the row in the flow with icon and word).
- **About pared to ✕; a swipe dismisses the keyboard; Online/Offline in the thread and on profiles
  (2026-09-11, the owner's sixth look).** (1) The About dialog keeps only ✕ (and *Reload to update*
  when stale): the Copy button and the bottom Close are gone — the selectable body is what copies,
  and ✕, the scrim and Escape dismiss; `mcDialog` no longer adds a Close of its own, and focus lands
  on the ✕. (2) **A downward swipe over the page while a composer has the keyboard up dismisses it**
  (`swipeDismissesKeyboard(ta, composer)`, comments.ts): the owner found the page scrolling under a
  keyboard that stayed. Touch only, passive, live only while the field is focused, ignoring a swipe
  that starts inside the composer, 48 px downward blurs the field once; the DM composer and the
  merecat ask box take it; the listeners carry `bootSig`. (3) **Presence**: the thread header now
  reads **● Online** / **○ Offline** once the hub has seeded the other party's state (the lock line
  stands only while unknown), and the profile card carries the same line under another member's name
  (`profilePresenceInto`: one keyed `/dm/presence` read on open, then a `presence:<hash>` sub the
  hub seeds and fans; the live frame reaches it through `state.profilePresence`) — never for
  yourself or the bot, never without an identity. A member who chose *appear offline* (Settings →
  Privacy, `Domain.Presence`) reads Offline everywhere: the hub applies `isVisible` before it
  answers, so no client can show what the member hid. Guards: `tests/js/footer_about.test.mjs`,
  `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs` (the swipe's passivity, scope and threshold; Offline in the header;
  the profile line's gates and roads), `webtest/test_about_dialog.py` (✕ the only button),
  `webtest/test_dm_actions.py` (the 390px swipe: a small move keeps the keyboard, 48 px over the
  thread blurs the field, a swipe inside the composer is left alone; the header sub may read Offline
  once seeded).
- **Sent and received bubbles read apart in every palette (2026-09-11, the owner's seventh look:
  "received messages are nigh the same color of gray as the messages I send").** They were: on dark
  phones mine sat on `--cream` (#20242a) and theirs on `--cream-2` (#262b32), and on desktop mine on
  `--cream` against theirs on `--surface` — a step of gray apiece. The ruling: distinct, sane, not
  loud, and each of the six palettes (charcoal, slate, ink; paper, mist, sepia) respected. **Mine is
  the surface tinted with the palette's own accent** — a `--bubble-mine` / `--bubble-mine-rule` pair
  each palette block defines (the accent mixed 14% into the surface and 22% into the rule in the
  light palettes: a pale rose on paper and mist, a dusty rose on sepia; 20% / 30% in the dark ones,
  where the coral accent has further to travel on a dark ground: a muted plum on charcoal and slate,
  a warm one on ink — deepened from a first 9% / 16% the same day so the pair also reads apart
  against the phone's `--cream-2`); **theirs stays on the neutral surface** (`--surface` on desktop,
  `--cream-2` on phones); the inks stay the palette's own. The first cut wrote the tint as
  `color-mix()` at the point of use, and the build showed why not: the minifier emits a fallback for
  every `color-mix` from its FIRST color, so a browser without `color-mix` (older Safari) would have
  painted the sent bubble solid maroon — the pair is precomputed into tokens instead, read as
  `var(--bubble-mine, var(--cream))`, and a palette that forgets its pair falls back to the old
  cream rather than to nothing. **The light-phone iMessage fill is retired** (the owner's tenth
  look, the same day: "a harsh red against the white… it feels intense"): light phones take the same
  tinted sent bubble, with the palette's own inks, and every white-on-fill override (meta, quote,
  ticks, the librarian's `.merecat-who`) went with it. The librarian's log, the other 1:1 chat,
  takes the same pair on phones (`.merecat-log .merecat-msg.you`). Guards:
  `tests/css/test_style_css.py` (every palette block defines its pair; the bubble rule reads the
  tokens and never `color-mix`; theirs neutral; the librarian's pair) and
  `webtest/test_dm_actions.py` (on prod, all six palettes toggled in place: mine and theirs at least
  24 RGB units apart, the ink at least 300 from the tint).
- **"Last seen …" beside Offline (2026-09-11, the owner's eighth look), honouring appear-offline by
  construction.** The header of a conversation and the presence line on a profile now read **Last
  seen just now / 3 min ago / today at 3:09 PM / yesterday at 11:30 PM / Mon at 2:15 PM / Sep 8**
  (`dmSeenLabel`, the WhatsApp ladder in the reader's own zone; repainted each minute in the thread,
  the timer dying with the boot) where they read Offline, and Offline alone where no stamp exists.
  **The stamp**: `profiles.last_seen_at` (**migration `0013_profile_last_seen.sql`**; the next is
  0014), written by the **BoardHub alone** — the one party that knows a member's presence mode,
  which rides the socket's auth frame and was never a column: `webSocketClose` stamps `now` when the
  member's LAST live socket closes and the closing socket's mode records
  (`Domain.Presence.recordsLastSeen`, the one rule: only under "auto"), and an `auth` frame under
  "off" CLEARS any stamp the member holds (`#clearLastSeen`) — the settings switch re-sends the auth
  frame at once, so the moment a member chooses appear-offline their stamp is gone and nothing stale
  can be served. The worker only reads: `/dm/thread` carries `other.last_seen` (the hub's column
  as-is — its absence IS the privacy rule) and `/dm/presence` carries `seen: {hash: epoch}` for
  stamped members who are not online now (an online member's stamp is not served; they read Online).
  A live offline seen on screen reads "just now" without a fetch. **The inbox rows carry the same
  line** (the owner's ninth look, the same day): `mc-inbox` reads `/dm/presence` for the page's
  members once its threads arrive (`kit.dmSeenLabel` on the kit; the classic fallback's rows
  follow), renders Online / Last seen … / Offline under each name (`.dm-row-pres`, styled in
  main.css since the DM block's dots are injected only on a thread), takes the live frames through
  `state.inboxPresence`, and watches the first five rows live (`sanitizeScopes` caps a socket at
  five scopes; the snapshot covers the rest). **Only an online → offline TRANSITION stamps "just
  now"**: the hub also seeds "offline" on subscribe for a member who was never online in this
  session, and the first cut let that seed overwrite the read's real stamp — the prod battery caught
  it (the row read "just now" over a fixture stamped yesterday); every presence line (thread,
  profile, both inboxes) keeps the last known state and stamps only on the transition. Guards:
  `tests/purescript/presence.test.mjs` (the rule), `tests/worker/last_seen.test.mjs` (the ledger
  through 0013; the hub as the one writer, gated at the close by the closing socket's mode, clearing
  on an off auth; no worker handler writes the column; the thread and presence shapes, the online
  exclusion), `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs` (the ladder, the stamp's arrival with the thread, "just
  now" on a live offline, the minute repaint dying with the boot, the profile line's read),
  `webtest/test_dm_actions.py` (the fixture's stamp reads "Last seen yesterday at …" once the hub
  seeds the fake hash offline; the inbox row, over a stubbed thread list and presence read, the
  same).
- **An unread inbox row draws the eye (2026-09-11).** The row carried only a bold maroon name and
  the words " ● new". Now `.board-topic.dm-row-unread` (both the Lit `mc-inbox` and the classic
  fallback) takes a 3px accent edge, a step of cream, the bold name, and a small maroon **new**
  badge in place of the words — `unread` is a flag per thread (the worker's `CASE WHEN
  dmUnreadExists … THEN 1`), not a count, so the badge says *new*. Guards:
  `tests/css/test_style_css.py` (the edge and the badge survive the build) and
  `webtest/test_dm_actions.py` (the stubbed inbox's row: the edge, the badge, the old word gone).
- **Wave F: the classic client is feature modules (2026-09-11, the owner's ruling: "finish
  modular").** `client/comments.ts` had grown to 12,584 lines because it was ONE function —
  `mcBoot()`, re-run on every soft navigation — and every helper inside it was a closure over the
  same per-boot state, so nothing could leave without deciding how modules share that state (the
  passage above, *The Lit interior campaign*, kept the write paths there on purpose). The read views
  had already answered the question: `app/views/*.ts` take the per-boot `kit`. The same idea,
  applied to the rest, with a mechanical, verified split rather than a rewrite. **The shape**:
  `client/comments.ts` is the ROOT (the pre-boot page state, the core helpers — fetch/read pacing,
  formatting, `el`, the Turnstile road, identity primitives — the router, `start()`, and the `mcKit`
  assembly; 2,049 lines) and seven feature modules — `composer.ts` (the markdown editor, emoji,
  Scripture, drafts, media gate + compression + voice, mentions, the stash), `profile.ts` (identity,
  faith, mute/block, prefs, the profile card and editor, avatars, notifications), `board.ts` (the
  forum views, the comment renderer, quoting, editing, the board form, the journal, search, the post
  menu), `wall.ts` (feed + walls), `dm.ts` (E2E crypto and media, the bubbles, the surface, the chat
  screen, the inbox, presence, the live DM frames, calls), `merecat.ts` (the librarian's chat
  client), `admin.ts` (the acting consoles) — each `export function install<Feature>(B: Boot)`; 844
  to 2,304 lines apiece, 13,529 in all against the 12,584 they replace (the difference is the
  bindings, `bind`/`run` and the export lists — no body changed). **The mechanism**: a module's body
  is the old boot's text VERBATIM (every statement moved with its leading comments); the names it
  takes from elsewhere are `let` bindings at its top, filled by its `bind()` from the boot object
  `B` after every module is installed, so install order never matters and every body reads as it
  did; what ran at the boot's top level (listeners, the emoji tables, the `mc-live` dispatch) runs
  in its `run()` after all binding, in the old order; it returns `{ bind, run, exports }`. The root
  builds `B` from its own helpers, installs the seven, lands their exports on `B`, binds its own
  late names, binds and runs the modules, then assembles the kit and starts. The six pieces of state
  more than one module WRITES (`quotedSelection`, `mentionDir`, `notifBadgeT`, `profileWaiters`,
  `COMMENTS_KEY`, `COMMENTS_HREF`) live on `B` outright — a copied binding would have diverged.
  `client/boot.ts` types `Boot` as a bag on purpose: the bodies did not change. **How it was done,
  and what it found**: TypeScript 7 here is the native binary with no compiler API, so a one-off
  tool stripped the types with esbuild (source map kept), parsed the JavaScript with acorn, mapped
  each of the 425 top-level statements back to its TypeScript lines, and computed per statement the
  boot names it references and assigns; a curated map assigned each to a module; the generator
  emitted the files; `tsc` was the safety net (every unbound name is an error). Three things the
  tool caught that a hand split would have shipped: a module-level `var LAME_SRC = asset(...)` would
  have called a page-scoped helper before binding (initializers touching a boot or page-scoped name
  are deferred to `run()`); the root's `state` initializer calls `getKey()` at boot init, so the
  identity primitives stay in the root; and the callbacks typed through helpers (`.then(function (r)
  …)` on `fetchRetry`) lost their inference when the helper became an `any` binding — the bindings
  carry each function's own signature now (`let fetchRetry: (url: string, …) => Promise<Response>`),
  and thirteen async helpers gained explicit return types (type-only, inline, line count preserved).
  **The build**: `build:client` bundles (`--bundle --format=iife`; 349 KB against 334 unbundled —
  the module scaffolding). **The tests**: the source-rule tests read the client through
  `tests/_support/client.mjs` (`clientRoot`, `clientModule`, `clientAll`); the boot-listener sweep
  (`tests/js/dmblobs.test.mjs`) de-indents each module's `run()` and reads it with the boot-level
  shape, so a listener without `bootSig` fails exactly as before; the DM, About and merecat
  batteries pass against the split build locally, and the full `webtest/` suite runs against prod
  after the deploy against a baseline taken before it (the pre-existing failures in
  `test_admin_reads`/`test_post` are the read-only test key, per memory). Gates: `make jscheck`, a
  byte-deterministic double bundle, 376 unit tests.
- **A hold picks a message, never a word (2026-09-11).** The owner's screenshots: a press-and-hold
  on a DM bubble opened the reaction bar and the menu as designed, and iOS ALSO showed its
  text-selection callout (Copy · Look Up · Translate) over the bubble, then left selection handles
  in the header's presence line and blue selection bands across the composer's spacer and behind the
  menu. The bubble already carried `-webkit-user-select:none` + `-webkit-touch-callout:none` under
  `(hover:none)` — and that is the finding: **WebKit starts a long-press selection anyway and
  anchors it in the NEAREST selectable text** when the touched element is not selectable (the
  presence line, a day chip, the spacer, the app bar's title), then extends it while the finger
  stays down; the rule on the bubble alone only moved the anchor next door. The fix follows
  WhatsApp: on a phone the conversation is not a text document. `viewDm` stamps `dm-screen` on its
  section (and takes it away on the boot signal, so the next view on that section is a document
  again); under `(hover:none)` `.dm-screen` is not selectable text and only its `textarea`/`input`
  are; the action surface (`.dm-act`) is never selectable on any device; the phone chrome
  (`.mc-appbar`, `mc-tabbar`, main.css) is not either; and the phone surface calls
  `dmClearSelection()` as it opens, dropping whatever selection the hold managed to start before it
  matured. Copy stays on the menu, which is where WhatsApp keeps it. Desktop keeps text selection
  everywhere but the surface. Guards: `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs` (the injected rules, the stamp
  and its removal, the clear), `tests/css/test_style_css.py` (the chrome rule in the build), and the
  DM battery's phone flow (computed `user-select` on the bubble body, the presence line, the day
  chip, the spacer, the app bar, the tab bar and the menu; the field stays `text`; a scripted
  selection made before the hold is gone once the surface stands) — headless Chromium cannot
  reproduce iOS's anchoring itself, so the battery proves the rules and the clear, and the phone
  proves the outcome.
- **Reading back is never interrupted, and never blind (2026-09-11).** The owner's next ask, after
  the hold: typing must be obvious even while reading scrollback, there must always be a way back to
  the foot, a word that arrives while reading back must be obviously unread — "basic chat app
  features that WhatsApp, Snapchat, Instagram, Facebook all have; what else are we missing?" The
  audit first, against what those four share: typing (we had it — the header's line, and the
  keystroke signal over the socket), read receipts (✓ → ✓✓ on `dm-read`), presence and last seen,
  reactions, reply, edit, delete, copy, media and voice, day chips, drafts, Enter-to-send with a
  keyboard, the tab badge, per-thread mute and block, an info sheet, calls — all there. What was
  not: the way back, the count, the line, and typing where a scrolled-back reader would see it; and
  the inbox badge said "new" where every one of them shows a number. **The thread now.** A `scroll`
  listener (one, rAF-throttled, on the boot signal) judges `nearEnd()`; whenever the foot is out of
  view a round `.dm-jump` button stands above the composer — hung from the FIXED composer, so it
  rides the keyboard — and a tap rides down smoothly. A live word that lands while the reader is at
  the foot scrolls into view as before; one that lands while they are reading back stays put, an "N
  unread messages" line (`.dm-unread-line`) goes above the first waiting word (the count bumps on
  the line and on the button's badge for the next), and the reader is NOT moved. "Seen" is honest
  now: the ping went out for every live word before, watched or not; it goes out for a word that
  arrived at the foot, and otherwise once, when the reader comes down to the waiting words
  (`updateJump` at the foot: `unseenLive` → one `dmSeenPing`). On open, the server tells what was
  unread BEFORE the open marked it read — `handleDmThread` reads the viewer's stamp from the thread
  row it already fetched and answers `unread` (the count) and `unread_from` (the first unread id)
  ahead of its own UPDATE — and the client stands the line above that word with the count on the
  button; the landing is WhatsApp's: the foot when the unread words all fit under the header, else
  the line just under it (`landing()`, also what the post-load re-landing calls). Typing paints the
  header's line as before AND a three-dot bubble (`.dm-typing-bubble`, three `.dm-typing-dot`s) at
  the foot, kept in view when the reader is there, gone on stop, on the 6 s silence, or on the word
  itself. **The inbox.** `unread` on `/dm/threads` is the COUNT (the new `dmUnreadCount(now)`
  fragment beside `dmUnreadExists`; `unread_total` still counts threads, so the tab badge did not
  change meaning); both inboxes show the number. Both inboxes hear the `typing` frame — the hub fans
  it to my own scope — and the row reads "typing…" with the dot lit for 6 s, then the presence line
  again (`state.inboxTyping` classic; `_typing` on McInbox). **Privacy.** The hub now drops a typing
  signal from a socket whose presence mode is "off": `Domain.Presence.isVisible(mode)(true)` — the
  rule that hides their socket hides their keystrokes; nothing in the client had to know. Guards:
  `tests/js/dm_chat_basics.test.mjs` (the source rules above), `tests/worker/dm_unread.test.mjs`
  (the counting fragment and the thread's pre-open query RUN on the real ledger with held, expired,
  cleared and own words seeded; the handlers' shapes; the hub's gate), and the DM battery's third
  flow at 390 px over the `?mcunread=1` fixture (nine unread words: the line above id 105 just under
  the header, the button with 9, no seen ping; the ride down; a live frame while scrolled to the
  top: appended, the reader unmoved, a fresh "1 unread message" line, the badge 1, still no ping;
  the ride down: ONE ping; typing: the header word and the three-dot bubble in view, both gone on
  stop) and the inbox's typing row and counted badge. Still deliberately absent: forward (the
  owner's ruling), search within a conversation, link previews — none of the four share them all.
- **The tab badge counts words, and the bar has no hero (2026-09-11).** Two asks in one breath: "the
  inbox button at the bottom should also indicate if there is an unread(s) and how many total across
  all", and "redesign the community button to be the same size as the other buttons in the bottom
  bar menu". **The badge.** The Inbox tab always carried a number — it was a count of THREADS
  (`handleDmUnread`'s `COUNT(*) … dmUnreadExists`), which the previous wave deliberately left alone
  even as the inbox rows started showing per-thread word counts. So a reader with twelve messages
  waiting in three conversations read "3" on the tab and `4 · 5 · 3` on the rows: the tab did not
  add up to what it opened onto. Now every unread number on the site comes from ONE fragment,
  `dmUnreadCount(now)`: `handleDmUnread` answers `COALESCE(SUM(<fragment>), 0)` over the viewer's
  threads, and `/dm/threads`'s `unread_total` (the same number, since the inbox refreshes the badge
  cache from its own response — `dmCacheSet(d.unread_total)` in both the classic inbox and
  `mc-inbox`) became `COALESCE(SUM(unread), 0)` instead of a tally of threads with something unread.
  The two roads now agree by construction, which is the whole point: they feed the same
  `mc-dm-unread` cache, and a disagreement would have made the badge flicker between two numbers as
  a reader moved between pages. `dmUnreadExists` had no caller left and is DELETED from `lib.ts` —
  the one-fragment rule is only true while there is one fragment. Held, expired, cleared and own
  words never count, as before, and the 90-second cache, the live-push tick and `badgeText`'s `99+`
  cap are untouched. The badge is also spoken now: a tab carrying one is labelled "Inbox, 7 unread
  messages" (`badgeLabel`), on the phone bar and the desktop rail alike — a red disc says nothing to
  a screen reader. **The bar.** Community was the raised circular hero at slot 4. A six-item bar
  cannot optically centre a hero — the lifted disc read as an accent that had slipped sideways,
  which is what the owner was seeing — so `mc-tab-hero` is gone from the markup and the stylesheet:
  six identical tabs, one 25px icon over one label each, colour alone saying which is lit. Measuring
  the flat bar at 390/375/360/320 px surfaced a real bug the hero had been hiding: `.mc-tab { flex:
  1 }` keeps the default `min-width: auto`, so "Community" (61px at 0.66rem) refused to shrink and
  ate its neighbours' width — 61px against 49px on a 320px device, the bar visibly crooked. Fixed
  with `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0` and a viewport-scaled label (`clamp(0.54rem, 2.6vw, 0.66rem)`,
  nowrap, ellipsis rather than a second line that would break the bar's height): every slot is now
  exactly equal at every width tested, and the longest label still fits inside its slot at a 305px
  viewport. Guards: `tests/worker/dm_unread.test.mjs` runs the tab badge's own query — lifted from
  `handleDmUnread`'s source — against the real ledger over two conversations (four words, not "two
  threads"; reading one leaves the other's two), locks `unread_total` to the summed form, and locks
  `dmUnreadExists` out of the tree entirely.

- **Every post opens the one surface: reactions on the board and the feed, and the bell that says
  "reacted" (2026-09-12).** The owner's ask: "Not only DMs should be react-able by press and hold
  ... likewise community posts and feed posts. And all of this should include more than just react
  but eg a way to edit if the post is yours ... you can react to your own post too ... streamline
  our system. Moreover when someone reacts to you it should send a notification ... just say foo
  person 'reacted' to your message ... and clicking takes you to the message." What stood: the DM's
  surface (2026-09-10) bound to the bubble; on the board a ⋯ `postMenu` (a desktop pop, a phone
  sheet) and no reactions at all; on the feed a ❤️ Like with its own two tables (`wall_likes`,
  `wall_comment_likes`), a likers popover and a `wall-like` bell; and three inline copies of the
  notification label map that had already drifted once. **The kernel.** The reaction grammar moved
  out of `Domain.Dm` into **`Domain.Reaction`** (`normalizeReaction`, `quickReactions`, plus
  `targets` = `post` · `wall` · `wallc` and `notifKind`), re-exported by `Domain.Dm` so every DM
  reader still finds it; **`Domain.Notif`** holds the kinds the ledger's CHECK admits and, for each,
  `label` (the sentence) and `href` (the door) and `hasSnippet` — the classic list, the member page
  and the bell sheet all render through `mcCore.notifLabel`/`notifHref` now, and a fourth inline
  copy for the reaction kinds was exactly the moment to make it one rule. 31 modules. **The
  worker.** Migration **0014**: the `reactions` ledger (one per member per target, PK on the three,
  an author index for the viewer's batched "mine") backfilled from both like tables as the ❤️
  reaction (U+2764 U+FE0F, the way 0012 carried the DM heart; the like tables are frozen and read by
  nothing), and the notifications table swapped for the CHECK widened with `react`, `wall-react`,
  `dm-react`. ONE handler `handleReact` for every target (`{key, target, id, emoji}`; `''`
  withdraws; `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` replaces): visibility is the target's own — a live row, a
  back-room post only to an admin (else the missing-post 404), the wall's targets behind the social
  switch — and the tally goes out live as `{t:'react', target, id, reacts}` over the target's scope
  (`topic:<id>`, `feed:global`) so every open page repaints. `/reacts` gives a keyed viewer their
  own over a batch (the board's payloads are cached, so `react_me` is `''` there and rides this
  after); `/react/who` names the reactors with their emoji (muted hidden; a back-room post answers
  the empty list). `stampReactions` puts `reacts`/`react_me` (and the old `likes`/`liked`, derived)
  on every served post row — `topicViewPayload`, `handleGet`, `wallEnrich` — in two batched reads.
  The three like roads are aliases of the two handlers for one deploy. `notifyReact` generalises the
  wall like's coalescing to every kind: one row per (recipient, kind, actor, thread, post), reopened
  after read, never minted while one is unread, the public ledger's writes gated by `EXISTS
  reactions` (the react/withdraw race), a withdraw retracting an unheard bell; never for your own
  post, the bot, or from a muted reactor; a DM reaction rings `dm-react` only for THEIR message. The
  list handler joins each family to its own tables (`NOTIF_POST_KINDS` → `comments`,
  `NOTIF_WALL_KINDS` → `wall_posts` and, for a feed comment, `wall_comments`) — before, a `dm-react`
  message id would have landed on a forum post sharing the number; `notifHideWall` and the orphan
  sweeps read those lists. `/dm/thread` takes `find=<id>` and places the page (the topic view's
  idiom) for the bell that lands on a message. **The client.** A new feature module
  **`client/surface.ts`** (`installSurface`, the eighth): `openActs(spec)` is the DM's overlay
  verbatim — the bar, the hole between four pieces of scrim, the menu, the picker, the phone placing
  and lock, the desktop popover — told what to show: `react {current, onPick}` or nothing, `items`
  as `{label, icon, fn}` specs OR the caller's own elements, which travel into the menu and go HOME
  on close (the ⋯'s pop keeps its eager children, so the DOM contract the standing webtests assert
  holds); `armHold(node, open, opts)` the gestures — a nested armed node (a feed comment in a feed
  post) takes the hold itself, a control or a pill never is one, a right-click and the hover ⌄ only
  where asked (the DM); and the LEDGER: `reactRegister` mounts a target's painters, `reactSend` goes
  optimistic and settles from the answer (validated by `mcCore.reaction` first), `reactLoadMine` the
  keyed read after a cached payload, the live `react` frame repaints without touching the viewer's
  own. The DM keeps a thin `dmOpenActions` (its acts) and `dmArmGestures` (its swipe). `postMenu`
  now OPENS the surface — the ⋯ click and the hold it arms on `opts.hold` — with the bar for a keyed
  reader and the pill at the post's end unless the caller paints its own; both post renderers
  (classic and Lit) pass `hold: article, react: {target:'post', id, seed}`, and a keyed reader gets
  the ⋯ even on a post with no acts. The feed: the action bar's summary is the pill of chips (a tap
  gives that reaction, a hover or a long press says who), Like is the ❤️ (mine again withdraws), a
  hold on Like or on the card opens the whole bar; a feed comment carries `id=wc-<id>` and the
  detail page lands on it. A DM bell lands on the message (`?m=` → `find=`, scrolled to and
  flashed). Under `(hover:none)` `.comment` is not selectable text (its fields are) — the DM's
  2026-09-11 lesson, applied: a hold picks a post, never a word; Copy is on the surface. The
  surface's classes keep their `dm-act` prefix: the prod webtest's selectors are unchanged.
  **Deliberately not:** no bell for a reaction on your own word or post; no re-inlined regex
  anywhere; no column for the DM landing (`find` is a read parameter). Guards:
  `tests/purescript/reaction.test.mjs` and `notif.test.mjs` (the re-export is the same function; the
  kind list is the newest CHECK, verbatim; every kind has a sentence; every door),
  `tests/js/core.test.mjs` (the membrane's coercions), `tests/worker/reactions.test.mjs` (0014
  builds and backfills on a pre-0014 ledger; the bell's two statements, evaluated as the worker
  builds them, run on the real ledger — rings once, quiet while unread, reopens after read, needs
  the reaction to stand; the handlers' visibility rules; the list's joins; the sweeps),
  `tests/js/surface.test.mjs` (one overlay root in the whole client; both renderers arm the hold
  with the reaction; the acts go home; the nearest armed node; the kernel before the wire; the
  selection rule), `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs` re-pointed at the shared functions,
  `tests/css/test_style_css.py` (the built `.comment` rule, merged by the build into the chrome's
  selector list). Proven headless against a local serve over a fetch stub: the ⋯ on a board post
  opens the phone surface — four scrims, the six-cell bar, the acts travelled in — and closing
  returns every act to its pop.

- **Admins edit any post (2026-09-12).** The owner's check after the surface shipped: admins must
  keep the ability to edit or delete other members' posts, not only their own. Delete already was
  own-or-admin on both the board and the feed (`Domain.Access.canDelete` / `Domain.Wall.canDelete`,
  server-enforced); edit was own-only everywhere — `handleEdit` and `handleWallEdit` selected the
  row `WHERE author_hash = me` and answered "Not yours" to an admin, and the three renderers offered
  the act on `author === me`. Now `Domain.Access.canEdit author me isAdmin` is own-or-admin (never
  keyless), `mcCore.canEdit(author, me, isAdmin)` erases it, both renderers and the feed offer the
  act through it (`canEditPost` in the classic client), and both handlers select the row by id and
  admit the editor when they are its author OR an admin (`isAdminHash`), logging `admin_post_edit`
  to the tail the way an admin profile edit is — the middle ground between doing nothing and
  deleting. The refusal string is unchanged for everyone else. Guards:
  `tests/purescript/access.test.mjs`, `tests/js/core.test.mjs`, `tests/worker/admin_edit.test.mjs`
  (the two handlers' admin door and their log line).

- **DMs are never AI-screened (2026-09-12, locked).** The owner's check: no AI scanning of DMs —
  they are end-to-end encrypted and privacy-strong; Turnstile is fine. The audit found it already
  so, and had been since the E2E work (2026-07-31: "DMs are not AI-screened — none ever were"):
  `handleDmSend`, `handleDmEdit`, `sendSystemDm`, `handleDmMediaUpload`/`Get` carry no
  `screen(`/`screenImage(` — the server holds only ciphertext and COULD not read it — and the
  worker's screen calls sit in `handlePost`, `handleEdit`, the profile save, the three wall writes,
  the wall/board `mediaUpload` (a per-section toggle that knows no `dm` context) and the avatar.
  What was missing was a guard: `tests/worker/dm_privacy.test.mjs` finds every top-level handler
  whose name says DM and asserts none screens, that every screen caller is a non-DM handler, that
  the DM upload stores opaque ciphertext, and that Turnstile stays on the send. The rule is in
  CLAUDE.md now.

- **A profile picture pops out full size (2026-09-12).** The owner's ask: a click on the picture on
  a profile page should pop it out and show it as large as the viewport allows. The feed's media
  theater already did that for a post's media and, bare, for a comment's (`openMedia(mediaKey, kind,
  post)` in `client/composer.ts`) — but it took a media KEY. It is `openLightbox(src, kind, post,
  filename)` now, with `openMedia` and a new `openImage(src, filename)` as its two doors, and
  `renderProfile` (the one renderer both the classic page and the Lit view draw through) makes the
  avatar a button (`role=button`, focusable, Enter/Space, `cursor: zoom-in`) that opens the very URL
  the head shows in the bare theater: the image capped by the stage (`.wall-lb-media` max 100%/100%;
  on a phone the bare stage's 60vh), a Download beside it (`avatar-<hash8>.jpg`), and the theater's
  five ways out (scrim, ✕, Esc, back, a navigation). Guard: `tests/js/profile_avatar.test.mjs`.
  Proven headless over a profile stub at 390 and 1280 px: the tap opens the bare theater on the same
  src with the download, and ✕ closes it.

- **The keyboard shackle (2026-09-12).** The owner's law: "when we tap an input box — merecat, a DM,
  a community post, whatever — the input box is always purposefully controlled to be 100% visible
  and directly above the keyboard, so the user always sees what he is inputting ... this applies to
  reply or edit buttons as well; it all needs to be controlled and shackled so that we have a
  consistent typing experience app-wide." What stood: the shell computed `--mc-kb` (`innerHeight −
  visualViewport.height − offsetTop`, the keyboard's overlap in layout coordinates, published on
  every visualViewport resize/scroll) and `body.mc-kb-open`, and exactly two things used it — the DM
  composer and the merecat row, both `position: fixed` with `bottom: var(--mc-kb)`. Everything else
  — a topic's reply box, the edit box a tap opens mid-page, the topic composer, a profile field, the
  report prompt in the sheet, the comment box in the theater's rail — was left to the browser's own
  focus scroll, which runs BEFORE the keyboard stands (the box lands under it), never runs for a box
  that grows while typing, cannot scroll a locked document (a sheet is open), and cannot reach a
  fixed container's lower part that sits behind the keyboard. Those are the blind boxes. **Now**, in
  the shell (it must outlive every boot; a document listener inside `mcBoot` would leak per soft
  navigation): `kbAlign(el)` places a field into the visible region — the visual viewport under the
  fixed top bar (`.mc-appbar`/`.mc-deskbar` foot) — by scrolling every scrollable ancestor first
  (the sheet, the theater's rail) and then the document, unless a fixed ancestor holds the field (a
  fixed composer: the document's scroll could never move it and would only jump the page behind).
  The move is idempotent and moves nothing already wholly visible: foot onto the keyboard's top when
  hidden below, head under the bar when hidden above, and a field taller than the room shows the
  half the caret is in (one rule, so a keystroke never bounces it between head and foot). When the
  document ends short of the room a field needs (a reply box at the page's foot), a shell-owned
  spacer (`.mc-kb-room`) gives it exactly the missing height and goes when the keyboard falls. It
  runs on `focusin` through a settle ladder (0 · 120 · 300 · 520 · 800 ms — the keyboard takes ~300
  ms to rise), on every visualViewport `resize` (the keyboard rose, fell, or changed to a taller
  emoji keyboard), and on every `input` (autosize growth; typing under the keyboard). The sheet is
  lifted onto the keyboard (`body.mc-kb-open .mc-sheet { bottom: var(--mc-kb); max-height:
  calc(100dvh − kb − 3rem) }` — dvh is the layout viewport, which the keyboard never shrinks; the
  body is locked while a sheet is open, so nothing else could have reached its lower part) and so is
  the theater on phones (`body.mc-app.mc-kb-open .wall-lightbox { bottom: var(--mc-kb) }`; the tab
  bar it sat on has slid away). `window.mcKeyboard = { region, align, inset, isField }` exists for
  the headless proof, which cannot raise a keyboard: it passes an explicit region. Not done,
  deliberately: the body's tab-bar reservation is NOT swapped for a keyboard reservation while the
  keyboard stands — the DM's landing is tuned to it, and the spacer gives room only where a field
  actually falls short. Guards: `tests/js/keyboard.test.mjs` (the net is the shell's and the boot
  never re-implements it; the three triggers; scrollers-then-document, never past a fixed ancestor;
  the caret-half rule; the lifts). Proven headless over the topic stub at 390 px with a pretend
  keyboard covering the lower 45 %: the reply box at the page's foot is placed with its foot on the
  keyboard (the spacer supplying the 4 px the page lacked), a second placing moves nothing, an edit
  box opened under the keyboard mid-page is placed onto it, a field in a fixed box is never
  document-scrolled; and the report sheet, with `--mc-kb` at 300 px, stands on the keyboard with its
  textarea above it.

- **The keyboard shackle, second look: the region minus the site's own chrome, and the browser's
  late scroll answered (2026-09-12).** The owner's screenshot an hour after the first cut: scrolled
  up in a DM, hold → Edit — the composer riding the keyboard correctly, the edit textarea under
  iOS's floating accessory pill, visible only through its translucency. Two defects, both real
  whatever the timing. (1) The region ended at the keyboard's top, and on a DM page the fixed
  composer stands exactly there: "foot on the keyboard" placed an in-flow field BEHIND the composer.
  The region now takes the site's own chrome off both edges by their rects — the fixed top bar and
  the DM's sticky header at the head; at the foot every fixed bar touching the visual bottom
  (`.mc-tabbar`, `.dm-composer`, `.merecat-form`, `.mc-dock`), in two passes so a bar standing on a
  bar (the composer on the tab bar) is taken off too; a tab bar slid away under the keyboard is
  below the visual bottom and drops out by itself. (2) iOS scrolls a focused field into view itself
  once the keyboard has risen and, on 17+, lands it under the floating accessory — after the
  ladder's last tick on a slow first rise, so nothing re-placed it. Now any scroll (document or
  visualViewport) inside a 1.5 s settle window after a focus or a keyboard change re-places the
  field once the scroll settles (80 ms); our own placing re-checks to nothing; outside the window
  the reader's scrolling is theirs. The ladder gained a 1200 ms tick. Proven headless over the DM
  stub at 390 px on the exact path — a real touch hold on my bubble, the Edit act: the region ends
  at the composer's top and starts under the header; a scroll that hides the box behind the composer
  inside the window is answered; with `--mc-kb` at 300 px the box is placed above the composer's new
  top; a second placing moves nothing.

- **The keyboard shackle, third look: the editor is what is placed, and a DM is edited in the
  composer (2026-09-12).** The owner's second screenshot, on the fixed build: the edit textarea now
  above the composer — and the editor's Save/Cancel row under it, the composer eating 120 px of the
  room while a message was being edited. Two changes. (1) The net places a field's EDITOR when that
  fits the room — `KB_GROUPS` = `.dm-edit-box`, `.comment-editor`, `.wall-edit-box`,
  `.comment-form`, `.mc-confirm`, `[data-mc-kb-group]`: the box that holds the field with its
  Save/Post row, found by `closest()` — and the field alone (by the caret rule) when the editor is
  taller than the room; so a reply box shows its Post, an edit box its Save, the report prompt its
  Send. (2) A DM is edited IN the composer, WhatsApp's way: the surface's Edit hands the message to
  `ctx.edit`, which loads its text into the field under an "✎ Editing message" strip (the reply
  strip's shape, `dm-edit-bar`, gold edge), turns Send into a ✓ (`mcIcon('check')`, new), hides the
  + (an edit changes words, never media), and keeps Send standing while the field is emptied; ✓
  saves through `dmSaveEdit` — the one routine, lifted out of the old box's handler, that re-wraps
  the quote the message answered, seals, posts `/dm/edit`, re-renders the bubble and marks it edited
  — ✕ or Escape gives the field its earlier draft back, an unchanged text just ends the edit, and
  one thing at a time: a Reply ends an edit, an edit disarms a reply. The composer is fixed on the
  keyboard by construction, so the edit can never hide behind anything; and the thread stays a chat
  screen ("everything that is not a message lives in the ⓘ sheet" — an editor in a bubble was
  against that law too). The in-bubble box (`dmStartEdit`'s second half) stands only for a thread
  with no composer to edit in. Guards: `tests/js/keyboard.test.mjs` (the group rule),
  `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs` (the composer road: the strip, the ✓, the one routine, the ✕,
  one-thing-at-a-time). Proven headless over the DM stub at 390 px: hold → Edit loads the composer
  (no box in the bubble, the strip, the text, the ✓, the + gone); ✓ saves (the bubble re-renders
  with "edited", the strip goes, the field empties, `/dm/edit` was written); and the kept in-bubble
  box, exercised directly, is placed WHOLE above the composer's new top under the pretend keyboard.

- **The ring that reaches a closed app, and the miss recorded once (2026-09-12).** The owner: "if my
  app is installed it should give the user the opportunity to swipe open and answer the call like
  all the modern apps do; it should only notify the phone if there is a missed call, and the missed
  call should be in the DM history; currently it only works if both users have the app open." The
  diagnosis was right in substance. The engine (`app/call.ts`) rings on any page WHILE the member's
  live socket is open; a backgrounded or locked PWA has no socket, and what fired then was a push
  that could not be answered — the SDP offer lived only on the socket, the caller's ICE candidates
  had gone out to nobody, and the 'call' bell row was written at the OFFER (so an answered call left
  a read row behind, and "missed" was a guess). **Now.** (1) The offer is STORED for the ring:
  migration **0015** `calls_pending` (call PK, from, to, sdp, created_at, answered_at, missed_at — a
  day's rows, swept; not a call log). (2) A callee with no live socket is rung by a push
  (`ringCall`, the old `notifyCall` minus its bell): `{kind:'call', tag:'call:<from>'}`, URL
  `messages.html?dm=<from>&call=<id>`; the service worker draws it standing (`requireInteraction`),
  buzzing like a phone, with an **Answer** action where the platform draws actions (Android; iOS
  shows the body and opens on a tap — the tap IS the swipe; no CallKit exists for the web, so "swipe
  to answer" is the system notification opening the app). The engine's `wake()` reads `?call=` on
  install, asks `/call/pending` (the callee's own row, fresh — the ring + 15 s — untaken, not
  missed) and rings the SAME answerable panel a live offer does, marked `late`; `/call/answer
  {late:1}` makes the caller re-send every ICE candidate it gathered (`CALL.iceAll` — they went out
  to no socket the first time; a duplicate is a no-op for the other side). Taken on another device
  meanwhile: "Answered on another device"; missed already: the thread's line is the record. The ring
  is **45 s** now (`Domain.Call.ringTimeoutSecs`, 30 before): a pushed callee needs the seconds to
  unlock, open and answer. (3) The miss is recorded ONCE: the caller's engine reports the outcome
  (`/call/end` — `noanswer` from its ring timer, `canceled` from its Cancel;
  `declined`/`hangup`/`failed` otherwise, from either side), and `recordMissedCall`'s one UPDATE
  (`missed_at`, where unanswered and unmissed) is the lock — the thread's line (`sendSystemDm(from,
  to, 'call:missed', {quiet})`, a system word the client draws by side as "Voice call · No answer" /
  "Missed voice call", a centred pill with no surface and no bell of its own), the coalesced 'call'
  bell (`notifyMissedCall`, the same SQL as before, rung only here), and a `{kind:'call-missed'}`
  push replacing the ring's by its tag when the callee is away. The hourly `sweepCalls` is the
  backstop (a caller whose app died mid-ring): two minutes on, unanswered and unmissed → the line
  and the bell, no hour-late push; rows a day old go. Every other end stamps `answered_at` so the
  backstop never counts a call that ended in front of both. What a PWA still cannot do, said
  plainly: ring the lock screen with a native call UI, answer without opening the app, or keep a
  call alive through an iOS lock (the Active bar's honesty line stands). Guards:
  `tests/worker/call.test.mjs` (0015; the store before the fan-out; no bell at the offer; the miss's
  UPDATE run twice on the real ledger — once; the pending read's callee/freshness; the end's
  caller-only miss; the quiet line; the sweep in the chain), `tests/js/calls.test.mjs` (the wake,
  the late re-flush, the report by reason, the service worker's ring and its Answer, the thread's
  line), `tests/purescript/call.test.mjs` (45 s). Proven headless over the DM stub: a thread opened
  with `?call=<id>` and a stubbed pending offer rings the Incoming panel; Decline reports
  `declined`; a stubbed `call:missed` word draws the line by side.

- **The call log: every call leaves one event line (2026-09-13).** The owner: "Calls should state in
  the DM messages, in some muted font, call missed at x time from so-and-so, or call was answered
  and lasted x hours or minutes on such-and-such date — an event log in the chat that expires; all
  major chat and calling apps do this, follow their precedent." Until now only a miss left a word
  (`call:missed`, 2026-09-12); an answered call, a decline, a busy line vanished without a trace.
  **The grammar is `Domain.Call`'s** (`CallLine`): `call:missed` (no answer, canceled while ringing,
  or busy), `call:declined` (the callee's press), `call:answered:<secs>` (connected, and for how
  long); `parseCallLine` reads it back, `callLineText mine` gives each side its sentence,
  `callLineMissedFor` says whether it is a miss for THIS reader, `durationLabel` speaks "45 sec" /
  "12 min" / "1 hr 5 min", `endReasons` is what `/call/end` accepts, and `callOutcome {caller,
  answered, reason}` is the server's one rule for what a report records (the caller's
  noanswer/canceled/busy → missed; either side's declined → declined; any end after the answer →
  answered; an unanswered break-up → failed, stamped and unwritten; a callee's "cancel" → nothing).
  The membrane erases it (`callLine`, `callLineText`, `callLineMissed`, `callDuration`,
  `callEndReasons`); the client (`client/dm.ts` `dmCallLine`) draws the line through it — no
  sentence inlined, the test sweeps for one. **The precedent followed**: WhatsApp's caller-side
  "Voice call · No answer", Signal's callee-side "Missed voice call" in the missed tint, an answered
  call naming its direction and length on both sides ("Outgoing voice call · 12 min"), the whole
  line muted (`--faint`), a ↗/↙ on the receiver, the time beside it under the day chip. A decline
  reads "No answer" to the CALLER: the callee's decline is their private act, the same
  indistinguishability a block or the calls-off switch enjoys — the callee alone sees "Declined
  voice call". A failed setup writes nothing — no app logs "couldn't connect" into the chat; the
  caller's panel already said so. **Recorded once**: migration **0017** adds `ended_at` and
  `outcome` to `calls_pending`; `recordCallEnd`'s one UPDATE (`ended_at` where `ended_at IS NULL AND
  missed_at IS NULL`, plus `answered_at IS NOT NULL` for an answered line — a miss also stamps
  `missed_at`, so a pre-0017 miss is never re-recorded) is the lock, whichever party's `/call/end`
  lands first or the hourly sweep after it (`answered_at IS NULL AND missed_at IS NULL AND ended_at
  IS NULL`, two minutes on). The line is `sendSystemDm(from, to, line, {quiet})` — from the caller,
  so "mine" on the client means "I placed it" — expiring with the conversation like any word (the
