  backstop at insert, the thread's TTL once opened); only a miss goes on to `notifyMissedCall` (the
  coalesced bell, the push). An answered call's length is the server's measure, `ended_at −
  answered_at` — the SDP answer to the first hang-up it heard, a few seconds over the connected time
  and the same on both sides. The engine (`app/call.ts` `end`) reports `busy` for a busy line now
  (it was `declined`), `hangup` only from Active or a hang-up during Connecting, `failed` for the
  setup watchdog; the callee's decline reports `declined` as before. An answered call whose end
  nobody reported (both apps died) is left unwritten — a guessed length is worse than none. **The
  caller sees it land** (2026-09-14; the owner: "still not seeing any call attempt history after I
  call and cancel" — the row and the line were there, the screen never heard): `sendSystemDmLine`
  publishes the frame to the ACTOR's own sockets as well as the other members' (a system line has no
  local echo), and the thread's `append` places an own enc-2 word where it drops its own echoed
  messages — no receipt, no unread count, never "seen". **One grammar reads a call's word**
  (2026-09-14; the owner, the caller: "for me it says missed voice call"): the member model's
  `Domain.Dm.parseSysLine` had learned `call:missed` too (`SysMissedCall`, a side-blind "Missed
  voice call") and its branch ran first in `dmRenderMsg` — a second reader of the same word, the
  trap the rulebook names. It is gone: `parseSysLine` reads only `sys:*`, the renderer asks
  `callLine` before `dmSysLine`, and the tests pin both. Guards: `tests/purescript/call.test.mjs`
  (the round-trip, the sentences by side, the durations, the outcome table),
  `tests/worker/call.test.mjs` (0017; the lock's SQL verbatim and run on the real ledger — once, the
  first word wins, an unanswered call is never an answered line, an old miss never re-recorded; the
  reasons and the rule are the kernel's; the end never forges an answer; the sweep skips an ended
  call), `tests/js/calls.test.mjs` (the report by reason; the client's line through the membrane,
  muted, tinted).

- **Haptics (2026-09-12).** The owner: most apps buzz slightly on the press-and-hold that reacts to
  a message; other moments may deserve one; tastefully. What stood: the surface's own
  `navigator.vibrate(12)` on a hold and `(8)` at a swipe's release. Now ONE engine, the shell's
  (`app/haptic.ts`, `window.mcHaptic`, installed before anything that buzzes): `haptic(kind)` with
  small patterns — `hold` 12 ms, `pick` 6, `arm` 8, `tap` 6 — and the ring's `[300,150,300]`
  repeated every 2.2 s while a call rings, stopped by the engine's one teardown. Where: the hold
  that opens a surface; a reaction picked (the bar, the picker, a chip); a swipe or a pull crossing
  its line (not the release — the crossing is the moment); the ring. Not: a send, a save, a tap on a
  button. The platform truth, said plainly: Android has the Vibration API; **iOS Safari has none, in
  a tab or the installed app** — the one known road is the haptic iOS 17.4+ plays when a `switch`
  checkbox toggles, so the engine toggles a hidden one (best-effort, silent where it does nothing,
  never for the ring, only under `(hover: none)`); and Chrome refuses a vibrate before the frame's
  first real tap (`userActivation`). The client's surface buzzes through the engine when it stands
  and through the API alone when it does not. Guard: `tests/js/calls.test.mjs` (the patterns, the
  iOS road, the install order, every call site). **The same day, the owner on his iPhone: "vibes on
  iOS didn't work."** As expected on reflection — the switch haptic fires only under a real tap on
  the switch itself, never a programmatic toggle from a hold's timer — so the hack came out (a road
  that does nothing is a lie in the code): the engine is the Vibration API alone, honestly silent on
  iOS. A native shell (CallKit, the app stores) is the only way to haptics or a ringing screen on an
  iPhone; see the calls passage.

- **Badges everywhere, and reading marks read (2026-09-12).** The owner's three reports, one root
  each. (1) "On the home tab my friend messaged me and I got no notification until I clicked over to
  community." The live socket was open on Home (the "badges everywhere" contract of app/live.ts) but
  the handlers that turn a `dm` or `notification` frame into a badge refresh lived in the classic
  boot (client/dm.ts `liveDmBadge`, client/profile.ts `liveNotifBadge`), which runs only on the
  platform pages: on Home, in a book, in the Bible reader, nobody was listening. Now
  **`app/badges.ts`**, installed by the shell right after the live layer, owns the refresh on every
  page: a `dm` frame not for the thread on screen (that thread appends the word and pings seen) →
  `/dm/unread` and one bell sound; a `notification` frame unless the notifications list is open (it
  reloads and marks read) → `/notifications/unread`; a `mc-live-resync` (the socket reopened after a
  hidden or idle spell — frames it missed are not replayed) → both, when their cache is stale.
  Event-driven, debounced, one read per trigger, never a poller; the caches are the ones the chrome
  reads and a write dispatches `mc-badge`; the classic handlers defer to it when it stands (no
  second read, no second bell), and the identity line repaints on a shell write. (2) "The inbox
  badge lit but the bell didn't" — the same root: the two frames arrive separately and the classic
  heard neither off-platform; both roads are the shell's now. (3) "Sitting in an open DM thread, he
  received a message, clearly saw it, and the bell never unlit — why was it even lit?" Two halves.
  The bell had not been rung for THAT message (the send's quiet bell, `dmViewing`, already skips the
  row when the thread is on screen) — it was an older unread row from the same sender, and nothing
  cleared it: opening a conversation stamped the thread read but never the notifications, and the
  bell's badge kept a 90 s cache. Now `handleDmThread` (open) and `handleDmSeen` read every bell the
  sender rang me — `dm`, `dm-react`, `call` — and hand back `notif_unread`, which the client sets
  the instant the payload lands; `handleWallPostGet` (keyed) reads a feed post's bells (`wall`,
  `wall-like`, `wall-react`) the same way, and `viewPost` sets the count; `handleBoardRead` already
  did this for a topic. And a reaction to a word on screen rings no bell now, the send's rule
  mirrored in `handleDmReact`. The law, as every app has it: a bell never stays lit for a thing the
  reader is looking at, however they reached it. Guards: `tests/js/badges.test.mjs` (the shell owns
  the refresh; the classic defers; every door marks read and sets the count),
  `tests/worker/notif_read.test.mjs` (the read-marks run on the real ledger — the thread's clears
  the sender's three kinds and no other sender's; the post's clears its own bells and no other
  post's). Proven headless over the DM stub: on `index.html` — no classic boot — a synthetic `dm`
  frame lights the Inbox tab and a `notification` frame the bell; opening the thread with a payload
  saying `notif_unread: 0` unlights the bell at once; a `dm` frame for the thread on screen leaves
  the shell silent.

- **The hole above the chat header (2026-09-12).** The owner's screenshot: a band of nothing between
  the app bar and the thread's header, appearing only after a hard fling to the top. Not the
  pull-to-refresh (a fixed pill that pushes nothing) and not the sticky machinery: it was the board
  section's own `margin-top: 1rem` (`section.comments.board`), which the thread's section inherits.
  A thread opens at its foot and stays scrolled, where the sticky header is pinned under the app bar
  and the margin lies out of sight above it; a fling that reaches the true top un-sticks the header
  and bares the margin. Measured headless at the top: 16 px between the bar's foot and the header.
  The chat screen's section carries no top margin on phones now (`body.mc-app
  section.comments.board.dm-screen{margin-top:0}` in the DM's injected block) — the header begins
  where the bar ends, at rest and at 0 alike. Guard: `tests/js/dm_actions.test.mjs` (the chat-screen
  test); the local DM fixture measures 0.

- **Media hygiene: every delete takes its media (2026-09-12).** The owner's ask: when a message with
  media is deleted — feed, community or DM, by hand or by expiry — the media must go with it, to
  keep storage minimal and clean. The audit, road by road. **DMs**: a redacted message purges its
  object first, then blanks the row (`handleDmRedact`); a conversation purges every object once BOTH
  sides have cleared and no word outlives the earlier clear (`handleDmDelete`; one side's clear
  alone keeps the other's view, by design); the hourly `sweepExpiredDms` purges expired unsaved
  messages' objects before deleting the rows, purges and stamps `media_expired` on attachments past
  the retention cap even inside a saved message, and reclaims orphans (an upload unlinked past
  fifteen minutes, or whose message is gone). **The feed**: `handleWallDelete` purges a comment's
  object before its row, and a post's own plus every comment's under it before theirs;
  `runWallPrune` purges before it deletes; `sweepWallOrphanMedia` and `sweepMediaRetention` back it.
  **The board**: a soft-deleted post's attachment is purged at once and its key nulled (retraction
  semantics — the row never renders again); a topic delete and a move into the back room gather the
  whole thread's keys and purge them before the status flips; the orphan sweep reclaims any `board`
  object whose owner is neither live nor pending within the hour. Both purge helpers delete the R2
  object AND the accounting row (`dm_media` / `wall_media`), so the byte counters stay true. **The
  one gap**: admin "delete user and all posts" (`handleDeleteUser`) predates the feed — it retired
  their board posts and purged those attachments, deleted their profile and avatar, locked the hash,
  and left their FEED entirely: posts, comments, media. It now hard-deletes every post of theirs
  with the comments under it (everyone's, as `/wall/delete` does), every comment of theirs under
  others' posts, the reactions on all of it, recomputes the surviving posts' comment counts, and
  purges every gathered key — keys read before the rows go, the orphan sweep the backstop. Their DM
  media is deliberately left to the conversation's own rules (the other party's view, the expiry
  cap). Guard: `tests/worker/media_hygiene.test.mjs` — every road above checked on the sources
  (purge before drop, never a null without a purge), the sweeps in the cron chain, and the
  delete-user road run on the real ledger (my post's image, the comment's image under it, my
  comment's image under theirs; their post and their own comment untouched; counts and reactions
  right).

- **The fixed chrome answers the finger, not the platform's click (2026-09-13).** The owner, iPhone:
  Inbox pressed, the tab lit, the screen never loading; a second press worked; rare. The screenshot
  said what the words could not: measured, the Home tab was drawn in `--maroon` (the tab bar's own
  `mc-tab-on`) and Inbox in `--maroon-dark` — the global `a:hover`, iOS's sticky hover, which rides
  the synthetic mouse events a click comes with. So the shell's state had never moved: no `pending`
  tab, no skeleton, no swap — `softNav` had not run, because no click had been dispatched. A phone
  synthesizes the click after the finger lifts and withholds it whenever it decides the tap meant
  something else: iOS for a tap that lands while the page is still decelerating (the tap stops the
  scroll instead — a fling to the top and straight to a tab, which is the Home page's whole shape)
  and for a tap whose hover it judges to have changed content (the first tap becomes a hover, the
  second the click). Nothing in our CSS reveals content on hover and nothing of ours swallows the
  click (every document-level listener was read), so the platform's judgement was the cause, and it
  is not ours to argue with — the finger's own events are delivered every time. The cure: the tab
  bar and the app bar answer them directly (`armTap`, `app/appchrome.ts`). A touch that begins on a
  control and lifts on the same one (`elementFromPoint` — sliding off cancels, as it does a native
  button), in place and quickly, IS the press: `Domain.Tap` gives the verdict (`slop` 12 px on the
  farther axis, tracked at every move rather than only at the lift; `holdMs` 500, under iOS's link
  preview; travel judged before time), the control's own `click()` is dispatched inside the touchend
  — so the shell's click road runs unchanged, the destination painted while the finger is still
  lifting — the platform's synthetic click is cancelled by the touchend's `preventDefault` (no
  second navigation, no hover tint left behind), and an engine that sends its click anyway meets a
  capture-phase guard that swallows a trusted click within `echoMs` 700 of the press. A drag, a hold
  and a lift elsewhere are left to the platform exactly as before, and every other road in — a
  mouse, a keyboard, a screen reader's double-tap — still arrives as a click. Deliberately only the
  two bars: a link in the content keeps the platform's judgement, where a tap during deceleration
  stopping the scroll is the right answer, as it is in every native scroll view. Guard:
  `tests/purescript/tap.test.mjs` (the verdict and its numbers), `tests/js/tabbar.test.mjs` (the
  road on both bars, the kernel's numbers only, the cancel, the echo guard, nowhere else),
  `webtest/test_tap.py` (a synthesized press on the Inbox tab navigates softly, once, by the road's
  own untrusted click with the engine's cancelled; a drag and a hold get nothing from the road —
  their touchend left alone, no click of ours — whatever the engine then does with them, and
  Chromium does send its own click after a long press).

- **One member model, envelope v2, and the object that outlives its message (2026-09-13).** The
  owner: forward messages without re-uploading an attachment (the same file, never fully deleted
  until every conversation using it is done with it — "careful not to lose anon encryption"), and
  let anyone add members to a two-way thread, a member staying for the life of the thread unless
  they leave, the thread expiring when nothing is saved and nobody remains; "keep things encrypted
  end to end in multi user conversation"; for every further decision, imitate Snapchat; audit every
  two-person assumption. What was two-party: `dm_threads(a_hash, b_hash, a/b_read_at,
  a/b_cleared_at)`, `dms(react_a, react_b, opened_at, expires_at)`, `dm_media(msg_id)` (claim-once
  at send, a read ACL through the ONE message), `dmPair` in eleven handlers, the two `CASE a/b`
  stamp pickers, `dmViewing(recipient, sender)` by the counterpart, `notifyDm` coalescing per actor,
  `Domain.Notif.href` addressing every DM door as `?dm=<actor>`, one scope per fan-out, and on the
  client the `?dm=` URL with eight live handlers comparing `m.from`, `ctx.other/otherPub`, a bubble
  with no author, receipts from one `opened_at`, the pill's literal `'2'`, typing to one `to`, inbox
  rows by `other_hash`. The decisions, with the owner: a cap of 25; a small "Forwarded" line; ONE
  member model for all (the pairs migrated). Migration 0016 rebuilds `dm_threads` (kind, `pair_key`
  — canonical, NULL-distinct UNIQUE — name, created_by; the six pair columns carried across nullable
  and left unread, 0012's rule), makes `dm_members`, `dm_reactions`, `dm_keys`, `dm_media_refs`,
  adds `dms.saved_by`, backfills every one and names each DM bell's thread in `topic_id`. Two
  findings shaped it: an `INSERT … SELECT id` copy sets the new table's AUTOINCREMENT sequence to
  the MAX id copied, not the old `seq`, so a deleted thread's id would be minted again and its stray
  rows re-attach — a sentinel row at the old `seq`, then deleted, carries the mark; and
  `tests/purescript/notif.test.mjs` greps the newest migration for `CHECK (kind IN (`, so `kind`
  carries no CHECK (a comment that quoted the phrase tripped it too). A legacy unique index on the
  nullable pair columns stays one deploy, because the ledger is applied BEFORE the deploy and the
  worker built before this upserts with `ON CONFLICT(a_hash, b_hash)`; `ensurePairThread` upserts
  target-less so it heals a room that worker made. Every primitive in `lib.ts` is per-viewer over
  `mb` (`DM_VIS` gains the group's read-time block, `DM_CLEARED` the joining, `DM_MINE` the seat);
  every handler reads through `dmThreadFor` (a stranger's id is "no such conversation"); the send
  takes `enc` 3 with `keys` checked against the roster (`Domain.Dm.membersEqual`, a stale roster
  answered with the fresh one), a shared object named again only by a reader of it, one `batch` for
  the keys, the reference and the stamps, a fan-out to every member's `user:` scope with the keys
  riding the frame, one hub RPC (`viewersOf`) for the quiet bell, a bell per absent member coalesced
  per thread, one batched push opening `?t=`. `releaseMediaRefs` replaces every bare
  `purgeMediaKeys` on a message road: the references go, then only the objects nothing names; the
  cap and the valve take an object from under every message naming it (`dmExpireObjects`); the
  orphan sweep is "nothing names it, past the window"; `sweepDms` takes the members, keys, reactions
  and references of what is gone. The other session's call log (0017, `recordCallEnd`) landed while
  this was being written: the missed-call line and bell now name the pair's thread through
  `ensurePairThread` + `sendSystemDmLine`, and `Domain.Dm.missedCallLine` is an alias of
  `Domain.Call`'s. What would break silently is on the record in
  `tests/worker/{migration_0016,dm_members,dm_media_refs,dm_notif_thread,dm_groups}.test.mjs`.

- **Multi-member conversations and forwarding (2026-09-13).** The Snapchat behaviours copied: a
  newcomer sees no history (the crypto's rule — no `dm_keys` row — and the server's `joined_at`
  filter, defence in depth); anyone adds, nobody owns; adding to a 1:1 chat creates a NEW group (the
  pair and its private history stay); leave any time, "X left" the last word; ✓✓ once EVERY other
  current member's read stamp reaches the word (`Domain.Dm.readByAll`; `dm_members.read_at` is the
  source, `opened_at` only the first-open clock — the FIRST open by any recipient starts
  everyone's); typing names who; save by anyone, the saver named; one TTL, last write wins;
  per-member clear, the purge when everyone has cleared or nobody remains; a block inside a group
  hides that member's words from the blocker (and a blocker cannot be added by the blocked — one
  generic refusal for "no key yet" and "blocks you", by design); a name and a collage; reactions one
  per member with a tally. Envelope v2: a random content key K per message, the body K's secretbox
  (`E3.`), K boxed once per member to their published X25519 key — the same primitives as E1, one
  road for pairs and groups; an edit re-seals under the same K; the live frame carries the whole key
  map (one RPC, the others' blobs ciphertext to keys a reader does not hold); no forward secrecy, as
  before; TOFU and the pairwise safety number, now per member row. Forwarding: the copy is a new
  word of the forwarder's, sealed afresh for each target's roster (read without a mark through
  `/dm/roster`), the quote dropped, the mark inside the envelope (the server never learns a TEXT was
  forwarded, nor from where; that two messages share an object it can see — the accepted cost of not
  re-uploading), one Turnstile for a batch of ten through `/dm/forward` and the send's own
  `deliverDmWord`. The client: `messages.html?t=<id>` is the door, `?dm=<hash>` the pair's resolver
  that takes the id in the address (`replaceState`, never a navigation) once the room exists; every
  live frame keyed on `thread_id` through `forOpen` (an unmade pair's first word adopts its id); the
  5-sub cap holds because a group watches no live presence (its ⓘ asks `/dm/presence` once); the
  group's pill is the public painter with the DM's own pick; the membership lines are the call
  line's shape; the two pickers (Forward; New group / Add members) warm Turnstile as they open,
  being presses. Rollout in three pushes: the worker (serving both shapes — `other`,
  `react_me`/`react_other`, `?dm=` for a bell without a thread, E1 sends on a pair,
  `dmview:<hash>`), the client, and a week later the shims. The daily R2 mirror of the database was
  found ABSENT (no `backups/comments-*.sql.gz` for any probed day; `wrangler d1 export` refuses a
  database with FTS5 tables); the DM tables were dumped through `wrangler d1 execute --json` to
  `~/.config/merecatholicity/backups/` before the push — the mirror's failure is open.

- **Who has read, in a group (2026-09-15).** The owner's first three-way conversation: a lone ✓
  under a word two people had read, and no way to know which — "the simplistic read receipt system …
  makes the whole thing kind of maybe broken in conversations that have more than two people. I like
  the way it works with two people." The two-person rule (✓ delivered, ✓✓ read) is kept as it was;
  the group gains what the per-member watermark already knew. Seen-by faces (Messenger's and
  Snapchat's way): each other member's small avatar sits under the last word they have read — their
  `read_at` against the words' `created_at` — moving down live on every `dm-read` frame, on a roster
  change, and on a member's own word (a sender has read up to what they wrote, and the server stamps
  them on send, so the client marks them read to it); never under their own word (implied), never
  for a member who hides their reads (their stamp is withheld), never in a pair. Message info
  (WhatsApp's): Info on my own word in a group — the surface's act, and the tick itself is a button
  — opens a sheet of Read / Delivered / Receipts off per member; the stamps are watermarks, so it
  tells that a member has read, never the minute. And the denominator: the thread now says whether
  each member reports reads (`receipts`, beside the withheld stamp), and ✓✓ waits only for those who
  do — a member with receipts off could otherwise keep a group's tick single for ever. Locked in
  `tests/js/dm_groups.test.mjs` and `tests/worker/dm_members.test.mjs`.

- **`contact-worker/`** — the contact form backend on custom domain
  `contact-api.merecatholicity.com`. Turnstile-gated, throttled, sends to a verified Email Routing
  destination address. Client side is `contact.js`.

- **The worker can speak: alerts by email and Discord, both from Platform settings (2026-09-16)** —
  the 2026-09-16 review counted sixty-one `*_failed` log events nobody read and no road from any of
  them to a human; the owner's ruling was to make the Discord webhook an admin setting, to send
  alerts by email through the same `send_email` road the contact form uses, to make the address an
  admin setting too, and to choose email, Discord or both. The road is
  `comments-worker/src/alerts.ts`: `sendAlert(env, {kind, subject, text})` reads the four settings
  (`alert_email`, `alert_email_on`, `alert_discord_webhook`, `alert_discord_on`), asks the kernel
  which channels they open (`Domain.Ops.channelsFrom` — a channel speaks when its switch is the
  literal `'1'` AND its field passes its validator; empty or off is silent), mails through the
  `send_email` binding `EMAIL` from `alerts@merecatholicity.com` (the `ALERT_FROM` var overrides;
  any address on the onboarded domain works with no setup) and posts to Discord through the
  `sendDiscord` the forum hooks already use, which now answers whether Discord accepted. It never
  throws: a refusal is a line in `errors`, logged as `alert_failed`, and the test door `POST
  /admin/alert-test` hands the whole result to the admin's screen — so "the destination address is
  not verified" is read, not guessed. The settings door validates at the door
  (`Domain.Ops.isEmailAddress`, the contact form's shape with no empty label; `isDiscordWebhook`,
  the SSRF gate), so a typo is refused when typed and not discovered at the first real alert. Two
  facts that shape it: the binding can write ONLY to a destination address the owner has verified in
  Cloudflare → Email Routing → Destination addresses — a dashboard act (Terraform cannot hold Email
  Routing; CICD §10 exception 5), so the panel says so and the test button proves it; and the
  `wrangler.jsonc` comment that said "no send_email binding here anymore" (after the per-comment
  email notifications were retired) is corrected — the binding is back for this one purpose. The
  usage monitor's admin DMs stay and will also ride this road; the daily backup and the self-check
  that follow (the next passage) are its first standing callers. Proven in
  `tests/purescript/ops.test.mjs` (the channel rule, the polarity) and
  `tests/worker/alerts.test.mjs` (through the doors: the validators, exactly the channels the
  settings open, a refused mail and a dead webhook as errors and never a 500, the address never in
  `/config`).

- **Backups are daily and verified, and every cron is a chain that cannot lose a step (2026-09-16)**
  — first, the premises the review got wrong, so nobody repairs the wrong thing: the backup had
  never died — it ran on the MONTHLY cron only (`backups/comments-2026-08-01.sql.gz`, 17 KB, and
  `…-09-01.sql.gz`, 31 KB, are in `merecatholicity-backups`), nothing was daily, and nothing could
  say so; the FTS5 tables break only `wrangler d1 export` (the hand road), `dumpDatabase` had always
  skipped `comments_fts%`; and the "~50-subrequest cron budget" the comments cited is the cap for
  EXTERNAL fetches — binding calls (D1, R2, KV) have 1,000 per invocation on the free plan, which is
  why the dump's ~40 reads and the avatar mirror's 600 calls fit. What was actually wrong with the
  dump: plain `INSERT INTO` (a replay into a non-empty database duplicated or died), `CREATE UNIQUE
  INDEX` not rewritten `IF NOT EXISTS` (sqlite_master strips the clause, so the second statement of
  a restore drill failed), the avatar mirror capped at 15, `list().truncated` ignored, and a BLOB
  column (none yet) would have been dumped as `[object ArrayBuffer]`. All five fixed;
  `tests/worker/backup.test.mjs` replays the dump into a fresh SQLite twice and gets the same ledger
  both times, search rebuilt. The cadence: a fourth cron, `15 3 * * *`, runs `runBackup` daily (the
  key is `Domain.Ops.backupKey(today)`; the prune keeps a daily 90 days and a first-of-month object
  400 — the monthly history survives the daily cadence — and deletes only keys the rule recognises);
  the monthly chain keeps the housekeeping and gains `mirrorAvatars` (cap 300). Every cron is now a
  chain through `comments-worker/src/ops.ts`'s `runChain`: each step in its own try/catch
  (`cron_step_failed` logged, the chain goes on — before, a throwing prune took the backup standing
  last in the chain with it), the chain's heartbeat stamped in `app_settings.ops_heartbeat` at the
  end, and its failures plus the self-check's findings folded through `Domain.Ops.foldOpsAlerts`
  into at most two alerts a run (trouble, recovered), coalesced across runs — a condition is told
  once when it opens and once when it closes, never twice a day while it stands, and a chain judges
  only what it can observe (`Domain.Ops.alertScope`: its own step failures, plus the backup and
  staleness conditions when it ran the self-check), so the hourly sweeps finding nothing wrong never
  "recover" the daily's missing backup. `runSelfCheck` rides the 03:15 chain (after the backup) and
  the 23:30 usage chain: today's object present and at least 1 KB (or the failure `runBackup`
  recorded in `ops_backup`), and every chain's heartbeat inside `Domain.Ops.staleAfter` (hourly 3 h,
  dailies 26 h, monthly 33 d) — a heartbeat that never beat is not stale (a fresh deploy must not
  cry about a monthly cron 33 days away; the health card shows "never"), and the backup is judged
  only once the daily chain has beaten at least once. Because a watchdog inside a dead worker says
  nothing, the outside leg is the report door `POST /api/comments/ops/report` (keyed like the
  librarian's pipeline — `requireIngest`, so no new secret; it joins `INGEST_DOORS` and skips the
  origin guard as CI has no Origin): `{probe: true}` answers `readOps` — heartbeats with ages, the
  last backup's record and the object actually in the bucket, open conditions, the nightly webtest's
  last report, and `ok`, the verdict — and `.github/workflows/ops-watch.yml` fails its scheduled run
  when `ok` is false, GitHub's own failed-run mail being the alert that needs no worker; `{source:
  'webtest', …}` stores the dev box's nightly report and alerts on a regression. `POST
  /admin/health` is the same object for the Health card in Platform settings; `POST /backup` ("Back
  up now") keeps its documented shape and now records `ops_backup`. The usage monitor's admin DMs
  stay and also ride `sendAlert`. Every state is an `app_settings` row (`ops_heartbeat`,
  `ops_backup`, `ops_alert_state`, `ops_webtest`) — no table, no migration. The hand road is the
  restore drill: `make comments-backup` fetches the latest daily object with the site token
  (`scripts/backup_fetch.sh`, to `~/.config/merecatholicity/backups/`, outside the repo) and
  `scripts/backup_check.py` replays it twice into a local SQLite and prints every table's count —
  `wrangler d1 export` left the Makefile (it refuses a database with FTS5 tables; the worker's own
  dump is the backup). Proven in `tests/worker/ops.test.mjs` (a throwing step does not stop the
  chain; once/quiet/recovered; the scope rule; the self-check's three conditions and the recovery;
  the health verdict; the report door) and `tests/purescript/ops.test.mjs` (every rule).

- **Rollback is a drilled road, a staged rollout is a switch, and the watchdog has an outside leg
  (2026-09-16)** — the review's P1-6: no rollback had ever been tried, every deploy was 100% at
  once, and two agents shared one checkout. Rollback: `scripts/worker_rollback.sh` (`make
  worker-rollback`, `VERSION=<id>` for a particular one) reads the current Version from `wrangler
  deployments status --json`, picks the newest other Version from `wrangler versions list --json`,
  runs `wrangler rollback <id> -y`, and probes `/api/comments/config` — Cloudflare keeps the last
  hundred uploaded Versions, so a rollback is a re-pointing, seconds long, no build. Its two
  refusals: across a Durable Object class migration, or when a binding the target Version needs is
  gone — then `git revert` + push is the only road. It undoes no D1 migration (additive by law, so
  the older worker keeps running against the newer schema), and it must ALWAYS be paired with a `git
  revert` push, or the next push re-deploys what was rolled away from. The site's rollback is `git
  revert` + push (the Pages artifact keeps one day; a rerun is not a rollback), Terraform's `git
  revert` + the gate. Drilled for real on 2026-09-16 (the timings are in CICD.md §12). Staged
  rollout: `workers.yml`'s `workflow_dispatch` gained `mode` (deploy | stage | promote) and
  `percent`; `stage` runs `scripts/worker_stage.sh stage N` — `wrangler versions upload` (a Version,
  not a deployment), then `wrangler versions deploy <new>@N% <current>@(100−N)%` — and `promote`
  sends 100% to the newest Version; a push stays the plain deploy. The CI road is the build of
  record; `make worker-stage` / `worker-promote` are the emergency hand roads. Caveats, both
  wrangler's: a staged Version does not update triggers or routes (`wrangler triggers deploy` after
  a cron change), and a split across a Durable Object class migration is refused. When to use it: a
  DM envelope, roster, hub or call change no webtest covers. The outside leg:
  `.github/workflows/ops-watch.yml` runs at 03:45 UTC, half an hour after the backup, POSTs `{probe:
  true}` to the ops report door on the workers.dev hostname with `MC_INGEST_KEY` (merecat.yml's
  secret — nothing new to hold), prints the heartbeat table into the job summary and FAILS THE RUN
  when `health.ok` is false — GitHub's failed-run mail reaches the owner without any part of the
  worker having to work; a `host` input points it at nowhere for the drill that proves the red path.
  The nightly webtest: `scripts/webtest_nightly.py` runs sixteen READ-ONLY webtest suites (none
  posts, messages or moderates; the admin-key and fixture-writing suites stay out), compares each
  suite's PASS/FAIL and exit with `webtest/nightly_baseline.json` (`make nightly-baseline` records
  the known shape — the admin-key failures included, so they never alert), and POSTs the report to
  the same door, where a regression alerts through the Alerts channels and the Health card shows the
  last run; `scripts/systemd/webtest-nightly.{service,timer}` (user units, 04:30 local,
  `Persistent=true`; `make nightly-install`) run it on the dev box, a box that is off reporting
  nothing — the card shows the age, no alert. Two agents: `scripts/agent_worktree.sh <name>` makes a
  detached worktree under `local/wt/` (now git-ignored) on `origin/main`, sharing `node_modules` and
  compiling its own `purescript/output`; the rule in CLAUDE.md — commit and push FROM the worktree,
  fast-forward the shared checkout, a migration in the same commit as the worker that reads it.

- **The four nits (2026-09-16)** — P3 of the review, done together. The root holds only
  tool-convention files again: `FRONT_END_REVIEW.txt`, the `I_AM_DONE` hand-over note and the review
  itself moved to `docs/architecture/reviews/` as dated files (the rule is now in CLAUDE.md's layout
  paragraph); `local/` is ignored. `tests/py/test_api_parity.py` holds `comments-worker/API.md` to
  the route table: every mounted route must be named (API.md abbreviates — `` `/lock` ·
  `/deleteuser` `` — so the test resolves a path with its prefix stripped inside backtick spans),
  and every full path the document names must be mounted or listed in the test's `RETIRED` map with
  its reason (`/api/merecat/ask`, `/api/merecat/store`, both documented as gone). It found fifteen
  undocumented doors on its first run — `/prefs`, `/bookmark`, `/recent`, the five `/call/*`,
  `/push/vapid-key`, `/shadowban` and its list, the three `/admin/discord/*`, `/wall/prune` — all
  written down now (§3.3, §4.3, a new §4.4 Voice calls, §8). The member directory never lists
  machinery: `hiddenHashes(env)` joins the `TEST_HASHES` secret (the interactive kit's write-bypass
  identities) with a new public `HIDDEN_HASHES` var (the nightly webtest's read-only probes and a
  second agent's identity — hashes are public by design, like the admins'), keeps only whole hashes,
  and `handleDmDirectory` excludes them (`tests/worker/hidden_hashes.test.mjs`); the "53 members for
  six humans" the review counted were these. And CODEBASE.md carries the TypeScript 7 note: the
  native compiler, exact-pinned, no JavaScript API (why typescript-eslint cannot run and the `: any`
  ratchet is a stdlib test), churn expected between minors.

## Build system

- **The long-form reference is a monthly log with a generated index (2026-09-16)** — the review's
  P2-4: `INFRASTRUCTURE.md` had grown to 391 KB in 313 lines (the longest 31,203 characters), two
  agents appended on the same day, and the index line in CLAUDE.md was a guaranteed merge conflict.
  The file was split by passage into `docs/architecture/log/YYYY-MM.md`: a passage is a block
  opening with a bold lead-in; its month is the date in its lead-in (47 of the 88), else the latest
  date in its text (7 — a passage narrating history cites earlier dates), else the month `git blame`
  gives its first line at HEAD (34); within a month the passages keep their original order under `##
  section` headings; every prose line is wrapped at 100 columns (bullets with a two-space hanging
  indent — a code span may break across lines, which Markdown allows); the preamble (the standing
  rules) and each section's intro paragraphs stayed in `INFRASTRUCTURE.md`, and the index of every
  lead-in by section is GENERATED at its end by `scripts/infra_index.py --write` between two marker
  lines. The split was proven neutral: the multiset of words of the old file equals that of the new
  index file plus the three month files (58,957 words). The one-off splitter is not in the repo (its
  method is this passage); the permanent tool is `scripts/infra_index.py` (`--check`, the test's
  judge: a stale index, a duplicated lead-in, a passage under no heading, a prose line over 100
  columns; `--write`; `--wrap FILE`). CLAUDE.md's seventy-line index became a paragraph pointing
  here — half of P2-3 as well. Appending a passage is now: the current month's file, its section
  heading, a bullet with a bold lead-in and a date, wrap, regenerate.

- **CLAUDE.md compacted: the laws in full (2026-09-16)** — the review's P2-3: CLAUDE.md, "kept short
  on purpose", had grown to 436 lines, with law bullets of thirty lines and a "next migration"
  number that drifted twice in a week. Each law now keeps its operative sentences (six lines at
  most; `tests/py/test_claude_md.py` holds the file to 280 lines and every bullet to six — the plan
said 260, but the laws alone are 130 lines at six each, so the ratchet sits at 280 and may only
fall), the two
  descriptive sections are pointers, the next migration number is derived (`make migration`), and
  everything CLAUDE.md said beyond that moved here VERBATIM, one sub-bullet per law — nothing the
  owner wrote is lost, only relocated. The rule stays in CLAUDE.md; this is the reasoning and the
  detail that stood behind it.
  - **Workers ship on push** — **Workers ship on push** (`workers.yml`: gates → D1 ledger →
`wrangler deploy`). **So does the librarian's shelf** (`merecat.yml`: waits for the Build, ingests
against its `docs/` and the private shelf, incrementally, daily resume; `make librarian` is the hand
road). `make worker-deploy` is the emergency road; `wrangler secret put` is the only home for worker
secrets. **Rollback is a drilled road** (`make worker-rollback`, seconds; then `git revert` + push —
CICD §12); **a staged rollout is a switch** (`gh workflow run workers.yml -f mode=stage -f
percent=10`, then `mode=promote`) for a change no webtest covers; `ops-watch.yml` asks the worker
daily how it is and goes red when it is not.
  - **Every page's client is a boot the shell drives** — Since 2026-09-11 the boot installs the
feature modules (`client/*.ts`, each an `install<Feature>(B)` factory) per boot: a module's body is
boot-scoped exactly as before, its cross-module names are bound from the boot object `B` after every
module is installed (`bind()`), and what ran at the boot's top level runs in its `run()` — so a
module-level `var` may not call another module's or a page-scoped helper in its initializer (it is
not bound yet; the generator deferred such initializers to `run()`), and state more than one module
writes lives on `B` (`B.quotedSelection` and friends), never in a copied binding. A new feature is a
new `client/<feature>.ts` factory wired in the root's install list. Page-scoped state stamped on
`<html>`/`<body>` must be cleared by the shell on navigation (only `<main>` is swapped).
  - **Turnstile** — The host is the document's own (`tsHost()`, `body > .mc-ts-host[data-mc-app]`),
never inside `<main>`, never off-screen, never without its stylesheet. The contact form
(`docs/contact.js`, its own sitekey, nobody to spare) mounts on the first focus only, and
`contact.html` is a **document page** in the shell (`DOCUMENT_PAGES`): every door reaches it by a
full load, because the challenge completes on a hard-loaded document and killed soft-navigated ones.
  - **Comments sections are admin-switched and ship CLOSED** — A section may stand only under the
site's own writings, and that list is DETECTED, never kept: `scripts/writings.py` reads every
`content/` page (unless its frontmatter opts out with `comments: false`) and every book the root
Makefile builds, and writes the generated, git-ignored `Domain.Writings` on every `psbuild` — a new
article or book brings its own switch; a library work never; a book target must use `book-tail.html`
(the detector refuses one without); a path is a storage key, never rename one. A closed section
answers exactly as an unknown page; a deleted journal article retires its comments
(`sweepJournalComments`); a switch deletes nothing.
  - **A NEW state store must be added to `runBackup()`'s mirror** — Ops state is `app_settings` rows
(`ops_*`), never a table.
  - **A DM message's acts live on ONE surface** — A reaction is ONE emoji per MEMBER per message
(`dm_reactions`, one row each; a pair's pill shows the two sides, a group's the tally through the
surface's painter with the DM's own pick — never the public ledger's wires), validated by
`Domain.Reaction.normalizeReaction` (re-exported by `Domain.Dm`) on both ends (never re-inlined; a
custom `:token:` is ours); a quoted reply rides INSIDE the E2E plaintext behind
`Domain.Dm.replySentinel` — the server never learns what answers what, and no `reply_to` column may
appear; so does the small "Forwarded" mark (`fwd` in the same header, `env.fwd` for media) — no
`forward_of` either. The surface keeps the overlay's three layers through `mcSheet.lock()` and
releases only a lock it took. **The thread is a chat screen**: a sticky header (avatar · presence ·
📞 · ⓘ) that begins where the app bar ends — no margin above it, which a fling to the true top would
bare — and a FIXED composer (+ · field · 😊 · mic-or-Send) always in view — fixed, never sticky: a
sticky bar floats above the tab bar at the document's end, where a thread opens; everything that is
not a message lives in the ⓘ sheet; the saved mark is a gold ★ and a gold-tinted border, never a
ring; on a phone the emoji picker and the keyboard never share the screen (opening the picker blurs
the field; a pick hands the keyboard back). **Editing a message happens IN the composer** (an
"Editing message" strip, the text in the field, Send a ✓, ✕/Escape giving the draft back) — never a
box in the bubble, which the composer riding the keyboard would cover. An element toggled by
`hidden` needs a `[hidden]{display:none}` rule if it carries its own `display`. **A hold picks a
message, never a word**: under `(hover:none)` the whole chat screen (`section.dm-screen`) and the
phone chrome are not selectable text, only the fields are; the surface never is; opening it drops
any selection — iOS anchors a long-press selection in the NEAREST selectable text, so a rule on the
bubble alone is not enough. **Reading back is never interrupted and never blind**: a word that lands
while the foot is out of view stays put under an "N unread messages" line, the jump button above the
composer carries the count, and "seen" goes out only for words the reader reached (at the foot as
they arrived, or when they come down); on open the server names what was unread BEFORE the open
(`unread`, `unread_from`) and the landing is WhatsApp's (the line under the header when the unread
words do not fit); typing shows in the header, as a three-dot bubble at the foot, and on the inbox
row — never for a member who appears offline (the hub's gate, `Domain.Presence.isVisible`); the
inbox badge is the count.
  - **One member model** — Adding to a pair FORKS a new group (Snapchat's and WhatsApp's way; the
pair and its history stay); leaving stamps the seat, the last member out purges the thread, and a
thread with no surviving word dies (`sweepDms`) — nobody remains. The six pair columns of
`dm_threads` are legacy: no new code may read `a_hash`/`b_hash`/`a_read_at`/… ; every conversation
is addressed by `thread_id` (`messages.html?t=<id>`, the resolved form of a pair's `?dm=<hash>` door
and the only door a group has), every live frame carries it and the client keys on it, and a DM bell
names its thread in `topic_id`. **Who has read, in a group**: ✓✓ once every other member who REPORTS
reads has (`Domain.Dm.readByAll` over the members whose `receipts` is on; a member with receipts off
sends no stamp and is not waited for), each member's small face sits under the last word they read
(the seen-by row, Messenger's way — never under their own word, never in a pair), and Info on my
word — the surface's act, or the tick itself — lists Read / Delivered / Receipts off. Membership
changes are system lines (`sys:add:`, `sys:leave`, `sys:name:` — `Domain.Dm.parseSysLine`), never
message content; the group name is server-visible metadata by design.
  - **Envelope v2** — The client's sealing functions are proven by running them with tweetnacl
(`tests/js/dm_envelope.test.mjs`).
  - **ONE press-and-hold surface, ONE reaction grammar, ONE bell** — The public reactions are the
`reactions` ledger (one per member per `post`/`wall`/`wallc` target — a like is the ❤️ reaction, the
like tables are frozen); tallies ride every served post row (`reacts`), the viewer's own ride the
keyed `/reacts` after a cached board read; the client's ledger (`reactRegister`/`reactSend`) is the
one painter, and a reaction goes to the wire only through `mcCore.reaction`. A reaction rings
`react`/`wall-react`/`dm-react` — coalesced, never for your own post, withdrawn with the reaction,
and the word is always "reacted". The notification list's sentences and doors are `Domain.Notif`
(`mcCore.notifLabel` / `notifHref`) — never an inline label map again (three had drifted). Under
`(hover:none)` `.comment` is not selectable text, its fields are: a hold picks a post, never a word.
  - **The keyboard shackle** — The shell (`app/appchrome.ts`, never a boot) publishes `--mc-kb` /
`body.mc-kb-open` from `visualViewport`; the fixed composers (DM, merecat), the sheet and the
theater ride it by CSS; every IN-FLOW field is placed by the shell's net — on focus (a settle ladder
across the keyboard's rise, and any scroll inside the settle window — the browser's own late
focus-scroll), on every keyboard resize, on every input — into the visual viewport MINUS the site's
own chrome (the top bar and the DM's sticky header; the tab bar, the DM composer, the merecat row,
the dock — a field "on the keyboard" would otherwise sit behind the composer riding it): the field's
EDITOR when it fits (`KB_GROUPS`: the box with its Save/Post row — a new editor container joins that
list or carries `data-mc-kb-group`), else the field; scrollable ancestors first, then the document
(never for a field a fixed ancestor holds), a spacer for room when the page ends short. A new
composer gets nothing of its own: an in-flow field is covered; a fixed one rides `--mc-kb` in its
`bottom`. `window.mcKeyboard.align(el, region)` is the headless proof.
  - **A call rings the phone, and only a missed call is a notification** — **Every call leaves ONE
event line in the conversation** (2026-09-13, the call log every chat app keeps): `call:missed` ·
`call:declined` · `call:answered:<secs>` — the grammar, the sentence each side reads and the
server's outcome rule are `Domain.Call` (`parseCallLine`, `callLineText`, `callOutcome`; the
membrane's `callLine`/`callLineText`), never inlined. The line is a quiet system DM from the caller,
muted, by side (the callee's miss tinted; a decline reads "No answer" to the caller — the callee's
private act), expiring with the thread like any word. `recordCallEnd`'s stamp (`ended_at`, 0017) is
the lock: whichever party's `/call/end` or the hourly sweep arrives first writes it, once; only a
miss rings the 'call' bell and the "Missed call" push, never at the offer; an answered call's length
is the server's measure; a failed setup is stamped and writes nothing.
  - **Badges are the shell's, and reading marks read** — Opening a conversation reads every bell its
sender rang (`dm`, `dm-react`, `call`) and `/dm/thread`, `/dm/seen` and `/wall/post/get` hand back
`notif_unread`, which the client sets at once — a bell never stays lit for a thing the reader is
looking at, however they reached it; a message or a reaction to a word on screen rings no bell at
all (`dmViewing`).
  - **Phones show no footer except on the home tab** — The merecat ask row is the same composer
shape; a FIXED bar's `bottom` places its MARGIN edge, so a fixed composer carries `margin: 0` in
main.css AND in any injected block (which wins).
  - **The fixed chrome answers the finger, not the platform's click** — The tab bar and the app bar
(`armTap`, `app/appchrome.ts`) act on `touchend` when `Domain.Tap` says the lift was a tap (in
place, quick, on the same control), dispatch the control's own click, cancel the platform's, and
swallow any echo; a drag or a hold stays the platform's. Only the two bars — a content link keeps
the platform's judgement — and every other road in still arrives as a click.
  - **Architecture in brief (as CLAUDE.md carried it until 2026-09-16)** — The PureScript kernel
(`Domain.*`: ADTs, smart constructors, `Maybe`/`Either`, illegal states unrepresentable, pure) →
**`app/core.ts`, the one audited membrane where types are erased** → Lit views (`app/views/*`,
light-DOM, presentational — THE read screens) and the classic client (`client/comments.ts` + its
modules: the write paths, the DM thread, merecat and every screen without a Lit view; a `viewX` with
a Lit twin is a one-line door to it — since 2026-09-16 there is no no-bundle fallback, because the
bundle always stands; the classic reaches the kernel through `window.mcCore` / `mcKit` / `mcRich`).
The worker (`comments-worker/src/index.ts` the `ROUTES` table, `routes/*.ts` the handlers one file
per feature, `{lib,durable,db}.ts` + `pure.js`, `webpush.js`) imports the same compiled kernel.
**New application logic goes in PureScript**; JS is interop only (DOM, fetch, crypto, storage,
Turnstile, WebSocket). A slice ships through four gates: byte-deterministic rebuild; parity (`make
pstest` + headless delegate-vs-classic); `npm run lint` green; headless render parity against a
**local** serve (`make serve`, `MC_BASE=http://127.0.0.1:8000`). Realtime: the `BoardHub` DO (forum
fan-out over WebSockets) and the `ChatRoom` DO (merecat generation and @mentions). Full map:
CODEBASE.md.
  - **Infrastructure at a glance (as CLAUDE.md carried it until 2026-09-16)** — - **Hosting**:
GitHub Pages serves the artifact `build.yml` deploys (`build_type: workflow`; `docs/` is the
packaged folder; CNAME + .nojekyll are asserted before packaging). Cloudflare fronts it: `ssl =
full` (NOT strict — the origin is Pages), `browser_cache_ttl = 0` (what makes `?v=` work), the
response-header/CSP ruleset, bot management — all in Terraform. - **PDFs**: the 244 published PDFs
live in R2 `merecatholicity-files` at `files.merecatholicity.com`, reached by a dynamic redirect
from `/<name>.pdf`; `docs/pdfs.txt` is the manifest; `scripts/publish_pdfs.py` publishes and
verifies; one PDF has no build (`resources/docs-src/The_Bishop_of_Rome.pdf`, a mirrored source). -
**Workers**: `comments-worker` (routes `/api/comments*`, `/api/merecat*`, `/@*`; D1
`merecatholicity-comments` + three librarian rooms; R2 avatars/backups/dm-media/wall-media;
Vectorize; Workers AI; two Durable Objects; four crons) and `contact-worker`
(`contact-api.merecatholicity.com`). Both TypeScript; `wrangler.jsonc` is the config truth. -
**merecat**, the librarian bot: a WebSocket state machine in the `ChatRoom` DO, five-legged
retrieval over three D1 rooms + Vectorize, `librarian/` is its mind; Cloudflare Workers AI is its
only backend (the GPU twin retired 2026-09-10). Its dials are `Domain.Merecat` (the reasoning
ladder, temperature, the nine band weights) stored in the librarian D1 `config` table: the
file-owned ones ride `librarian/config.yml`, the reasoning ones the merecat admin page. The band
weighting and persona are the owner's standing law — read the merecat passage in INFRASTRUCTURE.md
before touching anything there. The **AI budget guard** (`quota_guard_on`/`_pct`, default on at 95%
of the Workers AI day, `comments-worker/src/quota.ts`) reads the account's meter through
`CF_USAGE_TOKEN` before every ask and mention, admins included, rests merecat with the hours until
midnight UTC, and stands OPEN when the meter cannot be read — a margin, never the wall. -
**Terraform** owns the zone settings, DNS, the four rulesets, bot management, the R2 buckets, the D1
databases (as records), the Turnstile widgets, both GitHub repos, Pages, the two environments, the
Actions policy and variables; state is in R2 (`merecatholicity-tfstate`, unmanaged, SECRET).
wrangler owns everything a deploy rewrites. It cannot hold Vectorize, the R2 custom-domain bindings,
the TURN key, or Email Routing settings. - **Credentials**: three Cloudflare account tokens
(terraform / site / workers), their R2 pair, and one fine-grained GitHub PAT — inventory and
rotation in CICD.md §4. - **History** was rewritten 2026-09-09 (554 → 153 MB); every earlier sha is
gone — re-clone.
  - **The document list at the head of CLAUDE.md (until 2026-09-16)** — # CLAUDE.md Guidance for
agents working in this repository: the website **merecatholicity.com**, its Cloudflare backend, and
the build/deploy system. This file is the **rulebook and the map**, kept short on purpose. The
long-form reference — every design decision and postmortem since July 2026 — is the dated log under
**`docs/architecture/log/`**, indexed by `docs/architecture/INFRASTRUCTURE.md`: before changing a
subsystem, read its passage (find the bold lead-in in the index, grep it in the month's file). -
`docs/architecture/CICD.md` — **how work ships**: the workflows, the approval gate, every
credential, the exceptions, the traps. Read before deploying, changing infra, or touching a secret.
- `docs/architecture/CODEBASE.md` — code architecture: PureScript kernel → `core.ts` membrane → Lit
views; the worker's module split; a newcomer reading order. - `README.md` — the human how-it-works:
layout, hosting, build pipeline, cookbook. - `comments-worker/API.md` (wire contract),
`librarian/README.md` (the bot's mind), `tests/README.md`, `terraform/README.md`. - If
`CONTEXT_DUMP.txt` is present in the root and you work on text content, ingest it first. **Keep the
documents current in the same change**: this file for rules; the log for the why (a dated passage
with a bold lead-in appended to `docs/architecture/log/<this month>.md`, then
`scripts/infra_index.py --write`); CICD.md when a workflow, secret or make target it names changes;
CODEBASE.md when module structure changes; README when the human story changes.
  - **Repository layout (as CLAUDE.md carried it until 2026-09-16)** — The root holds only
tool-convention files (`Makefile`, `eslint.config.js`, `package.json` + lock, `tsconfig.json`,
`globals.d.ts`, `.gitignore`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `LICENSE*`); a review or a hand-over note is
a dated file under `docs/architecture/reviews/`, never a root artefact; `local/` is the box's own
and ignored. **`docs/` is the served site and a MIXTURE**: hand-maintained source (nav.js, sw.js,
the page scripts, the vendored libraries, turnstile.html, every image, the 17 hand pages, CNAME,
.nojekyll) is tracked; everything the build writes is git-ignored and rebuilt;
`tests/py/test_docs_sources.py` enforces the split. A new hand page needs an `!docs/<name>.html`
line in `.gitignore` and an entry in `scripts/nav.py`'s `PAGES`. Elsewhere: `book/` (LaTeX +
`build-confession.sh`), `content/` (hand-page sources), `resources/` (corpus converters, the
committed `*-body.tex`, `docs-src/` preserved sources), `partials/` (pandoc includes — a partial
carrying a versioned asset is stamped too), `scripts/`, `styles/main.css` (the one Tailwind v4
entry), `app/` (Lit shell + views, TS), `client/` (the classic client: `comments.ts` is the boot —
core helpers, the router, the kit — and `composer.ts` · `profile.ts` · `board.ts` · `wall.ts` ·
`surface.ts` · the six DM factories `dm-{crypto,message,pickers,inbox,thread,styles}.ts` ·
`merecat.ts` · `admin.ts` are feature modules installed per boot; bundled to `docs/comments.js`),
`purescript/src/Domain/*.purs` (the kernel, 33 modules), `comments-worker/`, `contact-worker/`,
`librarian/` (`librarian/private/` is a separate PRIVATE clone — never a submodule, never
committed), `webtest/`, `tests/`, `terraform/`, `.github/workflows/`.
  - **Build and verify (as CLAUDE.md carried it until 2026-09-16)** — - **Gates**: `make tests`
(unit suite: PureScript, JS, worker, Python, CSS; runs `psbuild` first); `make jscheck` (eslint +
tsc + psbuild); `make check` (jscheck + linkcheck); `make check-pdfs` (bucket vs manifest vs local
PDFs; CI runs it with the site token). - **Build**: `make css` (Tailwind → `docs/style.css`), `make
bundle` (purs + esbuild → `app.js`, `comments.js`, then the version stamp), `make content` (hand
pages), `make writings` (detect the own writings → the generated `Domain.Writings`; every psbuild
runs it), `make html` (book + corpus + post-processing + stamp + manifest + check; incremental — one
target per work), `make menu` (nav — `NAV_ENABLED = False`, the old menu is kept in code), `make -C
resources pdf`, `make pdf` / `publish` / `chart-pdfs` (`CHROMIUM=…`) / `logos`, `make publish-pdfs`
/ `mirrored-pdfs` / `pdf-manifest`, `make migrate` / `migrate-status` / `schema-snapshot`, `make
serve` (binds 127.0.0.1 only — load-bearing), `make comments-backup`, `make librarian`. - Builds are
pinned to `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=1784160000` and byte-deterministic: a double `make bundle` must leave
`docs/app.js` unchanged. - Toolchain is npm: `npm ci` only, never `sudo npm` or `-g`; esbuild and
purs exact-pinned. This dev box is Ubuntu/WSL2, not the Arch machine older notes assume: official
Node 24 in `~/.local`, a `python` shim (see the toolchain memory). - Every pandoc call in a
resources loop ends `|| exit 1`; a new loop must too.
  - **Testing policy (as CLAUDE.md carried it until 2026-09-16)** — Tests exist to clarify what the
code does and to guard the rules that break silently — no coverage target, no tests for trivial
getters. Layer 1 `tests/` is hermetic (`make tests`, the standing gate; one file per concern;
`tests/README.md`); a worker road is RUN against a real SQLite through `tests/_support/worker.mjs` —
lock source text only for a law that is textual. Layer 2 `webtest/` is headless Chromium against
prod (`webtest/audit.py --pages …`, `--app --journey …`, the per-slice `test_*.py`; a matched chrome
+ chromedriver pair lives in `~/.cloakbrowser/chromium-<ver>/`). When you add or change a rule, add
or adjust its test in the same change; never delete a test to go green.

## Cloudflare Workers (dynamic backend)

- **The handler preambles are one function with the variance as options (2026-09-16)** — the
  review's P2-1: eighty handlers opened with the same eight lines (parse the body, rate-limit by IP,
  read the key, hash it, maybe the block gate) while `keyedGated` existed and was used twenty times.
  They were not copies, though: they differed in the 429 sentence (`Too many requests.`, `… Slow
  down.`, `Too many messages at once. Wait a minute and try again.`, seven more), in whether a
  missing key was refused and with what (400 `Bad request.`, 401 `Sign in to react.`, or not at all
  — an empty key hashing to the empty identity), and in whether the lock/ban gate ran. So `lib.ts`
  gained `gated(request, env, {bucket, limited, key, missing, block})`, plus `adminGated` (parse, an
  optional limit, `requireAdmin`), `readLimited` (a keyless GET's READ limit, JSON or the plain text
  `/feed` always answered) and `ingestGated` (the pipeline's key), and a converter rewrote every
  handler whose preamble was in the CANONICAL order into a three-line call carrying exactly the
  options that reproduce its old texts and refusals; a handler that validates BEFORE it rate-limits
  (a bad id must not cost a limiter token) or refuses the admin AFTER validating keeps its own order
  and was not converted — the difference is now visible at the call site instead of buried in eight
  lines. The proof is the bundle's string-literal set (`scripts/worker_bundle_set.sh` now writes
  `strings.txt`): not one wire text removed, only the helper names and `optional` added; no function
  dropped; the behavioural suites and the reads webtest green; and the `: any` ratchet fell (869 →
853 → 825) because a converted handler takes a real `Request`.
  `tests/worker/gates.test.mjs` runs each option. First slice: `routes/dm.ts` (13 handlers) and
`routes/merecat.ts` (12); the second: board (12), wall (1), notify (3), profile (1), admin (8),
media (3) — 53 converted in all; the sixty-odd that keep their own order are exactly those whose 400
must come before their 429 or 403. The `gated(bucket, fn)` wrapper in
  `ROUTES` the review sketched was not taken: every handler's line in the table is locked by tests,
  and a wrapper would hide which bucket a door pays into.

- **One road registers a member, and the directory lists members (2026-09-16)** — the review's P2-2:
  `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO profiles (hash, created_at)` stood in four places (the board's unread
  count, the settings gear, the merecat ask, the hub's last-seen stamp), so any keyed read created a
  member row, and the directory read 53 members for six humans. `registerMember(env, hash, now)` in
  `lib.ts` is now the one road that row appears by; the profile save, the avatar upload and the
  first post keep their richer upserts. And a member, for the directory's purposes, is someone who
  has said or shown something — a nick, a live forum or feed post, or a published DM key
  (`dm_pubkeys`); a bare row is an identity that has acted, and stays out of `/dm/directory` (and
  out of the DM picker, which could not have written to it anyway: no key). `isEstablished` — the
  Turnstile spare — still reads the bare row; nothing about being spared got easier or harder.
  Proven in `tests/worker/directory.test.mjs`: a fresh key's first keyed read makes the row and is
  spared, and is not listed; a nick, a post or a key lists it.

- **Additive is not forever: the retirement cadence and its tripwire (2026-09-16)** — the review's
  P2-8: the migrations law says additive, and additive had come to mean for ever — `dm_threads` kept
  its six pair columns and a legacy unique index, `dms` its `react_a/react_b/liked_a/liked_b`,
  `dm_media` its retired `msg_id`, the two frozen like tables stood, and the one-deploy shims
  (`/dm/like`, the three wall like aliases, `react_me` and its siblings on a pair's rows, E1
  accepted on the wire) had no date. The cadence, now a law in CLAUDE.md: a shim lives one deploy
  and at most 30 days; a column nothing reads for 30 days is dropped by a `retire` migration — the
  one non-additive kind, after a fresh daily backup shows in the Health card and an owner heads-up.
  The tripwire: `tests/_support/retirements.json` names each item with the file it lives in, a
  pattern that finds it, the day it was frozen, the day it is due and how to retire it;
  `tests/worker/retire.test.mjs` requires the pattern present before the due date (an entry for
  something already gone is stale) and ABSENT from the due date on — so `make tests` goes red the
  morning something is due, and stays red until it is retired or the date is moved with a reason.
  Due 2026-09-21: the four like routes, the `react_me` family, E1 on send and edit. Due 2026-10-16:
  the legacy `actor_hash` clauses of the bell marks (a bell from before 0016 named its sender). Due
  2026-10-14, together, as migration 0018 `retire_pair_columns` (owner-gated): the pair columns
  (`a_hash`/`b_hash` are still WRITTEN by `ensurePairThread` — that write goes first), the `dms`
  reaction columns, `dm_media.msg_id`, the two like tables. Not in the ledger: `dmview:<hash>`,
  which the review listed as a shim but the client still sends for a pair whose room is not yet made
  — a live road, not a leftover.

- **The CSP has somewhere to report (2026-09-16)** — the review's P2-7, first move. The zone's
  Content-Security-Policy had been Report-Only since Terraform took the rulesets (2026-09-08) with
  no `report-uri` and no collector, so nobody could know what an enforced policy would break; and
  the "11 innerHTML sites" the review counted were eleven comments saying "never innerHTML" — zero
  real sinks, no eval, no `new Function` (locked now: `tests/js/no_html_sinks.test.mjs` sweeps
  `app/` and `client/`). The collector is `POST /api/comments/csp-report` (`routes/ops.ts`):
  keyless, `READ_LIMIT`, the body capped at 16 KB, both shapes a browser sends
  (`application/csp-report` and the Reporting API's `application/reports+json`), and it TALLIES
  rather than stores — one row per effective directive · blocked origin (a blob: or data: URL folds
  to its scheme, an extension URL to `extension`) · document path with the query string dropped, a
  count, first and last seen, the top hundred in `app_settings.csp_report_tally`; anything
  unreadable is a quiet 204, because a collector that argues fills the browser console. The Health
  card reads it (`readOps` → `csp`). Second move, in Terraform: the Report-Only header names this
  door as `report-uri` and `report-to`, gains the hashes of the two inline scripts (the `mc-fout`
  anti-flash script on 270 pages and turnstile.html's), names `wss://merecatholicity.com` outright
  and admits `blob:` images and media; then a watch window of a week, and the flip to an enforced
  `Content-Security-Policy` only when the tally reads as noise.

- **The key is the account, said plainly; and the bundles have a budget (2026-09-16)** — the
  review's P2-9 and the first half of P2-5. The identity export the review asked for had shipped
  days earlier (Settings → Account → Show my key: copy, key file, QR for a second device; the
  onboarding save gate; the third-visit nudge); what was missing was the threat model in words and
  any judgement of a pasted key. `content/privacy.html#your-key` now says what the key is (the
  account; no recovery because the site never has it), what loses it, what leaks it, and what it
  protects; the key panel links there. `Domain.Auth.keyStrength` names a key's shape — `Generated`
  (the 43-character base64url `makeKey` mints), `Strong` (twenty or more characters, three classes),
  else `Weak` — and the shell's paste box signs a weak key in and says so once ("anyone who guesses
  it is you"), never refuses: custom keys exist and a refusal would lock their holders out. And
  `tests/js/bundle_budget.test.mjs` holds `docs/comments.js`, `docs/app.js` and `docs/style.css` to
  a ceiling (today's size plus two percent, in `tests/_support/bundle_budget.json`): a build over it
  fails, a build more than five percent under it asks for the ceiling to come down — the ratchet the
  code-splitting that follows will click.

- **The classic client is split: three lazy chunks, and a reader of a KJV chapter no longer
  downloads the admin console (2026-09-16)** — the review's P2-5, second half. `docs/comments.js`
  (368 KB) rode to every app page whole, the admin console, the librarian's screens and the chat
  screen included. The binding matrix decided what could go lazy: a module whose exports an EAGER
  module binds cannot (the board, wall and profile bind `isAdmin`, `adminGate`, `ADMIN_HASHES`,
  `annotateMeta`, `topicAdminCorner`, `adminProfileEditor`, `buildFingerprint`; every module binds
  `MERECAT_BOT_HASH`). So `admin.ts` was split by its own call graph into `admin-core.ts` (eager:
  those seven and the moderation lines they need) and `admin.ts` (the nine views and the pending
  queue — nothing eager binds them), the two merecat constants moved to the root's helpers, and
  `admin`, `merecat` and `dm-thread` became LAZY: esbuild builds `client/comments.ts` as an ES
  module with `--splitting` (`docs/comments.js` + `docs/chunks/<name>-<hash>.js`, content-hashed so
  a chunk URL is immutable without a `?v=`; `rm -rf docs/chunks` first so no stale chunk lingers),
  the one `<script>` tag on the fourteen app pages is `type="module"` (deferred like before;
  `stamp_versions.py` stamps it as before), and the root fetches a chunk by `import()` the first
  time a route needs it: `B.ensure(name)` loads, installs the factory into the current boot, binds
  and runs it and puts its exports on B; a chunk once fetched is kept PAGE-SCOPED above `mcBoot` and
  installed eagerly by every later boot, so its `bind()`/`run()` are exactly a bundled module's; the
  router reaches a lazy view through `lazyView(module, name, …)`, which also declines to render if
  the boot was aborted while the chunk was in flight. The seam's law, in `tests/js/client_modules`:
  nothing eager binds a lazy module's export, the root copies none, the root never imports a lazy
  module statically. Result: `comments.js` 368 → 242 KB (−34%), the chunks 47 (admin), 46 (merecat)
  and 31 KB (dm-thread); `tests/js/bundle_budget` clicked down with it. `sw.js` needed nothing
  (comments.js and its chunks were never in its shell set); `test_docs_sources` admits `chunks/*.js`
  as build output; the CSP's `script-src 'self'` covers module scripts and their imports. Not split:
  `dm-pickers` (dm-message and dm-inbox bind it) and `app.js`.

- **A declared count is shown, never told (2026-09-16)** — the first 23:30 check after the fourth
  cron shipped DM'd the admins "Cron triggers (this worker): 80% of the free tier", and the
  standing-warning rule would have repeated it weekly for ever. `cron.triggers` is `FREE.cronsUsed`
  over `FREE.cronsLimit`, a number typed into `usagecalc.ts`, not a measurement: it moves only when
  `wrangler.jsonc` does, four of five is 80% exactly, and the account really does hold four
  (verified against the API: the comments worker's four, none on either contact worker; the limits
  page says five per free account). The owner's law, said the same day: **only useful
  notifications** — an alert is something a human must act on, and there is nothing to do about a
  slot count until a sixth cron is wanted, when the deploy itself refuses. So the row carries
  `declared: true`; `foldUsageAlerts` skips declared rows (the stale `usage_alert_state` entry the
  check wrote falls out on the next run — the fold rebuilds the state from the rows); `worstPct` and
  the health card's headline count only measured meters; the bar stays, its note naming the four
  crons; `tests/worker/usage.test.mjs` locks it at 80 and at 100. Not taken: folding the monthly
  chain into the daily one to free a slot — it would reshape the chains and heartbeats built the
  same day for no gain.

- **The live hub is sharded, and every operation inside a shard is the size of its audience
  (2026-09-17)** — every socket on the site (presence, board events, DM frames, notifications,
  typing, call signalling) landed in ONE Durable Object instance, `idFromName('board')`:
  single-threaded, a soft thousand requests a second by Cloudflare's own limits page, and — worse
  than the count — `publish`, `#fan`, `#isOnline`, `presenceOf`, `viewersOf` and `dmViewing` each
  walked `ctx.getWebSockets()` and `deserializeAttachment()` for EVERY socket on every call (a DM
  send was two such walks). Now `Domain.Hub` is the law: the hub is `HUB_SHARDS` instances (a
  wrangler var; shard 0 keeps the name `board`, so `1` is bit-for-bit the July hub), a socket is
  PLACED by its member's hash — the client's `?h=` hint on the upgrade URL (`app/live.ts`; a socket
  opened anonymously is closed and dialled again when the identity arrives), an anonymous reader by
  a sha256 of its address — so every socket of one member lives on one shard and "their last socket
  closed" stays a local, exact fact; `lib.ts`'s `sendToHub` routes a private-only event (`user:`
  scopes) to its home shards and fans anything public to all N in parallel (`Promise.allSettled`, a
  failed shard logged, never its siblings); `hubPresenceOf`/`hubViewersOf`/`hubDmViewing` ask each
  home shard about its own; `hubStats` gives `readOps` a socket count per shard for the Health card
  — the number that says when to raise the var. Inside a shard the sockets are INDEXED in memory (by
  scope, by member, the parsed attachment per socket), built once from the attachments when the
  object wakes and kept current by every accept, auth, sub, close and failed send, so a publish
  costs its recipients and nothing else. What crosses between shards, by a `relay` RPC: a presence
  change (a watcher sits anywhere), a typing or call-signal frame to a member homed on a sibling,
  and the presence seed a new subscriber asks for. A member whose socket sits on the wrong shard (a
  bundle from before the hint) is accepted and logged `hub_misrouted` once — their private frames go
  to their home shard until the page reloads. The var shipped at `1` (the July hub, byte for byte)
  and was raised to `2` the same day at the owner's word — the two nightly identities land on
  different shards under 2, so the cross-shard road is proven live and runs in production every
  day rather than waiting untried (the rollback lesson) — with `wrangler tail` watched for
  `hub_misrouted` across the raise. Raising it is the whole scaling act: the docs say a deploy
  restarts every Durable Object and disconnects its sockets, so the clients reconnect under the new
  count and no drain step exists. Not a lever: more Workers — a Worker scales out by itself, and
  the free tier's request cap is the account's, so a second worker only spends the same budget. The
same slice made `deliverPush` send twenty at a time instead of
  one after another (a popular thread's tail of watchers fell past the `waitUntil` window). Proven
  in `tests/purescript/hub.test.mjs` (the placing, the names, the routing) and
  `tests/worker/hub.test.mjs`, which RUNS three real `BoardHub` instances over fake sockets through
  a fake namespace: the fan, the private scope, a watcher on shard 2 seeing shard 0's member come
  and go, typing and call signals landing on the recipient's shard only, the last-socket stamp, a
  wake rebuilding the index, the worker's routing by the spy's recorded names, the upgrade placed by
  the hint. The `: any` ratchet clicked from 825 to 799 on the way. Not done here, and named so it
  is a decision: D1 read replication (the Sessions API across three hundred `prepare` sites is its
  own slice), and the hub's two D1 writes per last close.


- **A rotated push key is followed on the next app OPEN, not the next Settings visit
  (2026-09-17)** — told after the rotation that members would stop being notified "until they next
  open Settings", the owner asked whether that meant toggling the switch: unacceptable if so, fine
  if it only meant reopening the app. It meant neither, and was worse than the second: no toggle
  was needed (`McSettings._reflectPush` already moved a subscription whose
  `applicationServerKey` differed from the served key), but that code ran only when the
  `<mc-settings>` element mounted — the Settings sheet on a phone, the gear menu on a desktop — so a
  member who never opened Settings would have gone on silently un-notified. The repair now lives in
  `app/push.ts` and the shell starts it once per document, after first paint
  (`installPushHeal()`, beside the service-worker registration and never in `boots()`); Settings
  calls the same `healPushSubscription`, and its own copy of the move and of the key helpers is
  gone. It touches only a device where THIS identity turned push on (`mc-push-owner`) with the
  permission still granted; a subscription already on the served key costs one cacheable read and
  no write; a moved one is unsubscribed (a new key beside a live one is refused), its token
  unregistered, and the new one subscribed and registered. Where the browser will not subscribe
  without a user gesture (WebKit may, even with the permission granted) the move waits for the
  member's next tap or key press anywhere — `pointerup`, `mousedown` or `keydown`, the events that
  grant activation — and `subscribe()` is started synchronously inside that handler so the
  activation is still live; an unfinished move is remembered in `mc-push-resub` and completed on
  the next open, and a Settings sheet open at the time hears `mc-push-healed` and re-reflects its
  switch. `tests/js/push_heal.test.mjs` drives it with fake browser objects (what it leaves alone,
  the no-write current case, the move in order, the gesture path — whose fake refuses
  `subscribe()` outside the tap — the half-done move across opens, single flight, and where it may
  run). Verified in headless Chromium against `make serve` with the notification permission
  granted by DevTools: a visitor and a member without a subscription cause no request, and a
  member with an unfinished move reads `/push/vapid-key` on app open with no Settings element in
  the page.

- **The rotation after the disclosure: four of six done by the agent, two need the owner
  (2026-09-17)** — the owner asked for the whole rotation to be done for them and pasted a new
  Cloudflare token with its R2 S3 pair. That token did not work: the account and user `verify`
  endpoints both answered "Invalid API Token", and the S3 pair was refused by every bucket, while
  the same hand-signed SigV4 request with the EXISTING Terraform R2 pair read the state bucket —
  so the signer was sound and the token was not. The pair was internally consistent (R2 derives
  the secret access key as the SHA-256 of the token value, and it matched), so it was not a
  typo; it is an R2-scoped token, which could not have reached the two items below even if live.
  It is kept, mode 600, in `~/.config/merecatholicity/rotate.env`. The four the existing tokens
  could reach, each changed and then proven: **`MC_TEST_BYPASS`** (a fresh 48-hex value on the
  worker, and `webtest/.testkeys`' `MC_TEST_TOKEN` rewritten to `TEST:<new>` — the box's old copy
  had been stale anyway); **`MERECAT_INGEST_KEY`** in all three homes back to back — the worker,
  the `MC_INGEST_KEY` Actions secret (`gh secret set`), and `ci.env` — then the ops door answered
  the new key 200 and the old one 403; **`TURNSTILE_SECRET`** through the widget API's
  `rotate_secret` on the comments widget (`0x4AAAAAAD8IYH9_xQ0HE0yB`) with
  `invalidate_immediately: false`, so the old secret covered the seconds before the worker had
  the new one, then `siteverify` with the new secret and a dummy token answered
  `invalid-input-response` rather than `invalid-input-secret`; **the VAPID pair**, generated with
  the same WebCrypto calls `webpush.js` makes (P-256, PKCS8 private, raw public), round-tripped
  sign→verify before use, the private half by `wrangler secret put` and the public half in both
  places in `wrangler.jsonc`, deployed together by the emergency road and committed at once
  (`0dcae34`); `/push/vapid-key` serves the new key and a signature from the uploaded private key
  verifies against it. Existing push subscriptions are bound to the old key; a key mismatch is
answered 401/403, which `deliverPush` counts as `failed` rather than `dead`, so no row was pruned,
and each subscription moves to the new key on the member's next app open (the passage below). **Two
remain, and no
  token on this box can reach them**: the TURN key (`TURN_KEY_SECRET`, with its public id
  `TURN_KEY_ID`) needs a token with Realtime/Calls edit, and `CF_USAGE_TOKEN` needs a new
  Account-Analytics-read token and the old one revoked — every existing token answered 403 on
  `/calls/turn_keys` and on the token endpoints. The TURN secret is the more urgent: it mints
